# Listening stats

Listening stats count the plays on the card and show them in the Library: a hub with the time listened, the last 7 days, and shelves of tracks to play (Most played, New finds, Old favourites, Not played yet, Recently played), with a first row on Now Playing's navigation sheet. The player works them out from its own play log. It needs no network, writes nothing more to the card for a play than the play's own slot, and a tap never reads the card. (As decided, 2026-11-20: a tap's own pass never reads the card. Entering the stats pages queues one ask that the card worker answers in about 1-2 s, and the lifetime line is in RAM: section 1. The ask's card work on the loop runs in other passes, after 500 ms of input quiet, each piece under 35 ms by run 3a-2's rule; a touch that lands during one of them waits for it, as it would for any pass.)

**Status (2026-11-22): the review's three cases settled; case (a) changed (V10b, 86f1990; its review 8d56155, a comment), host-gated, not on a device.** V8's review sent three cases to the owner (the status below: (a)-(c)). Word for word, the options chosen: for (a), **"Don't wait at the floor"**: "While the floor pauses the scan, a visit goes ahead; its memory take still checks there's room for a decoder, so playback stays safe. One gate condition plus a host test." For (b) and (c): **"Keep both as built"**. As built (9.7's "S3a: the owner's calls as built"): `CardTasks` hands `restAhead(active_ && statsvisit::restHolds(scanHeld(), scanStuck(), batteryLow()))` (`src/app/CardTasks.cpp:445-446`; `lib/core/StatsVisit.h:303-305`), so while the battery floor pauses the scan (below 10 % off USB, until USB or a reading above 15 %; `ScanScheduler::batteryLow()`) the tag scan's memory doesn't hold the visits, though memory carved mid-rest stays held there with no step to give it back. A visit then goes ahead beside it, and its take (`statshold::take()`, with a decoder's room) guards playback. Outside the floor the visits wait the rest out as built, a fresh card's or a Rescan's whole rest included (minutes). Memory no step gives back (`scanStuck()`) still doesn't hold them, and the gate still stops a take only. The floor can't be driven on the device: no console command changes the battery or the power state the scan reads, and the console's USB lets the floor go. So the rule is host-tested only (`test_stats_visit`'s `test_the_rest_gate_s_word`, `test_scan_scheduler`'s `test_the_battery_floor`). Owed before S3b: V8's device pass on V10b's image (9.7's "S3a: V8-V10's device pass") and the whole `pio test -e native`. The push and the PR remain the owner's word. *Done (2026-11-22, later; the status above):* the pass, with 3a-8's second boot to the owner, and the whole run (2,468 of 2,468 on 8d56155's code).

**Status (2026-11-21, later): the owner's three calls settled, built as V8 and V9 with V8's review (747a368), these docs V10; host-gated, not on a device.** The device pass's three calls (9.7's "What goes to the owner") went to the owner. Word for word, the options chosen:
1. **"Borrow Dance's block"** (call 1, 3a-4's held snapshot): instead of carving the stats lists in PSRAM (or freeing and recounting them at every track change), lend them the 4,096 B block the player already keeps for the Dance tab, idle whenever that tab isn't showing. The memory manager never sees the lists, so PSRAM stays exactly main's with no hooks in playback, the queue, Auto DJ or the fence, and no blank page at track changes. Opening the Dance tab drops the lists (they recount on return), a console ask while Dance is up is refused, and 4,096 B caps the library at 18,432 albums. The owner counts it as "nothing held": the stats own no memory.
2. **"Only while held"** (call 3, the tag scan's rest): the stats visits wait out the card worker's tag-scan rest, but only while the scan's memory is actually carved (its View or its 90 KB work memory). A battery-held scan with nothing carved doesn't hold the pages; a Rescan still does while its memory is held.
3. **"Cap on"** (call 2, the lit cap; chosen earlier the same day): kLitCap on (lit visits rank and walk as dark ones; a visit's block given back once its holds reach 3 s in all).

They replace the owner's first picks that day for calls 1 and 3: "Free + recount" (the snapshot freed before each track's open and Auto DJ's join, then counted again by itself), which is not built (9.7's "As decided (2026-11-21): the snapshot in the Dance tab's block" says why), and "Visits wait it out", which "Only while held" narrows to the scan's memory actually carved. As built, on `claude/listening-stats-s3`, not pushed (9.7's "S3a: the owner's calls as built" has the commits, the tests and the figures):
- **The loan** (V8 a4853d1): the snapshot is the Dance tab's tracker scratch, lent while that tab is down (`DanceMode::lendScratch()`, `statsvisit::loanOk()`). Nothing of it is carved or freed. The Dance tab coming up releases it first (`DanceMode::setActive(true)` calls `dancelend::reclaim()`), and an ask in flight then ends without writing. A refused loan (the tab up, past 18,432 albums, or no block made at the boot) ends the visit No room. For the owner: the console's lean ask (`Hn`, `Hn1`) needs no snapshot, so it still runs with the Dance tab up (counting from its walk); "a console ask while Dance is up is refused" holds for `Hv`, `Hv1` and `Hu`.

S2's asks don't. *Since V10b (2026-11-22, "Don't wait at the floor"):* not while the battery floor pauses the scan (`statsvisit::restHolds()`).
- **The cap** (V8): `kLitCap` is `true`.
- **The heap model** (V9 ec3f13c; MODELLED): a borrowed way gives the visits alone's figures to the byte in the suite, the wide and the old decoders' builds, the snapshot lent at every turn (78 of 78, 432 of 432, 78 of 78) and carved at none. So 3a-4's 3,908 B and its moves go by construction, which 3a-4, run again on the device, must show. *As run (2026-11-22; MEASURED):* it shows it: main's to the byte, free included (the status above).

Against main de4f52f's CI build: `.bss` still 16 B over (`StatsLists` 44 B); `.iram0.text` and its 25 `libc_a-*` objects main's; `DanceMode` (2,008 B), `ui::Ui`, `CardTasks` and Auto DJ's object main's sizes; the image 26,932 B larger (af1969f's clean build; the review's uncommitted tree measured 16 B more, its `-dirty` version strings).

Three cases from V8's review go back to the owner, since each bears on "Only while held" as worded. Each is built provisionally. *Settled (2026-11-22; the status above):* (a) "Don't wait at the floor", changed in V10b; (b) and (c) "Keep both as built".
- **(a) A battery floor met mid-rest.** The loop gives the scan's memory back only once the rest is done (`!restWork()`), so a floor met mid-rest (below 10 % off USB) keeps it, and the pages wait out the floor (USB or 15 %) and then the rest's end. Only a scan the floor held before its first step, or one a library update's fence emptied, holds nothing. Built to the letter, and documented. *Changed (2026-11-22, "Don't wait at the floor"):* while the floor pauses the scan a visit goes ahead, and its take still checks there's room for a decoder.
- **(b) Memory no step gives back.** A chunk the card refused after a failed compaction (a full card) keeps the scan's memory with no step left to give it back (`CardJobs::scanStuck()`). By the letter the pages would wait for good. As built that memory doesn't hold them: they go on to the take's own check (a decoder's room), as before the gate. This departs from the letter. *Kept as built (2026-11-22).*
- **(c) The gate stops a take only.** A scan whose first carve comes during a visit's hold (from its rank's end through its walk, about 3 s at most with the cap) carves its memory beside the visit's block, with no decoder-room check, as main's own carves come. Documented, not changed. *Kept as built (2026-11-22).*

How long the pages wait meanwhile: on the spare about 15-20 s after the boot's walk ended (the work memory's trim, 10 s after the scan's last step: 9.7's "S3a: the device pass"); on a fresh card, or after a Rescan, the whole tag scan, minutes (a 20k-track scan about 2.6-3.0 min dark and idle, about twice that lit, 3-6 min while playing: `docs/METADATA.md` 3.3.4, ESTIMATED).

**Status (2026-11-20, later): S3's design under A; the owner's four calls.** S3 is designed, not built: the branch `claude/listening-stats-s3` is main de4f52f (S0 #59, S1 #61, S2 #66 merged), with no S3 code. A design run (three designs, two judges, a synthesis and an adversarial review of it, every load-bearing citation read at de4f52f) put four calls to the owner, and the owner picked the recommended option of each (2026-11-20; the option labels relayed as picked):
1. **"While on stats pages"** (how long a page holds its lists). One ask when the stats pages are entered ranks every list and walks once: about 1-2 s of "Counting plays…" on the hub. Its lists go into a **snapshot**: one plain PSRAM block of at most 4,096 B, 3,860 B on the spare (9.7's layout; the 3.5 KB put to the owner left out each list's 16 rows past its cap, which the copy-out needs). The snapshot stays while the listener moves between stats pages, and an album or a sheet opened from one counts as on them. It is freed when they leave them or the screen goes dark; coming back asks again. It is the same kind of page-held block as the mood filters' 6.8 KB FilteredAlbums block on Albums (`lib/core/FilteredAlbums.*`; `docs/MOOD-FILTERS.md:605`, `:1024`). It must be checked against main on the board before it is relied on: 9.7's run 3a-4, which passes only where PSRAM is main's to the byte (free less the snapshot's carve) and sends any difference to the owner. There is no 60 s linger, and nothing is kept across tabs, Now Playing or the dark. *As decided (2026-11-21, later; "Borrow Dance's block", the status above):* the snapshot is the Dance tab's 4,096 B block, lent, not carved: nothing of the stats is in the heap, and opening the Dance tab drops it. *This replaces* "S3 decides how long a page holds its lists" (the S2 status below) and 7.6's "kept until the next `enter()`".
2. **"Lifetime line, no memory"** (the week's figures). The entry row (Library > Artists, item 0) shows lifetime figures already in RAM: HEAD's listened time since the first day ("74 h 10 min since 1 Sep"), with fixed bars as its icon. Last 7 days (hub row 1 and the Week page) fills from the hub's ask, a 1-2 s wait: the ask reads the 7 days (DAYS ⊕ the records) into the snapshot. Nothing new is held: no week block, no ring of plays, no hub summary, and no growth of the UI's PSRAM object (`ui::Ui`, `psramNew` at `src/main.cpp:4068`). *This replaces* 2's and 7.3's "7 h 37 min in the last 7 days" with the week's bars, and the design run's 400 B week-and-ring block and 112 B hub summary. Two readings of the call are the plan's, not the owner's, and go to the owner with S3b's figures:
   - **The plays.** The option's text was "HEAD's listened time and plays". The line draws the time alone, as the option's own example does: with the plays ("74 h 10 min · 1,204 plays since 1 Sep", about 250 px in English) it overruns its Small 204 room in English already. HEAD's plays are read for the Empty state (plays 0) and not drawn.
   - **"No growth"** is read as `ui::Ui`'s carve unchanged: its size within main's TLSF class, read with gdb on both ELFs. TLSF carves the class's rounded size, so a size inside the class carves the same bytes at the same place. That is S2's precedent, the other way round: the walk's 48 B pushed Auto DJ's object over a class, a resident shift, and S2 took them out (9.6). S3b adds a few bytes to `ui::Ui`, in its `ListView` and `LibraryPage` members (7.2, 7.11 name each). If the owner reads "no growth" as main's size to the byte, those bytes move to `StatsLists`' `.bss` (internal RAM, counted against G5's margin) and S3b's gate becomes main's size.
3. **"5-byte fingerprints"** (Not played yet after a library update). The played set is the top 40 bits of each path hash (u40, 5 B each), ascending without a sort, since the top bits of an ascending u64 sequence ascend. The lean Not played yet ask is 122,336 B on the spare and 130,912 B at P = 19,478 (9.7's grades). It fits at every point the board measured after an update (at P = 19,478 within one class of the line when the snapshot comes out of the largest free block: 9.7's fit, run 3a-3 decides). The error is one way: an unplayed album can be hidden when an unplayed track's top 40 bits equal a played one's (at worst about 1e-4 expected a walk, 6e-6 on the spare); a played album is never shown. A host test pins an injected collision. *This replaces* 13's "u32 prefixes confirmed by the full hash".
4. **"30 days, kept without clock"** (O5). Not played yet opens after 30 days of counting, as New finds and Old favourites do. With the board's clock unset, it stays open when the card's DAYS already hold 30 or more days between their first and newest dated day (the ask reports both); otherwise it is disabled with "Needs dates". The shelf ranks fine without a clock (`src/app/StatsLists.cpp:245`). `statsview::notPlayedOpen` gains that input. *This settles* 3.1's O5.

Two defaults were stated to the owner with them (3.2):
- **(a) Lit asks.** An ask made while the screen is lit ranks at priority 0 (`lib/core/ScanScheduler.h:419-421`), which is unmeasured: 9.6's device pass ran every rank dark. Every UI ask is lit, since the pages hold their lists only while the screen is lit. S3a measures it from the console first (9.7's run 3a-1). If an ask's block is held past 3 s, S3 caps it, and the figures go back to the owner, who may overrule. The cap's form is S3's default (9.7, "The lit hold"): UI asks rank at priority 1 and walk with the dark's 8 ms passes, lit or not, and a visit's block is given back when its holds reach 3 s in all. "Accept dips" is not claimed for any UI ask until that measurement.
- **(b) The wait after a reset.** For about 50 s after a reset, a stats page shows "Waiting for the card…" while Auto DJ's join and the card's boot walk run (9.6's run F: the block taken at 50.7 s). Accepted.

The review's fixes, all in 9.7:
- the copy-out writes each kept row into the snapshot before the Ranker's room is reused;
- "Couldn't read the stats" only for a read fault, never for a yield;
- the device runs send no settings, clock or language command;
- the held snapshot's device check carves it again after each Live, and passes only where PSRAM is main's to the byte.

Earlier calls still bind:
- **"A: nothing held"**: nothing is resident, and an ask comes only from the listener's own navigation;
- **"Accept dips"**: 9.6's B and C lines are waived at an ask's own 1-3 s peak, every one measured dark, but apply again to anything held longer than a moment, as the snapshot is;
- **"Move it"**: no resident PSRAM, and the device decides PSRAM, not the model (13).

Where each call lands: 2, 3.1, 3.2, 4.2-4.6, 6.5, 6.8, 7.1-7.11, 8.2, 9.3, 9.7 (S3a and S3b: the work, the grades, the asks, the snapshot's life, the tests, the device runs), 11, 12, 13. Calls left open: none for S3's design. Still owed to the owner: the lit figures from run 3a-1 (and the cap, if a hold passes 3 s); call 2's two readings (above); any difference run 3a-4 finds; and S4's way to its row now that nothing is held while Now Playing shows (7.8).

**Status (2026-11-20): S2 built (C11-C15), reviewed, host-gated and device-checked (9.6's runs A-F on the spare, main 645679f the control), on the branch `claude/listening-stats-s2` from main 645679f; the owner's "A: nothing held"; the dips at an ask's peak accepted by the owner; pushed for its PR.** S2's sections as planned (6.1, 6.5, 6.7's S2 half, 6.8) were written before S1's rework (5.14) and held a hash → id map and the album bits resident, re-taken after every Live, and a rank copying a RAM snapshot of TRKD and RCNT. Since the rework RAM holds only the 40 records (`lib/core/StatsFold.h:182`), and 13 ("The resident set's budget", "PSRAM at the ceiling") says the map can't be resident. Asked, the owner chose **A: nothing held** (2026-11-20, relayed word for word: "A: nothing held"):
- nothing of S2 is held between asks, and S2 ranks only when asked: no rank at the boot, after a Live or at a day's change;
- each console ask (`Hr`, `Hl`, `Hw`) carves one block at the top through the hold (5.14's fix A: `autodj::TopHold`, caps), ranks on the card worker (`Job::Stats`, the Rank lane, gated against Auto DJ's join), walks on the loop, prints, and gives every block back;
- `Hp`'s read is a binary search of the base in the Track lane; no sparse index is kept;
- S3 decides how long a page holds its lists;
- device runs B and F become "`D` at the rank's peak" and "`Hw` during the boot's join" (9.6).

6.1, 6.4, 6.5, 6.7, 6.8, 9.3 and 9.6 each say what A replaced, beside the plan as it was. As built (9.6's "As built" has the commits, the tests and the figures):
- **C11** `lib/core/PathHashWalk` (`pathhash::Walk`, `trackPathHash()`): the join's Folders and Hash phases (at `lib/core/AutoDjJoin.cpp:818-881` before it; 6.2's `:584-647` had moved with fix A), the join's output unchanged.
- **C12** `lib/core/StatsRank`: 4.3's lists over the base, `stats.bin`'s TRKD and the records' copy (5.14's "The screens"), RCNT summed by hash, each list its cap and 16; the played set; checked against `tools/statsref.py`'s lists on every fixture boot and on new rank scenarios (`stats/rank.json`, every list past its cap).
- **C13** `lib/core/StatsMap`: the album bits and the lists' ids; Not played yet equal to statsref's on every fixture boot; the heap model's S2 half (`test_an_s2_ask_in_a_model_heap`: an ask's block leaves the heap as it found it, every run's figure the twin's; MODELLED: `Hw`'s and `Hl3`'s block at P = 19,478, 289,948 B, refused for room after a library update).
- **C14** the firmware: `Job::Stats` in its two lanes, `AutoDj::joinPending()` and the join's hold, `app/StatsLists` (the asks), the console's `Hr`, `Hl`, `Hw`, `Hp`.
- **C15** these docs, ARCHITECTURE's Auto DJ and Listening stats bullets, the README's console table and Layout. `docs/AUTODJ.md` 7.4 is unchanged: the join's blocks didn't move.

The review (9.1) ran, its fixes their own commit (72af6b9, "Code: what the review found": 9.6). The gate (9.6): the Done-when commands all pass; against main 645679f's CI build flash +19,280 B, RAM +24 B (`.bss`), IRAM left main's 5,157 B, and Auto DJ's PSRAM object main's size. The device pass (9.6, 2026-11-20) checked the spare card against main 645679f in the same scripts:
- all seven lists equal `statsref`'s;
- after every update and rejoin, each joined figure and every `D` outside an ask is main's to the byte;
- PSRAM free reads 56 B under main, from `CardTasks` growing 24 B with the ninth job;
- `Hr` ranks in 0.7-1.0 s, and `Hw` walks in 0.65-0.9 s;
- `Hp` equals `Ht`;
- after an update `Hw` is refused for room, as modelled;
- the Opus plays.

Run F found one defect, fixed in a5c2950: a rank's ask had held its block while queued behind the boot's walk.

**The owner accepts the dips (2026-11-20, relayed word for word: "Accept dips, push PR").** At an ask's own 1-3 s peak the largest free block dips under 9.6's B and C lines (`Hw`'s 204,788 B idle, `Hr`'s 233,460 B after one update). Those lines apply between asks, where S2 matches main. At the peak they don't apply: Auto DJ's join and a library update wait for the ask's block, and an ask is refused unless a decoder's buffers still fit beside it. If S3 holds a page's lists longer than a moment, the lines apply again. The push and the PR: the owner's word, the same message. The owner's calls left open:
- O5 (3.1, S3's);
- whether S3 must make `Hw`/Not played yet fit after an update on a card of nearly every track played (13's "PSRAM at the ceiling").

(Both are settled since, 2026-11-20, later, by the status above: O5 by call 4, "30 days, kept without clock"; the fit by call 3, "5-byte fingerprints".)

S1's gate after the give-back found that chunk was one more than 11's budget (16,384 B chunks hold no whole number of RCNT's or TRKD's rows, so RCNT's 4,096-row cap took a fourth) and fixed it, chunks of 16,392 B: **every shape is within the device's room, and the whole suite passes** ("S1's gate", below; 6.7). **S1's device round 1** ran on the owner's Core2 with the spare card (2026-11-12 UTC; 9.5's "Round 1"): A-D and J passed; H, K and L passed in part, and what they found is fixed (the plays waiting for their slots now come before `Hs` and before a library update's give-back, which since the fixes' review waits out a try the card failed too; the give-back's ring holds the Tracker's 32; `H`'s line for a save with nothing new; `statsref.py`'s `--now ...Z`: "S1's device round 1's fixes", below). E, F, G, I, the rest of H, K and L, and main's controls were owed. S1's device round 2, with main's control in the same script, then found what the rework answers (below).

**S1's rework (2026-11-12 to 2026-11-16): designed, reviewed and built, R1-R7** (5.14). S1's device round 2 and main's control in the same script:
- S1's largest free block after an Opus update was 188,404 B against main's 294,900 B (under the mood plan's 245,764 B line where main is over it), and 57,344 B under main's from the boot. The comparison found the cause: the resident blocks, carved again by good fit at each take-back, move where the decoders' long-lived blocks land.
- The owner's decision, "Rework S1": one block of at most 3,584 B held from setup (the state, HEAD, MARK's first 16 rows, 96 records of the slots since the last save); everything else in `stats.bin` alone, merged at each save (the boot's, one each 64 plays, `Hs`, the rotation's); no give-back, no re-read after an update.
- The design's review (2026-11-12, integrity and memory) kept that core and found 17 things, each checked and folded in (5.14's list): a held tmp would have counted a save's records twice and a held compaction lost its delta; a full log's first play rotates, so an unsaved boot overflow lost up to 6,553 plays after one fault (now its save is tried four times, the boot's waits apart); statsref couldn't express three save moments; the boot's scan keeps its wait for an asked update and the update waits for the scan's blocks; the records' save runs at 96 whatever a fence; a section damaged under the session stops the saves; the model's decoder carves weren't the device's.
- With the device's decoder requests the multi-update model fails none of 360 steps over 72 histories for the rework (main's own queue 2,048 B longer: 2; S1 as built: 47), and none of D, round 2's own sequence; with the old shapes 2, both main's variation's. Re-run that way, the scan's 10,896 B work memory held across its passes cost 8 steps, so the scan holds only its 3,072 B of ranges; and a full log's overflow (about 270 KB, once a card) fails up to 29 steps when a tap on Play opens the Opus during it, wherever it sits, so a track's start now waits the 1.4 s or so it is held. The model doesn't reproduce round 2 on round 2's own sequence: the device re-run against main's figures, not the model, accepts the design.
- The build plan is 5.14's R1-R7 and a device re-run against main's figures, within the class of main's own figure. Since R7, 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11 and 13 describe the rework as built (until then they described S1 as built, 414488c). R1 is built: the yardstick is `test_autodj_join`'s `test_the_listening_stats_across_updates`, which failed on purpose against S1 as built until R6 made it the rework's (`pio test -e native` exited 1 on it meanwhile). R2 is built: `tools/statsref.py` keeps the rework's save moments (the records and their save, the fence, Held, a boot overflow's tries, behind) and its 34 sessions are the fixtures; until R5 runs them through the firmware, `test_stats_store` fails two cases on purpose too (5.14's R2 "As built"). R3 is built: `lib/core/StatsFold` is the rework's fold (the records, MARK and HEAD in a 2,656 B object, the boot's overflow in chunks, the cursors a save merges the card's rows with), and `test_stats_fold` checks its merges against statsref byte for byte; S1 as built's fold stayed as `statsfold::s1::Fold` (`lib/core/StatsFoldS1`) until R6 deleted it (5.14's R3 "As built"). R4 is built: `lib/core/StatsFile`'s store reads `stats.bin` in one pass (HEAD and MARK into the fold, the rest only checked as it streams, MARK's block carved at the boot for a card past 8 rows) and saves by streaming, nothing in RAM but the records: a counting pass and a writing pass over the card's sections merged with them, and a compaction over three streams between the two; `test_stats_file` holds its files to statsref's byte for byte on the index's 34 boots and on 32 of the 34 sessions run through the store alone; S1 as built's store stayed as `statsfile::s1::Store` (`lib/core/StatsFileS1`) until R6 deleted it (5.14's R4 "As built"). R5 is built: `lib/core/StatsGlue` and the firmware run the rework (a 3,200 B glue object, the device's, held from setup; the records' save at 64 at a pass the write gate lets through, before its slot, not behind a library update's fence until 96; a boot overflow's save tried four times, a track's start, Auto DJ's join and an asked update waiting while the scan's blocks are held, `LibraryUpdate::Wait::Stats`); the give-back is gone; `test_stats_store` runs every index boot and all 34 sessions through the firmware's glue byte for byte; S1 as built's fold and store stayed for `test_autodj_join`'s S1 run until R6 (5.14's R5 "As built"). R6 is built: `test_autodj_join`'s `StatsRun` runs the firmware's own glue on a card in memory, every block it carves through the model heap, and the yardstick passes: the rework fails none of its 65 steps nor of the wide set's 360 with the device's decoders (with the old shapes 2 of 360, both the control's own), its boot's join main's, and `test_the_listening_stats_in_a_model_heap`'s 13 shapes join the control's 360,436 B. It found that every boot's scan, not a full log's alone, holds one overflow chunk, so a track's start waits during every boot's scan (5.14's R6 "As built"; the rework review took the scan's look-ahead out, to the owner's default). S1 as built's fold, store and run are deleted. R7 is built: the docs (`docs/METADATA.md` 3.11, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, the README's `H`; this plan's 1, 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11, 12 and 13 rewritten to the rework: 5.14's R7 "As built"). The rework review is applied (5.14's build plan, "The rework review"). Its gate is met on the host (2026-11-16, at 39513f1: "The rework's gate", above). Nothing of the rework is flashed: the device re-run is next. (Made since: the rework's and the move's re-runs, 2026-11-17, and fix A's runs, 2026-11-18: the statuses above.)

This document is the build plan, read and written at firmware bc29b54 (main; v0.14.0 released). PR #58 moves NEXT_RELEASE to 0.15.0 (`tools/version.py:54`), so the stats ship in 0.15.0 at the earliest.

**What S1 changed from this plan.** Each change is amended where its section stands, in the commit that made it, which says why:
- **The rework** (5.14, R1-R7, 2026-11-16; the owner's decision of 2026-11-12, "Rework S1", after S1's device round 2). Where the code departs from 5.14's design, each step's "As built" says so, and 5.14's text was fixed in the step's commit. The departures that matter: a save's work memory is 11,456 B on the device, not 10,896 B (R4: two containers, two row readers and the merge's rows beside the writer), 19,752 B with a save's two FatFiles; MARK's row is 28 B, keeping the seq a held tmp holds (R3); the glue's object is 3,200 B on the device (R5), under the 3,584 B `static_assert`; the compaction runs between the save's counting and writing passes (R4); the ranges' block is 1,284 B for 16 MARK rows and no ACKS row above them, 4 B over and aligned to 8 (R5); a boot overflow's save is tried again for no work memory and the card away too, not only a step the card failed (R5); the records sort by a heap sort (R5: `std::sort`'s introsort recursed on the loop's stack); a track's start waits through the headphones' `Hold`, and a track following one playing isn't held (R5), PlayGate deciding the rest since the rework review (`In::statsHold`); every boot's scan held one overflow chunk (its first piece reserved 128 slots, past the 96 records: R6), which the rework review took out (no look-ahead: `statsglue::kReserveAhead`), so a start waits only while a full log's fold holds chunks, once a card; and the model found the start's wait not load-bearing with the ranges' block at 1,284 B (R6) but load-bearing at its largest, 3,076 B (the rework review). statsref asks the records' save only at `play` and `hs` steps (R2). R7 folded 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11 and 13 into the rework; the give-back's text there is now history.
- **S1's device round 1** (2026-11-12 UTC, the owner's Core2; 9.5's "Round 1"). At the pass where the play log's write gate opened, the loop ran `Hs` before the Outbox wrote the slots that had waited for that same gate, so `Hs` saved "nothing new" with 4 plays waiting; and the update step waited for the gate only when the fold was already dirty, so a give-back found "nothing to save first" with 3 completed plays queued, and the ring took them. Now the plays waiting for their slots come first: `Hs` runs only at a pass with none due (`statsglue::turnOf()`), and the update step waits while one is queued, one behind a try the card failed too (`Glue::updateWaits()`; the fixes' review found it had waited only for those due, so a backlog behind a failed try could still fill the ring), so each is written, folded live, and held by that save (5.5, 6.7). The give-back's ring holds the Tracker's 32 plays, not 16 (5.6). `H` prints a save with nothing new against the card's generation, and `statsref.py` takes an ISO time ending in Z. The WriteGate, the update's safe point, the fence and the no-loss proof are unchanged.
- **The give-back** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-11, "Give back in updates"; gone with the rework, 5.14). S1's first gate found 6.7's pass rule failing in the heap model (C8, MODELLED): held through every library update, the stats' resident blocks came out of its largest free block at P = 19,478's resident ceiling and after a full never-synced log's fold (joined 180,212 B against the control's 360,436 B), and the ladder's steps 2-3 couldn't win back that much. Now, during a library update, the stats give their resident blocks back (MARK's kept), as Auto DJ's join already did, and read `stats.bin` from the card again after the update and the rejoin; a fold holding plays the card's `stats.bin` doesn't is saved first. Every shape of the model passes the rule (joined the control's 360,436 B; 16,384 B under it at P = 19,478 until S1's gate after the give-back, below): 6.7, and 5.4-5.8, 11, 13 where the data's rules change.
- **The give-back review** (2026-11-11, three lenses: no play lost, the heap model, the firmware's wiring), as built: a give-back whose save doesn't land (or a re-read that fails, a ring that overflows) can lose plays, to a rotation later in that session, where held through they'd have been saved; the docs, the comments and the `[listen]` texts had said "never a play" (5.5, 13; statsref's `update-behind-rotation`, 2 lost). A re-read the card fails is made again at later passes, the boot's waits apart, before the stats go off (5.4). The boot's scan waits while a library update is asked, and a fold not begun yet is given back, so it never begins or grows behind the update's memory check while the build runs (5.6, 6.7). The give-back's save waits for a pass the play log's write gate lets through (`LibraryUpdate::Wait::Writes`: the safe point is at least 20-30 s left, so it let a track's first seconds through) (5.5). `H` says what waits for the re-read from the glue's state (it had kept a count never reset). The heap model gains the device's room (16,368 B over the line after a FLAC update, MOOD-FILTERS Run D), which two P = 19,478 shapes crossed until S1's gate after the give-back (the chunks, next), and an update asked before the boot's fold (6.7).
- **The chunks** (S1's gate after the give-back, afbb05d, "Stats: what the gate found"; the rework keeps no RCNT or TRKD in RAM, and its overflow's chunks of 16,384 B hold 819 records, 4 B over). RCNT's and TRKD's chunks are 16,392 B, whole rows of both tables (1,366 and 683), not 16,384 B (1,365 and 682, 4 B and 16 B over), so RCNT's 4,096-row cap fills three and TRKD's 96 KB at the forced compaction six, as 11 has them; 16,384 B took a fourth and a seventh for the caps' last rows. Each carves the same 16,900 B (6.5). At P = 19,478 the re-read after an update is 178,576 B (194,880 B before), and joined is the control's in every shape of the model (6.7). The full log's fold peaks at 396,744 B (5.6, 11).
- **The S1 review** (2026-11-11) found, and the code now does: a fold out of memory is never saved, even with no work memory (it had trimmed plays never folded); a log file whose open finds damaged records is a file not read whole (5.6); the boot's fold's blocks and a remount's read of `stats.bin` wait for the scan's first piece, in a pass that may carve, never `setup()`'s or the card guard's (5.4, 5.6); stats off give every block back; RCNT is held to its cap at the fold's end, so a boot whose save doesn't land holds no full log's rows (5.6); a held tmp is promoted in its own call, off the save's stack (5.5, 11); a play waiting for the headphones, or a restart asked, counts as playing for a compaction (5.3); the heap model carves the glue and the read in the firmware's order and adds two shapes whose save doesn't land (6.7); the stack sums were too low (11); the gap counts the ACKS rows the root keeps (5.6).
- **The seq rule (C5) is its own commit**, f45fb48: the owner kept it on 2026-11-18. Two of the proof's sessions (`nvs-back`, `log-deleted`) rest on it (9.5's C8 "As built").
- **The reference was wrong once** (c1b0751): after a lost base 1, a boot's compaction wrote under the name `stats.bin` names, and a cut there left the stats off for good. `tools/statsref.py`, the `base-other` fixture and 5.3 were fixed.
- **The fold's peak** on a full never-synced log is 396,744 B, through the journal and in `test_stats_fold`'s own scan (396,608 B and 412,992 B with 16,384 B chunks: the chunks, above), MEASURED-HOST: RCNT grows too, pruned only at the save. This plan had modelled TRKD's growth alone (5.6, 6.7, 11).
- **The fold** (5.6): HOUR and DAYS drop their oldest row at their caps; the boot's index is freed at the scan's end, before the save; with no memory for an index the fold goes on without it; live folds keep at most 64 rows unsorted.
- **The files** (5.3-5.5, METADATA 2.12.6): the base is read whole at the boot, not its header alone (11's card reads); damaged records are read once; `StatsCheck` keeps a tmp with damaged records (Unknown); a remount never renames `stats.bad`, which never shares a cluster chain; a held tmp is promoted only while its header is the one written; a base's frame is written by hand, last.
- **The glue** (9.5's C9 "As built"): its rules are portable (`lib/core/StatsGlue`), so the proof runs the firmware's own; the work memory is carved for each read and save, not held; the ending flag is set as `flush()` starts; Hs's refusals and their order; no PSRAM for the glue: no `Folds` (0.14.0's trim).
- **The proof** (9.5's C8 "As built"): a second set of statsref fixtures, `stats/sessions.json` (15 sessions, 154 steps); `FlakyFs` and `FaultFs` moved to `test/support/FaultFs.h`.
- **The play log's hooks** (C4): `openRead()` opens every log file, with or without folds (the same card commands); `Journal::Booted` gains `partial`; `written()` comes after `tried()`; the journal itself asks no save after a partial scan, and the glue refuses one too.
- METADATA 3.10's "not flashed" is corrected (1.1).

**S1's device round 1's fixes** (2026-11-11, local time; the code at e37104b, MEASURED-HOST; the tests from Git Bash, the builds from PowerShell with MSYSTEM unset; 9.5's "Round 1" has what the device showed):
- b355229 "Stats: the plays waiting for their slots come first": `statsglue::turnOf()` (a slot while one is due, `Hs` only once none is) and `Glue::updateWaits()` (the update step's `writesWait`: a slot due, or, as before, a save first with the gate shut), in lib/core so the proof runs them; `PlayHistory::slotsDue()` (the Tracker's plays, the journal ready, the card not away, the Outbox's head due). df1e025: the give-back's ring is the Tracker's 32 (statsref's `RING` too). e37104b: `H`'s line for a save with nothing new. c6bd292: `statsref.py`'s ISO times ending in Z.
- `pio test -e native`: 111 suites, **2,351 test cases, all passed** (2,349 + `test_stats_store`'s `test_the_plays_waiting_come_first` and `test_many_short_plays_across_a_give_back`: 20 cases), no ERRORED suite, 20 min 20 s (the builds ran beside it), exit 0. The heap model: every joined, given-back, taken-again and held-through figure unchanged; only "free at least while placing" moved with the ring's 1,280 B (6.7).
- `python tools/card_fixtures.py --check`: 663 files up to date (647 + 16: `update-ring-full` writes the ring's 32 and 2 more; 24 sessions, 294 steps). `test_statsref`: 59 OK (+ the Z). `test_playlog`: 32 OK. `test_synthtransfer`: 63 OK.
- Mutations (each alone, the file restored after): `Hs` before the slots, the update ignoring the slots, the update ignoring the shut gate, the update waiting with nothing to give back, the ring at 16: **5 of 5 caught**.
- `pio run -e core2 -e core2-dio`: both SUCCESS, no warnings, every guard ok. core2 (the tree at e37104b with these docs uncommitted, `v0.14.0-50-ge37104b-dirty`): flash **4,474,359 B** (520 B over S1's gate's 4,473,839 B; its version string 6 B longer than a clean one's), RAM **59,160 B** (unchanged); core2-dio 4,474,391 B. The link map, both: **IRAM left 5,157 B** and the `libc_a-*` objects S0's 25, as at S1's gate. `sizeof(statsglue::Glue)` 5,136 B on the device (gdb on the core2 ELF; 5,824 B on the host).
- **The fixes' review** (2026-11-11, the code at f073406, MEASURED-HOST). The update step had waited only for a play due (`slotsDue()`), so while the Outbox's head waited out the 5 s after a try the card failed, the give-back went ahead with the backlog behind it (up to the Tracker's 32), which then went into the ring; one more play ending while given back passed it and turned the session's saves off (reproduced on the host: given back with 32 queued, ring 32, then 1 past it, behind). f073406 "Stats: what the round-1 fix review found": `PlayHistory::slotsQueued()` (the Tracker's plays, the journal ready, the card not away; no head's `due()`) is what `statsSaveWaits()` gives `Glue::updateWaits()`, so the update waits out a failed try, at most 10 s a play the card goes on failing (5.5); `Hs` keeps `slotsDue()`. `test_stats_store` 21: `test_a_backlog_behind_a_failed_try`, which failed before the fix (the give-back with plays queued behind a failed try). Round 1's L4 had the order at the gate too (9.5's "Round 1"). `TagConsole`'s scan line is 212 B at most with the play log's wait name (its comment said 208; the 224 B buffer holds it). `pio test -e native`: 111 suites, **2,352 test cases, all passed**, no ERRORED suite, 14 min 40 s (the builds ran beside it), exit 0. `card_fixtures.py --check` 663 up to date, `test_statsref` 59 OK, `test_playlog` 32 OK. `pio run -e core2 -e core2-dio` (the tree at f073406 with these docs uncommitted, `v0.14.0-52-gf073406-dirty`): both SUCCESS, every guard ok; core2 flash **4,474,431 B** (72 B over e37104b's), RAM **59,160 B** (unchanged); core2-dio 4,474,463 B; both link maps: **IRAM left 5,157 B**, the `libc_a-*` objects S0's 25.
- **9.5's Done-when: met on the host**, as at S1's gate (IRAM's 4 B still the owner's call). The device's runs owed: E, F, G, I, the rest of H, K and L, H and L again for these fixes, and main's controls.

**S1's gate: built and host-gated with the give-back; device pass owed.** 9.5's Done-when commands again after the give-back and its review, 2026-11-11, at afbb05d (MEASURED-HOST; the tests from Git Bash, the builds from PowerShell with MSYSTEM unset). At dc77a7e the heap-model case still failed as the review had left it: `test_autodj_join` ERRORED on the device's room alone (P = 19,478 with 300 slots, and with both saves refused: joined -16,384 B); that run was stopped once it had shown this, 60 suites in, every other one passed. The gate found the cause, which was not the ladder's to answer: a chunk of 16,384 B holds no whole number of RCNT's 12 B rows (1,365, 4 B over) or TRKD's 24 B rows (682, 16 B over), so RCNT at its 4,096-row cap took a fourth chunk for its last row, and TRKD at the forced compaction's 96 KB a seventh, where 11's budget has three and six. The model's P = 19,478 shapes load RCNT's cap, and that fourth chunk is the one the re-read took from the largest free block. Fixed in afbb05d ("Stats: what the gate found"): chunks of 16,392 B, 1,366 and 683 rows, nothing over, which carve the same 16,900 B on the device's heap (6.5); the re-read at P = 19,478 is 178,576 B (194,880 B before) and every shape's joined is the control's (6.7). No test was weakened: `test_stats_fold`'s memory case asserts the caps' chunks (3 and 6) and the boot whose save doesn't land RCNT in exactly 3; the heap model loads TRKD's whole 96 KB at P = 19,478 (4,096 rows, not the 4,092 six 16,384 B chunks held) and pins every shape again, the device's room check unchanged. With the fix undone (16,384 B), `test_stats_fold`'s memory case and the heap-model case fail.
- `pio test -e native`: 111 suites, **2,349 test cases, all passed**, none skipped or ignored, no ERRORED suite (the give-back review's run: 2,349 Unity cases, 1 failed). `test_stats_fold` 20, `test_stats_file` 13, `test_stats_store` 18, `test_play_log` 42, `test_card_root` 34, `test_autodj_join` 27 (the stats' case passing whole), `test_library_update` 39. 18 min 50 s (the builds and the Python commands ran beside it), exit 0. The six suites of 9.5's first command alone: 154 cases, all passed.
- `python tools/card_fixtures.py --check`: 647 files up to date. `test_statsref`: 58 OK. `test_playlog`: 32 OK. `test_synthtransfer`: 63 OK (81.9 s). The same at dc77a7e.
- `pio run -e core2 -e core2-dio`: both SUCCESS, no warnings (167 s and 173 s, a rebuild of what includes `StatsFold.h`). core2 is `v0.14.0-45-gafbb05d`, ELF a28dfc29: flash **4,473,839 B** (71.1 %; the app 4.37 MB, 73 % of its slot), 48 B under dc77a7e's 4,473,887 B and 50,148 B over S0's 4,423,691 B; RAM **59,160 B** (`.dram0.data` 25,112 B, `.dram0.bss` 34,048 B: 96 B over S0, as before). `iram_diet: 51 of 51 libc objects moved to flash, the MP3 hot set pinned (9 code and 3 data lines; sections.ld 140237 -> 121839 bytes)`; `cache_guard` and `flash_guard` ok. core2-dio: ELF 079fcb7c, flash 4,473,871 B, RAM the same, DIO 40 MHz, its guards ok.
- The link map, both builds: `.iram0.text` 0x40080404 + 0x1E7D7, ending 0x4009EBDB: **IRAM left 5,157 B**, as at S1's first gate (4 B under S0's 5,161 B: the libphy literal, below); its `libc_a-*` objects are **S0's 25 exactly** (bzero ... tolower), none added or missing.
- `git status`: clean after the docs' commit. Nothing pushed.
- **9.5's Done-when: met on the host**, every command exiting 0 and the libc list S0's, but for IRAM left, 4 B under S0's figure: whether 11's check counts that libphy word is still the owner's call. **Not checked on a device**: the runs A-L (L with the give-back's lines and a P = 19,478 card) wait for the owner's go-ahead.

The device pass (9.5's runs A-L) is owed and waits for the owner's go-ahead. (The first count is settled since: the give-back, below.)

**The give-back's gate** (2026-11-11, the code at ff05909; MEASURED-HOST, as above):

No ERRORED suite. 13 min 21 s, exit 0.

(The docs' commit after it changes the version string's count alone, at the same length.)

The failure is `test_autodj_join`'s heap-model case, on purpose: the device's room alone (6.7), for P = 19,478 with 300 slots and with both saves refused (joined -16,384 B); every other assertion of it passes, and every figure pinned before is unchanged. Every other suite passed.

The lines' frames, read from the core2 ELF, in 11.

**Designed** on the Claude Design canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/MhGiBt7yLecJZEErFQgSTq. Its final frames are Entry, Main, MostPlayed, NewFinds, NoClock, EarlyDays, NoPlays, NowPlayingSheet and Week. AlbumPlays was dropped by the owner (decision O3). The A- (Glance), B- (Recap) and C- frames are the directions not taken (14). The final spec lived in a scratch folder that Windows cleans up after about a week, so its essentials are inlined here (sections 2, 4, 5, 7, 8). Where this plan departs from it, 1.1 and 3.2 say so. The nine final frames are in [docs/img/listening-stats](img/listening-stats/README.md), captured from the canvas (C1).

**Picked by the owner, 2026-11-10:** O1-O4 and O6 of 3.1. **O5 is open.** (Settled 2026-11-20, later: the owner's "30 days, kept without clock", 3.1.)

**Assumed:** everything else. 3.2 lists each default with its alternative, and the owner can overrule any of them.

Line numbers are at these commits:
- firmware bc29b54
- phone app (`mstream_music`) d17c3fa5
- terminal player origin/main 410444f, and `claude/card-transfer` 3edd4a4 (PR #56)
- `docs/METADATA.md` as S0's branch holds it: main 241642d's, with C2's edits and S0's review (main since bc29b54 changed only `tools/version.py` and docs, so the firmware's lines hold at 241642d too)

S1's commits have since moved lines: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp`'s and `src/app/PlayHistory.cpp`'s, and `docs/METADATA.md`'s (its status paragraph, 2.12.6, 2.18, 3.10 and the new 3.11 add lines). The references here stay at the commits above.

Paths with no repository named are the firmware's.

Labels:
- **READ:** from the code or docs.
- **MEASURED:** on the device, quoted from the docs.
- **MEASURED-HOST:** a script or test run on a PC.
- **ESTIMATED:** the docs' own word, or a scaling of their figures.
- **MODELLED:** arithmetic, not run.
- **GUESS:** judgement.

### 1.1 Where the spec and the design run's maps disagree with the code

The code wins. Each item is READ at bc29b54.

- **The spec's "the card worker saves stats before `finishScan()` trims" has no window to run in.** `finishScan()` runs on the loop, called from the scan's last piece (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:315`), and in one call it stores the boot count, trims and sets Ready (`:318-347`). The save runs there, on the loop (5.5).
- **A journal state between the scan and Ready would drop plays.** `append()` returns Off for any state but Ready (`:523`), and the Outbox then drops that play for good (`:724-729`). `Outbox::flush()` would spin to its deadline (`:738-744`), since `scan()` returns true for any state but Scanning (`:246`). So no new state.
- **The scan reads nothing on a new epoch with no ACKS** (`:162-164`). After an NVS erase the old epoch's slots, and other players' (O2), would never be folded. With stats on, the scan always reads (5.6).
- **The scan reads the newest file first:** plays.jnl, then plays.1.jnl, then plays.2-9 (`:81-86`, `:249-256`). Fold rules that depend on order take it from the slot's own fields, never from the scan's order (5.6).
- **The spec's three files renamed one by one** (stats.bin, stats-trks.bin, stats-trks.d.bin) can disagree after a cut between two renames: rows counted twice or lost. This plan has one commit file that names its base file (5.2, 5.3).
- **"Skip the trim when the save fails"** (spec 8.3.3) can deadlock a full card: no save because it is full, no trim because the save failed. This plan trims a file when the MARK already on the card covers it (5.5).
- **The rotation deletes `plays.1.jnl` mid-session** with no ACKS check (`:411-463`, the delete at `:437`). The spec doesn't mention it. On a card never synced it is the only deletion. This plan saves first when that file holds a play the card's MARK doesn't cover (5.5).
- **A resident table keyed by track id can't name the rankers' hashes.** RCNT and TRKS are keyed by path hash, so the 7- and 30-day tops, New finds and Recently played come out as hashes. This plan keeps no per-track table resident (6.1).
- **The spec's walk cost** ("1.5 s idle, 3 s playing" with Auto DJ off) is the whole join's. A walk for stats is Folders and Hash only: no card reads, no sort. ESTIMATED 0.1-0.2 s of CPU (6.3).
- **The spec's redraw costs are low.** A row redrawn in place is about 11 ms with its push (`src/ui/ListView.h:273-274`), not 3 ms; a header push (320 x 36) about 5 ms, not 1. Section 7.10 uses these.
- **METADATA 3.10 says the play log was "not flashed"** (`docs/METADATA.md:7233`). The 0.14.0 checks ran it on the device: slots written in 14.8-16.6 ms (`docs/RELEASE-0.14.0-CHECKS.md:60`). S1's docs commit (C10) corrected it.
- **The terminal's time zone on Windows.** The design run's process map said `src/admin/tz.rs` gives no zone on Windows. Terminal origin/main reads the zone Windows is set to since 5eac90c (2026-10-29; terminal `src/admin/tz.rs:1-10, 32-49`). #56's branch (3edd4a4) doesn't have 5eac90c: there a Windows machine gets no zone (its `src/admin/tz.rs:6-7`). No branch sends it in `@time` yet (10).
- **ScanScheduler's jobs** are at `lib/core/ScanScheduler.h:101-102` (`kJobs` 8), not `:119-120`.
- **The log prefix.** `[stats]` is the audio's line (console `s`). The stats' lines are `[listen]`.

## 2. What the listener gets

**The way in.** Library > Artists gains a first row: seven small bars for the last seven days, "Listening stats", and "7 h 37 min in the last 7 days". It scrolls with the list. A tap opens the hub. From Albums or Folders: a tap on Artists brings its list back, a second scrolls it to the top, where the row is. Now Playing's navigation sheet (a tap on the cover or the title) gains a first row, "Go to Most played" with "9 plays · last Sun · 1 skip" under it. It opens the shelf at that track, tinted.

**The hub**, Listening stats. Under its title, the time listened since counting began ("74 h 10 min since 1 Sep"). Six rows follow, each with an icon, a title and a line:
- Last 7 days: "7 h 37 min · 118 plays"
- Most played: the top three titles
- New finds: "41 tracks first heard in 30 days"
- Old favourites: "38 tracks, played often, not lately"
- Not played yet: "612 albums since 1 Sep"
- Recently played: "Slow Lantern · 25 min ago"

*As decided (2026-11-20, later; section 1's calls 1, 2 and 4):*
- **The entry row's line** is the lifetime one, "74 h 10 min since 1 Sep" (HEAD's, in RAM), and its seven bars are a fixed icon, not the week. It shows as soon as the boot's fold is done.
- **The hub** shows its header sub at once. Rows 1-6 read "Counting plays…" until the hub's one ask lands, about 1-2 s (about 50 s after a reset: "Waiting for the card…").
- **Row 5** gives its count only when that ask could hold the played set. Otherwise it reads "since 1 Sep", and Not played yet, opened, says "Not enough memory right now" with Try again, which makes its own smaller ask.
- **The lists** stay while the listener moves between the stats pages and the albums opened from them. Leaving them, another tab or the dark lets them go, and coming back counts again.
- **Plays counted while a page is open** don't change it. The next visit has them.

**Last 7 days** is a page of its own: the time listened and the plays, seven bars with today in violet, the day listened most ("Saturday was your big day: 2 h 1 min"), and how often Auto DJ's picks became plays.

**The shelves** are Library lists that play like any list.
- Most played opens on All time, with a tray for 7 days and 30 days (O1). Each row: its rank, the title, "Artist · 12 plays".
- New finds: tracks first heard in the last 30 days, and who found them ("found by Auto DJ").
- Old favourites: tracks with 5 plays or more and none in 30 days.
- Not played yet: albums with no play, A-Z, with covers.
- Recently played: tracks newest first, under day headings.

A tap on a track opens its bar (Play, Play next, + Queue); a hold opens its sheet, as on an album page. The top bar plays the shelf.

**Early and odd states.**
- Nothing played yet: the hub is an empty state with Shuffle all.
- The clock never set: the totals and Most played (All time) work; the rest that needs dates says so, with the fix ("Plug in to the MP3 Player tab").
- The first month: New finds, Old favourites and Not played yet say when they open ("Opens 6 Dec, after a month of plays"), and Most played's tray grows with the days counted.
- No card: the row goes, and an open hub says plays can't be counted.

**What doesn't change.**
- A, B and C stay prev / vol-, play-pause / output, next / vol+.
- Album and artist pages stay as they are (O3).
- The play log's slots, files and ACKS rules stay as METADATA 2.20 has them, but for these device-side changes (5.5, 5.6): the boot's scan reads on a new epoch; a log file is trimmed only once the stats hold its plays; a rotation may save the stats before its delete; the boot's seq rule also takes the stats' and ACKS's highest seq for this device and epoch.

**The same as mStream:** a play is `playedMs >= min(30 s, half the track)` (mStream decides by this rule: `docs/METADATA.md` 2.20.6, "The post"); its id stays the slot's (2.20.4).

**Where it differs:**

| Topic | mStream | The player |
|---|---|---|
| Whose plays | The account's, from every client | The plays on this card, from every player that wrote them (O2) |
| Which plays | Those posted | The slots on the card. Plays under 1 s were never logged (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:377`) |
| Days | Its own time zone | UTC days until the terminal's zone arrives (O4) |
| History | Retention, 24 months by default | Since counting began on this card |
| A renamed or converted file | The server's track | A new path hash: its old plays stay in the totals and leave the shelves (6.6) |

## 3. Decisions

### 3.1 The owner's (2026-11-10)

| Id | Decision | Status | Alternative |
|---|---|---|---|
| O1 | Most played opens on All time. Its tray still offers 7 days and 30 days | **owner's pick** | Open on 30 days (the spec) |
| O2 | Every player's plays on this card count: no deviceId filter | **owner's pick** | This player's only |
| O3 | Album pages stay as they are: no plays line on album track rows. Per-track plays show in the shelves and the Now Playing sheet's first row only | **owner's pick** | The plays line (frame AlbumPlays) |
| O4 | Build on UTC days now. The terminal's time zone in `@time` comes later, applied at display only | **owner's pick** | Wait for the zone |
| O5 | When Not played yet opens | **Settled (2026-11-20, later; the owner's "30 days, kept without clock": section 1, call 4).** After 30 days of counting, as New finds and Old favourites; sooner once mStream's counts reach the card (later, not v1: 5.11, 14). With the clock unset: open when the card's DAYS hold 30 or more days between their first and newest dated day (the visit's ask reports both: 9.7), else disabled with `stats.needs_dates`. (Was: **OPEN**, assumed disabled whenever the clock is unset, as New finds) | From the first day; or disabled whenever the clock is unset |
| O6 | The console's silent test mode `z` keeps logging plays, and they count | **owner's pick** | Not folded |

O5 is one constant in one function, `statsview::notPlayedOpen(countingDays, dated)` (7.11), so the answer changes one line and its test. Its no-clock case goes to the owner with it. *As decided (2026-11-20, later):* `notPlayedOpen(countingDays, dated, datedSpanDays)`: dated, `countingDays >= 30`; else `datedSpanDays >= 30`, where `datedSpanDays` is the ask's newest dated day − its first + 1 over DAYS ⊕ the records (0 before the ask lands, or with no dated day). DAYS keeps its newest 400 rows (5.2), so a span cut by that retention is past 30 anyway. Before the ask lands with no clock, row 5 is pending, not disabled.

### 3.2 Smaller defaults made by this plan

All are the plan's, and the owner can overrule each.

| Area | Default | Alternative |
|---|---|---|
| Data | One commit file, `stats.bin`, that names a base file of per-track rows (5.2, 5.3) | The spec's three files |
| Data | The save runs synchronously on the loop at the scan's end, before the trim (5.5) | A worker save, with a journal state between scan and Ready (1.1 says why not) |
| Data | A file is trimmed only when the card's MARK covers every play in it. A failed save still trims what the old MARK covers | Skip the trim when the save fails |
| Data | A rotation over a `plays.1.jnl` holding plays the card's MARK doesn't cover saves first, in the orderly end's flush too when 1 s of its budget is left. A failed save there doesn't hold the rotation back | Let those plays go uncounted |
| Data | The boot's seq rule takes the highest of NVS's seq, the card log's, MARK's and ACKS's for this device and epoch (5.6). 2.20.4 allows it: a device MAY reserve seqs | MARK only; or today's rule |
| Data | Drop notes are not folded. No "lost" figure in v1 | Fold their counts into a lost figure |
| Data | A slot with an unknown outcome reads as stopped; source 0 and above 3 count as "you" | |
| Data | HOUR keeps its newest 840 rows and DAYS its newest 400, never pruned by date; RCNT keeps 32 days counted back from the earlier of today and the newest dated day folded, at most 4,096 rows, and nothing by date with the clock unset (5.2) | The spec's 13 months of HOURS; pruning by date from today |
| Data | No listened time per track | 4 B more a row |
| Data | `lastOrd` is a fold round (one a boot fold, one a session), not a counter a play | A counter a play: 4 B more a row |
| Data | firstAt is a track's earliest dated play, whatever the order of its plays; a track with no dated play is never a New find (5.2) | The first play's time, dated or not |
| Data | MARK rows are kept for good: one a (deviceId, epoch) ever folded on the card (5.2) | ACKS's leave-out rule |
| Data | A boot whose scan couldn't open or read a log file whole: no save this session; the next boot folds again (5.6) | Save what was read |
| Data | A save is refused when the card's `stats.bin` isn't the copy RAM stands on (another player saved meanwhile, or it was deleted on a PC) (5.5) | Save over it |
| Data | A `stats.bin` unread at the boot: no fold, no save, no trim this session; read again (once, no waits) at a remount before the boot's first slot | Fold into a session-only view |
| Data | A corrupt `stats.bin` is renamed `stats.bad` and counting starts again from the log (the spec's rule) | Keep it and stop |
| Data | The base is compacted only at a boot's save while nothing plays, when the delta is over 32 KB or a quarter of the base; past 96 KB at a boot's save at the update step's safe point, else at the next boot's (5.3) | Any save |
| Data | A newer minor of `stats.bin` is read; a save over it drops what it added, so a minor adds only what a save can do without (5.12) | A newer minor holds the saves back |
| Map | The walk runs alone, through code shared with Auto DJ's join, never in the join's pass (6.2) | Ride the join while Auto DJ is on |
| Map | No resident per-track table: ids only for RCNT's hashes and the lists' | The spec's played-track table |
| UI | The hub's Most played line shows the All time top three, the shelf's opening window (as decided, 2026-11-20: from the visit's ask, "Counting plays…" until it lands: 4.4) | The 30-day top three (spec 3.2) |
| UI | Most played's All time sub is "since 1 Sep" (rule 5.4 of the spec: every lifetime figure says its window) | "all time" (frame MostPlayed) |
| UI | A window with no plays keeps its tray and shows "Nothing in this period." under it | Fall back to All time |
| UI | The top bar's Play follows the house's container rule: with shuffle on it starts at random (`src/ui/LibraryPage.cpp:810-815`) | Always from No. 1 (spec 3.3) |
| UI | The NP sheet's row shows only when the target shelf holds the track: the All time top 50, else Recently played's 200 | Open Recently played at its top |
| UI | The NP sheet's row waits for the track's record: no row until it is read | The row without its skips |
| UI | (As decided, 2026-11-20, for the two rows above: no snapshot is held while Now Playing shows (call 1), so the row's "in the top 50 or Recently played's 200" needs an ask of its own when the sheet opens. That is S4's to design and put to the owner (7.8); S3 keeps nothing for it) | |
| UI | Recently played's lines give relative times within 24 h, then the artist alone; no clock times while days are UTC | "· 21:40" (spec 3.4) |
| UI | Ranks on Most played, New finds and Old favourites; none on Recently played | |
| UI | "(UTC)" only in Week's sub | On every date |
| UI | The tray splits at each gap's middle plus `filtergrid::skew()` (7.9) | The spec's 114 / 220 |
| UI | The artist page's " · N plays" is left out, read as part of O3 | Add it: about 5 KB of sums |
| UI | Stats unread, newer or with no memory: no entry row, as with the log off | A line saying so |
| UI | Totals show once the boot's fold ends; names once the walk ends (4.4). *Replaced (2026-11-20, later; calls 1-2):* the entry line and the hub's header sub (lifetime, HEAD in RAM) show at once once the boot's fold ends; every other figure (Last 7 days, the lists, the counts, the names) shows when the visit's ask lands, 1-2 s after the stats pages are entered (4.4's Working) | Totals from the card at once |
| UI | (2026-11-20, later; call 1) The lists are held only while on the stats pages: entered, an ask; left (Back to Artists, another tab, Now Playing), the dark, the fence, a Live, `browse`, a remount's new index, the card away or the stats off, freed. An album, artist page, bar or sheet opened from a stats page counts as on them, so Back from it asks nothing | Held across tabs with a linger (the design run's 60 s); or per page |
| UI | (2026-11-20, later) No ask by itself: only entering the stats pages, coming back to them (the Library tab shown again on one), a wake on one, and Try again (Not played yet's lean ask among them: 4.4). None at a boot, a Live, a day's change, a fold, a minute, a shelf's push, the card's return or while a page sits open | Re-rank on folds or at a new day; the lean ask at Not played yet's push (a fifth ask point) |
| UI | (2026-11-20, later) No patches: a page shows its lists as of the ask; a play counted while it is open changes no row; the next visit's ask has it | Patch counts and Recently played in place (7.6 as planned, the design run's ring) |
| UI | (2026-11-20, later; the owner's default (a)) An ask from a tap ranks lit, at priority 0, unmeasured: S3a measures it first. If a visit's holds pass 3 s in all, S3 caps them (the default form: UI asks rank at priority 1 and walk with the dark's 8 ms passes; a visit's block given back at 3 s in all, then Waiting with Try again) and the figures go to the owner | Rank every UI ask at priority 1 from the start; accept the lit holds as measured (the owner's to choose) |
| UI | (2026-11-20, later; the owner's default (b)) For about 50 s after a reset a stats page says "Waiting for the card…" while Auto DJ's join and the boot's card walk run | Let a UI ask go ahead of the boot's walk (a scheduler change); a cache file of the lists on the card |
| UI | (2026-11-20, later) "Couldn't read the stats" only for a read fault (Io, Changed, Damaged, Base). A UI ask takes no block while the Rank lane would wait (input, the ring low, an underrun, a slow decode pass, a track change, a seek, Bluetooth, a lit list moving) or while S2's gates hold it: it shows "Waiting for the card…" after 3 s, holding nothing. A wait that comes after the take gives the block back in that pass, and the ask waits again, uncounted | Take the block and queue it (S2's console asks); give up after a count of yields |
| UI | (2026-11-20, later; call 2: the UI's object doesn't grow) Most played's window is kept for the visit (a byte of the snapshot, set by `statsSetWindow()`), not the session; Week is drawn by `LibraryPage` through a body hook, not a page object of its own (7.7) | The window kept for the session and Week its own `Page` member of `ui::Ui`, if `ui::Ui`'s carve stays main's (gdb) |
| UI | (2026-11-20, later) A shelf's Play, Play next, + Queue, top bar and sheet act on its ids where the snapshot holds them (u16, in its rows), through the queue's id-view form: no copy, no carve (7.6) | A transient u32 array of the shelf's ids, carved at each act (800 B at most), held across the decoder's open |
| Frames | "No plays yet" (the phone app's `listeningEmptyTitle`) for the frame's "Nothing played yet"; "Listening time" (`listeningTileTime`) for "Listened"; "Recently played" (`autoDjRecentlyPlayed`). No translation owed for them but the short forms in 8.1 | The frames' words, translated |
| Frames | NoClock's open Not played yet row is drawn disabled, with `stats.needs_dates` (O5's no-clock case, 4.4). *As decided (2026-11-20, later; call 4):* drawn open, as the frame draws it, on a card whose DAYS span 30 days or more; disabled with `stats.needs_dates` otherwise | Open, as the frame draws it |
| Frames | (2026-11-20, later; call 2) Entry draws "7 h 37 min in the last 7 days" and the week's bars; the plan draws the lifetime line ("74 h 10 min since 1 Sep") and the bars as a fixed icon, `statsview::kEntryBars`, in every state the row shows (Folding, Empty and No dates too). Hub row 1's icon is the same fixed bars | The frame's week line and bars (a week held in RAM, which call 2 rules out) |
| Frames | Toasts follow the code: two lines only when the text is too wide for one Body line (`src/ui/Overlays.cpp:105`). "Needs a month of plays: Opens on 6 Dec" is one line in English | A forced split |
| Frames | The frames are browser renders (`.dc.html`): checked for layout and colour, not pixel for pixel | |
| Console | `H` for the stats (history), `uiS…` for the screens (9.3) | |
| Time zone | `@time`'s field 1, minutes east of UTC, kept in NVS `clock/tz` at schema 7 | In `stats.bin`'s HEAD |
| Release | S1 may ship on its own: counting starts with it. S5a runs before any build carrying S1 boots the owner's music card (9.9) | Hold until S3 |

## 4. The behaviour, precisely

### 4.1 Definitions

| Term | Rule |
|---|---|
| A slot that counts | Kind 1, valid (MPPL, major 1, its CRC), used (epoch not 0, seq at least 1, deviceId not all zeros: `docs/METADATA.md` 2.20.6 item 2), once per (deviceId, epoch, seq) |
| The line | `min(30,000 ms, durationMs / 2)`; 30,000 ms when durationMs is 0. The half is not rounded, as mStream's `decideCounted` takes it (playedMs ≥ 30,000, or playedMs ≥ durationMs × 0.5): in integers, a play is 2 × playedMs ≥ min(60,000, durationMs) |
| A play | A slot that counts with playedMs at or over the line, whatever its outcome |
| A skip | Outcome 2 (skipped) with playedMs under the line. Completed or stopped under the line is neither. Outcome 0 or above 3 reads as stopped (2.20.3) |
| Listened time | The sum of playedMs over every slot that counts: plays, skips and the rest |
| Source | 3 Auto DJ, 2 shuffle, anything else "you" (1 manual, 0 not said, above 3) |
| Dated | startedAt from 2026-01-01 to 2099-12-31 UTC (`wallclock::believable()`, `lib/core/WallClock.h:27-31`). Else undated: 0 while the clock was unset, or another device's clock |
| Its hour, its day | Of startedAt, UTC. hour = (startedAt − kMinMs) / 3,600,000; day = (startedAt − kMinMs) / 86,400,000; day 0 is 2026-01-01 |
| A time in a row | Seconds since 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z, plus 1. 0 means none (lastAt) or only undated plays (firstAt) |
| Today | The board's clock now, UTC. None while it is unset (`app/BoardClock`) |
| Counting since (sinceDay) | Set once, at a fresh `stats.bin`'s first save: the day of the earliest dated play folded, but not after today when today is known; else today; else the root's commitTime's day (`lib/core/CardManifest.h:68`, Unix seconds UTC: `docs/METADATA.md:843`), flagged; else unset until a save that knows a date |
| Counting days | today − sinceDay + 1, at least 1 |
| Windows | 7 days: today and the 6 days before. 30 days: today and the 29 before. All time |
| Times | `format.hours_minutes`, unpadded minutes: "7 h 37 min". Under an hour, `format.minutes`: "45 min" |

### 4.2 What each number is

| Where | Number | From |
|---|---|---|
| Entry line, hub row 1, Week's readouts | Time listened and plays in the 7-day window | HOUR, summed over the window's UTC days |
| Entry and hub bars (24 px), Week bars (44 px) | Each day's time listened; the top day is full height | HOUR by UTC day. No bar for a day before sinceDay, or with nothing |
| Hub header sub | Lifetime time listened, since sinceDay | HEAD |
| Hub, Most played's line | The All time top three's titles, cut with "…" like any names | The All time list |
| Hub, New finds' line | Tracks whose earliest dated play is in the 30-day window | TRKS |
| Hub, Old favourites' line | Tracks with 5 plays or more and none in the 30-day window | TRKS |
| Hub, Not played yet's line | Albums with no track played, since sinceDay | The album bits (6.1) |
| Hub, Recently played's line | The newest track's title · how long ago | The Recently played list |
| A shelf row | Its count in the shelf's window | RCNT (7 and 30 days) or TRKS (All time) |
| Week line 1 | The top day of the 7 by time listened; a tie goes to the later day | HOUR |
| Week line 2 | Auto DJ's plays / (its plays + skips) over the 7 days, rounded to a percent; shown only when the sum is 5 or more | DAYS |
| NP sheet detail | The track's plays, its last day, its skips (omitted at 0) | Its TRKS row (base ⊕ delta) |

*As decided (2026-11-20, later; section 1's calls 1-3; 9.7 has the snapshot):*
- **The entry line** is HEAD's lifetime time listened since sinceDay, as the hub's header sub: `Fold::head().listenedMs` and `Fold::sinceDayNow()` (HEAD's sinceDay, else the day the first save will set). It is RAM, at once. Its bars are a fixed icon, not data, and HEAD's plays are read for the Empty state, not drawn (section 1, call 2).
- **Hub row 1, Week's readouts and Week's bars** come from DAYS ⊕ the records' DAYS rows over the window's UTC days, read by the visit's ask into the snapshot's 7 days (each day's listenedMs, plays, and Auto DJ's plays and skips). HOUR is not read: by UTC day DAYS gives the same sums. S6's local days still need HOUR. Week line 1 takes the same 7 days.
- **Hub rows 3-4** (New finds, Old favourites) are the ranker's count of every row offered to that list (`Ranker::total()`), less the orphans the copy-out dropped among the kept rows. This is exact while a list's offers are at most its cap and 16. Past that it can over-count by orphans beyond the kept rows: still "TRKS rows" by this table's own rule.
- **Hub row 5** counts the album bits' clear bits from the u40 played set (4.3). It is given only when the visit's ask took the Full grade or a lean Not played yet ask has landed; otherwise it reads `stats.since_only`.
- **Hub row 6** is the snapshot's Recently played row 0. Its "25 min ago" comes from that row's time and now, at each minute.
- **Shelf rows** are as above, as of the ask.

### 4.3 The shelves

| Shelf | Rule | Order, ties | Cap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Most played | Plays in the window, at least 1 | Plays, most first; then the latest play (RCNT's day, or lastAt), newest first; then hash | 50 |
| New finds | firstAt dated and inside the 30-day window | Plays; then firstAt, newest first; then hash | 50 |
| Old favourites | Plays at least 5; lastAt dated and before the 30-day window | Plays; then lastAt, newest first; then hash | 200 |
| Not played yet | Albums of `albumsAZ()` with no track played | A-Z (`lib/core/LibraryIndex.h:399-400`) | All |
| Recently played | Plays at least 1 | lastAt, newest first; then rows with no dated play, by lastOrd, newest first; then hash | 200 |

- "Then hash": path hashes as unsigned 64-bit integers, the smaller first.
- A hash that names no track of this library (an orphan: 6.6) is left out of a shelf. The rankers keep 16 rows past each cap, so a few orphans don't shorten it.
- Recently played's headings are the UTC day of lastAt: "Today", "Yesterday", then "Sun 8 Nov" (`format.weekday_date`, 8.2). With no clock: "This session" (tracks played since the boot), then "Earlier".
- A track only skipped has no row on any shelf. Its album still counts as not played.
- *As decided (2026-11-20, later; call 3, "5-byte fingerprints"):* Not played yet's played set holds the top 40 bits of each played hash (u40, 5 B each, ascending as the u64s are). A track counts as played when its top 40 bits match one of the set's. The error goes one way only:
  - An unplayed album is hidden when one of its tracks' top 40 bits equal a played hash's. The expected count is at most (N − P) × P / 2^40 a walk: 8.6e-5 at P = N/2 for N = 19,417, and 5.9e-6 on the spare (P = 19,075). It is deterministic until the played set changes.
  - A played album is never shown.
  - Nothing confirms a match by the whole hash: that would take a card search for each matched album (about 25 s at P ≈ N).

### 4.4 States

| State | Trigger | Entry row | Hub | Shelves, Week |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Off | The log off (no card records, no PSRAM, NVS refusing: `src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:99-123`); `stats.bin` or its base unread after the retries (5.4); a newer major; no memory for the fold | Absent | Not reachable | — |
| No card | `cardAway()` or the card held (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:91-93`, `:185`) | Absent from the next `enter()` | The one line "No card: plays can't be counted" | The same line |
| Folding | Stats read, the boot's fold under way | "Counting plays…", no bars | No sub; every line "Counting plays…" | "Counting plays…" |
| Naming | The fold done; the lists or the walk not yet | Line and bars | Header, Last 7 days; the other lines "Counting plays…" | Week works; the shelves "Counting plays…" |
| Ready | | Line and bars | Everything | Everything |
| Empty | No counted play (HEAD's plays 0, none this session) | "Plays show up as you listen", no bars | The empty state (7.5) | — |
| No dates | The board's clock unset | "6 h 10 min since 5 Nov", no bars | Row 1 a status row (7.4); New finds and Old favourites disabled; Not played yet disabled with `stats.needs_dates` (counting days unknown; assumed with O5); Most played without its tray | Week not reachable; Recently played by session |
| First month | Counting days under 30 | As Ready | New finds, Old favourites and Not played yet (O5) disabled: "Opens 6 Dec, after a month of plays" | The tray by age (7.6) |
| Updating | Behind the update's fence (`Library::index()` null) | Absent: `topBar()` is false with no index (`src/ui/LibraryPage.cpp:585`) | Not reachable | Not reachable. The fence returns the Library tab to its root (`src/ui/Ui.cpp:748-755`) and Live does again (`:737`); the snapshots are dropped there. Now Playing's jump refuses with no index (`src/ui/NowPlayingPage.cpp:1140-1143`) |
| Synthetic library | `uil<n>` (`src/ui/LibraryPage.cpp:74`) | Absent | — | — |

- Whether the entry row is there is decided at `enter()`: adding or removing item 0 while Artists shows would move every artist by 42 px.
- A remount doesn't reset the Library tab (`src/main.cpp:3237-3284`); only a rebuild (`:1071`) and an update's Live (`:3467`) do, through `Ui::libraryChanged()` (`src/ui/Ui.cpp:731-746`). An open hub, shelf or Week follows the card state itself (the No card line), at its next `update()`.
- The status band (a scan, tags, an update) shows on the hub and the shelves as on any Library list. It never shows on Week, a page of its own.

*As decided (2026-11-20, later; section 1's calls; 9.7's "The asks" and "The snapshot's life"):*
- **Naming is gone.** Its place is the visit's ask, whose phase every stats page shows (the mapping is `statsvisit::phaseOf()`, pure and host-tested: 9.7):
  - **Working** ("Counting plays…"): the ask is carved, ranking or walking, or held by a wait for under 3 s.
  - **Waiting** ("Waiting for the card…", `stats.waiting`): held past 3 s by S2's gates (Auto DJ's join, the card worker's walk or compaction, a save's held tmp, an update asked), by the card worker's tag scan while its memory is carved (*as decided, 2026-11-21*, "Only while held": the visit's asks only, 9.7's `kWhyRest`; not while the battery floor pauses the scan, *as decided, 2026-11-22*, "Don't wait at the floor") or by the Rank lane's own waits (input in the last 0.5 s, the ring low, an underrun in the last 30 s, a decode pass over 40 ms in the last 5 s, a track change, a seek, Bluetooth, a lit list moving: `lib/core/ScanScheduler.h:72-92`). No block is taken while any of them holds. A wait that comes after the take gives the block back in that pass, and the ask waits again, uncounted. It goes on by itself once the wait clears: it is the same ask, made by the listener's navigation.
  - **No room** ("Not enough memory right now", `stats.no_room`, with Try again): every grade refused at the take. Also Not played yet's own state after a visit that took Lists because Full was refused (its Try again makes the lean ask). *As decided (2026-11-21, "Borrow Dance's block"):* also a visit whose snapshot gets no block: the Dance tab up (only a console ask can meet it, since a stats page and the Dance tab never show at once), or a library past 18,432 albums.
  - **Unread** ("Couldn't read the stats", `stats.unread`, with Try again): only Io, Changed, Damaged or Base from the rank.
  - **Capped** (only with the lit cap on, 9.7): a visit's holds reached 3 s in all; "Waiting for the card…" with Try again, and no ask by itself.
- **Ready is per visit.** The entry row's line needs no ask.
- **The entry row's icon** (the fixed bars) shows in every state the row shows, Folding, Empty and No dates among them: the table's "no bars" meant no week's data. Folding's "Counting plays…" turns into the lifetime line at the boot fold's end, redrawn in place if Artists shows (7.10).
- **Empty** (HEAD's plays 0, none this session) asks nothing.
- **First month:** Not played yet is disabled, so the visit asks for the Lists grade (no played set).
- **No dates:** the entry line "6 h 10 min since 5 Nov"; Not played yet follows O5 (3.1), pending until the ask reports DAYS' span.
- **No card** (the card away or held) cancels the ask and frees the snapshot (`release()`), and the page is attached again at 0 rows with the No card line. An ask asked while the card is away is dropped at once, the same way. When the card comes back, the page offers Try again; nothing asks by itself (A).
- **Updating:** the fence frees the snapshot (`StatsLists::drop()`, its bytes in the fence's line and `alsoFreed`: `src/main.cpp:3635`), and the tab goes back to Artists as before. *Since V8 ("Borrow Dance's block"):* it releases it, with no heap bytes (`StatsLists::snapshotHeapBytes()`: 0), so none in the fence's line or `alsoFreed` (`src/main.cpp:3654-3656` at V10).
- **A remount** (which doesn't reset the tab: the second bullet above) frees a snapshot whose key (`buildStamp()`, `trackCount()`) no longer matches, at the page's next `update()`, and attaches the page again at 0 rows with Try again.
- **The dark** frees it and attaches the page again at 0 rows (an open bar folds into its ref, a sheet closes: 7.2). A wake on a stats page asks again.
- **The Dance tab** (*as decided, 2026-11-21*, "Borrow Dance's block"): opening it drops the lists. The snapshot is the Dance tab's block, lent, so the tab's coming up releases it (`DanceMode::setActive(true)` calls `dancelend::reclaim()`), whoever holds it. A stats page shown again asks again and recounts, as after any other tab.
