A console visit's ask (`Hv`, `Hv1`, `Hu`) made while the tab is up is refused (No room), and one in flight when it comes up ends without writing (Cancelled); `Hn` needs no snapshot and runs.
- **Waiting for the card during the scan's memory** (*as decided, 2026-11-21*, "Only while held"): a visit's ask, and Not played yet's lean ask, wait while the card worker's tag scan holds its memory (its work memory, and any View in it: 90,636-124,052 B of PSRAM no other gate counts). Working, then Waiting ("Waiting for the card…") from 3 s, holding nothing; the ask goes on by itself once the memory is given back. On the spare that is about 15-20 s after the boot's walk ended; on a fresh card or after a Rescan, the whole tag scan (minutes). A scan the battery floor held before its first step holds nothing, so it doesn't hold the pages. While the floor pauses the scan (below 10 % off USB, until USB or a reading above 15 %), memory carved mid-rest stays held with no step to give it back, and it doesn't hold the pages either (*as decided, 2026-11-22*, "Don't wait at the floor": section 1's case (a)): the visit goes ahead, and its take still checks there's room for a decoder, so playback stays safe.

*As built (S3a, 2026-11-21; 9.7's "As built (S3a)"):* the phases are `statsvisit::phaseOf()`'s as above, with these departures. A wait met after the take reads Working, then Waiting from 3 s, as before the take. The 30 s after the card worker took no step of the ask in 30 s read the same way: Working, then Waiting from 3 s, counted from the give-up (held in Asked, holding nothing, then taken again). The remount's key check is `StatsLists::loop()`'s, each pass, not a page's `update()`, and it gives No card, as the card does, until the next ask. A landing reads Ready only while its snapshot is served; once it is freed, Idle (No card where the card or a remount's key freed it).

*As built (V8, 2026-11-21; 9.7's "S3a: the owner's calls as built"):* the Dance tab and the tag scan's wait as decided above. The wait's line names "the card worker's tag scan (its memory held)". A refused loan says its cause on its `[listen]` line (the Dance tab up, past 18,432 albums, or no block made at the boot) and reads No room. Memory the scan holds that no step will give back (a chunk the card refused after a failed compaction) doesn't hold the pages (section 1's case (b)). A landing released since reads Idle, as one freed did.

### 4.5 Toasts

| Toast | Kind | When |
|---|---|---|
| "Needs dates: Plug in to the MP3 Player tab" | `Ui::warn` (amber, 1.8 s) | A tap on New finds or Old favourites, disabled for want of dates |
| "Needs a month of plays: Opens on 6 Dec" | `Ui::warn` | A tap on a row disabled in the first month |
| The existing `act()` toasts, with Undo and View | toast | Play next, + Queue on a shelf |
| `toast.shuffling` | toast | Shuffle all, the empty state's |
| `toast.no_memory` | toast | A shelf's snapshot can't be carved. *Replaced (2026-11-20, later):* no toast; the page's own line, "Not enough memory right now" (`stats.no_room`) with Try again (`empty.try_again`), and "Couldn't read the stats" (`stats.unread`) the same way (4.4) |

A disabled row's tap also gives the inert double buzz (`Input::missBuzz()`, `src/ui/Input.h:123`).

### 4.6 Persistence

| What | Where | Life |
|---|---|---|
| HEAD, MARK, HOUR, DAYS, RCNT, the TRKS delta | `/.player/stats.bin`, and RAM | Saved at a boot with new plays, before a rotation over plays not yet saved (in the end's flush too, with 1 s of its budget left), and by `Hs`; RAM in between |
| TRKS rows as of the last compaction | `/.player/stats-<gen>.bin` | Until the next compaction |
| The lists, the ids, the album bits | RAM | Remade after each boot and each update's Live |
| A shelf's window | RAM, a shelf at a time | The session. Most played starts on All time |
| NVS | Nothing (S6 adds `clock/tz`) | |

*As decided (2026-11-20, later; calls 1-2):* the first two RAM rows become one:

| What | Where | Life |
|---|---|---|
| The lists' rows (ids and facts), the totals, the 7 days, the O5 span, Not played yet's bits, Most played's window | The snapshot: one plain `psramAlloc` block, at most 4,096 B (3,860 B on the spare; 9.7). *As decided (2026-11-21, "Borrow Dance's block"):* the Dance tab's 4,096 B tracker block, lent, nothing carved | While on the stats pages: from the visit's ask to leaving them, another tab, Now Playing, the dark, the fence, a Live, `browse`, a remount's new index, the card away or the stats off (*since 2026-11-21* the Dance tab coming up too; refused while it is up). Remade at each visit; served to the pages only once the visit's ask has landed |
| Anything else of the stats for the screens | Nothing | No week block, no hub summary, nothing in `ui::Ui`. HEAD, sinceDay and the counting days are S1's, already in RAM |

## 5. The data

### 5.1 The pipeline, and where the stats join it

As built (READ):
1. PlaybackController's word goes to the Tracker, a ring of 32 plays in PSRAM (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:362-427`). `tick()` counts playedMs and the pauses (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:593-618`). Plays under 1 s are dropped; a 33rd waiting play is lost (`:567-591`).
2. `stepPlays` (`src/main.cpp:3499-3518`) calls `PlayHistory::loop` every pass. It waits for the WriteGate (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:767-791`) and asks nothing while the card is held or away (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:181-192`).
3. Before Ready: a piece of the boot's scan, 128 slots (`src/app/PlayHistory.h:91`). After: one `Outbox::writeOne` a pass (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:214-217`): the f_stat, the slot, `Journal::append` (seq to NVS first, encode, write, f_sync: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:522-544`).
4. Written: `done()` counts it, calls `env_.tried`, then `pop()` (`:633-647`).

The stats add, all on the loop:
- **The live fold**, at `Outbox::done()` for a Written play only (5.6).
- **The boot's fold**, inside `Journal::scan()` for each valid slot (5.6).
- **The save**, inside `finishScan()` before the trim, and in `rotate()` before `plays.1.jnl` leaves the log's names (5.5).
- **The ranking and the per-track read**, on the card worker, after Ready (6.8).
- **The walk** that names the hashes, on the loop in slices (6.2).

### 5.2 `/.player/stats.bin`

A card container (`docs/METADATA.md` 2.4; `lib/core/CardContainer.h`):
- magic `MPST`, unused by any other file (READ: no other fourcc in `lib/core` or `src` spells it);
- major 1, minor 0;
- headerBytes 40, no type header;
- generation: the save count, from 1;
- cardId 0. The stats are never keyed to MSMF's cardId: a new set-up of `/.mstream` changes it while `/.player` stays (2.11's rows, `docs/METADATA.md:1789-1790`). The file is the card's because it is on the card.

Its sections, in this order. Every one is REQUIRED (a reader that doesn't know one leaves the file alone: 5.4).

| Type | Kind | Stride | Holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| `HEAD` | blob, 128 B | — | The totals and the file's state |
| `MARK` | array | 24 | The highest seq folded, per (deviceId, epoch) |
| `HOUR` | array | 8 | Each UTC hour with a slot that counts, the newest 840 |
| `DAYS` | array | 16 | Each UTC day with one, the newest 400 |
| `RCNT` | array | 12 | Plays per (UTC day, track), the last 32 days, at most 4,096 rows |
| `TRKD` | array | 24 | The TRKS delta: rows changed since the base |

All little-endian.

**Retention**, at each save:
- HOUR and DAYS are capped by rows, the oldest going first: HOUR at most 840 rows (35 days of every hour), DAYS at most 400. Neither is ever pruned by date, so no clock (unset, wrong or foreign) can prune a row of them. DAYS is primary data the log can't rebuild.
- RCNT keeps 32 days, at most 4,096 rows. Its days count back from the earlier of today and the newest dated day folded. With the clock unset or not believed (5.9), nothing is pruned by date at that save. A row newer than the reference is kept.
- So one foreign slot dated 2099 (believable: 4.1) moves nothing: sinceDay is never after today (4.1). A clock set a year ahead for one save, then corrected, costs RCNT's windows up to a month, never a lifetime figure, an hour or a day.
- Two cases still set a future sinceDay, for good: a board clock already fast at the first save, and, with the clock unset at that save, a foreign slot dated ahead (nothing then to cap it by). Then the first month's states last until the clock catches up.

**HEAD** (128 B):

| Off | Size | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | plays |
| 8 | 8 | skips |
| 16 | 8 | listenedMs |
| 24 | 8 | slots: slots that count, folded |
| 32 | 48 | per source, "you", shuffle, Auto DJ: plays u32, skips u32, listenedMs u64 (16 B each) |
| 80 | 4 | undated: plays that weren't dated |
| 84 | 2 | sinceDay (0xFFFF: unset) |
| 86 | 2 | flags: bit 0 sinceDay came from the sync's commitTime; bit 1 a base was lost (5.4); the rest 0 |
| 88 | 4 | round: the last fold round given out (5.6) |
| 92 | 4 | baseGen: the base file's number (0: none) |
| 96 | 4 | baseRows |
| 100 | 4 | baseCrc: the base file's header CRC |
| 104 | 4 | twice: slots the boot's folds met a second time (a diagnostic) |
| 108 | 4 | gap: seqs ACKS covers above MARK that the log no longer holds, added up at each boot that finds some (plays a firmware without stats trimmed; an estimate). That boot's MARK then covers ACKS's seq, so the next boot doesn't count them again (5.6) |
| 112 | 16 | reserved, 0 |

**MARK** row: deviceId 6 B at 0; 2 B reserved; epoch u64 at 8; seq u32 at 16 (the highest folded, or counted in HEAD's gap); 4 B reserved. Strictly increasing by (deviceId's bytes, epoch). MARK rows are never dropped: one row (24 B) for each (deviceId, epoch) ever folded on this card. ACKS's leave-out rule (`docs/METADATA.md:1071-1073`) does not apply to MARK: the scan can miss slots that are on the card (an open the card fails reads as a missing file, a failed read ends a file: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:256-261, 302-306`), and a row dropped then would let the next boot fold those slots again. Never capped at ACKS's 16 rows.

**HOUR** row: hour u24 at 0 (UTC hours since 2026-01-01; 16.7 M hours); plays u8 at 3 (saturating); listenedMs u32 at 4 (a slot's whole time goes to the hour it started in). Strictly increasing by hour.

**DAYS** row: day u16 at 0; plays u16 at 2; skips u16 at 4; Auto DJ's plays u8 at 6 and skips u8 at 7; listenedMs u32 at 8; shuffle's plays u8 at 12 and skips u8 at 13; 2 B reserved. "You" is the rest. u8 counts saturate. Strictly increasing by day. This is the spec's DSRC with the day's totals added.

**RCNT** row: day u16 at 0; plays u16 at 2; path hash u64 at 4. Only plays. Strictly increasing by (day, hash). Past 4,096 rows whole days go, oldest first, until 4,096 or fewer rows are left (a day of more than 4,096 tracks goes whole too), and the 30-day window then holds fewer days (its tray cell says how many: 7.6).

**TRKS row** (TRKD's, and the base's TRKS):

| Off | Size | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | pathHash (`docs/METADATA.md` 2.3.3; the slot's) |
| 8 | 2 | plays, saturating |
| 10 | 2 | skips, saturating |
| 12 | 4 | firstAt: the earliest dated counted play's time (4.1); 0 counted plays, none dated; 0xFFFFFFFF none (skips only) |
| 16 | 4 | lastAt: the latest dated counted play's time; 0 none |
| 20 | 3 | lastOrd: the fold round of the latest counted play |
| 23 | 1 | flags: bits 0-1 the source of the play firstAt keeps (0 you, 1 shuffle, 2 Auto DJ); bits 2-7 0 |

Strictly increasing by hash. A row exists for a track with a play or a skip.

**Merging two rows of one hash**, `a ⊕ b` with `a` the older (the base before the delta; a row before a slot). Every rule is order-free, so a row saved from live folds (a rotation's save, `Hs`) and the same row folded at a boot (newest file first) are the same but for lastOrd, a round (5.6: a live fold's is HEAD.round + 2, a boot's + 1), which the proof compares by order:
- plays, skips: sums, saturating;
- firstAt: the earlier dated time. 0 (undated) only when neither side has a dated one. 0xFFFFFFFF (none) loses to both. The source flag goes with the time kept; on equal times, two undated included, the smaller source code wins;
- lastAt, lastOrd: the larger.

A slot is folded into its row as the row of that one slot:
- a play: plays 1, firstAt and lastAt its time (firstAt 0 when undated, lastAt 0), lastOrd its round, its source;
- a skip: skips 1, firstAt 0xFFFFFFFF, lastAt 0, lastOrd 0.

### 5.3 The base file, `/.player/stats-<gen>.bin`

- `<gen>` is HEAD's baseGen in 8 lowercase hex digits.
- The same frame (magic `MPST`, major 1), generation baseGen, one REQUIRED section `TRKS` (array, stride 24), rows strictly increasing by hash.
- It is written under a name `stats.bin` doesn't name; a stale file there (left by a `stats.bin` deleted or corrupt, or by a base lost: 5.4, 5.7, which start baseGen again at 0) is replaced, as the reference does (created with `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS`). `stats.bin` names it, so the rename of `stats.bin` is the one commit point.
- So a compaction never writes the name the card's `stats.bin` names. After a base lost, baseGen starts again at 0, and its next, 1, may be the lost one's name: a cut after the create would leave a base shorter than its header under the name `stats.bin` names, Unread at every boot (5.4), the stats off for good. After a held tmp that compacted is promoted (5.5), the name is the held base's, and a failed save would lose it. That compaction waits for the next boot's save, whose `stats.bin` names another base or none. (As built, S1 C7: `tools/statsref.py` first compacted over a lost base 1's name; it now skips as the firmware does, and the `base-other` fixture is two boots, the second compacting over that file.)
- **Compaction** writes `base ⊕ delta`, a merge of two sorted streams, to `stats-<gen+1>.bin`. Then `stats.bin` names it with an empty TRKD. Then the old base is removed.
- When: at a boot's save, while nothing plays, when the delta is over 32 KB or over a quarter of the base (with no base, any delta: the first boot's save writes one). Forced past 96 KB, but only at a safe point: nothing playing, or the heard track with 20 s or more left and no seek in the last 2 s (the update step's own safe point, `src/main.cpp:3559-3563`). Otherwise at the next boot's save. A play waiting for the headphones, or a restart asked, counts as playing here, as the safe point counts it (`LibraryUpdate::safePoint()`: its start, or the restart, mustn't wait behind a compaction; as built, the S1 review, where the glue had passed PlayState::Playing alone). The boot's save runs inside `finishScan()`, one synchronous call after a WriteGate checked once at the pass's start (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:767-791`: TrackEnd only under 5 s left), so the gate alone doesn't keep a compaction of 0.8-1.6 s out of the gapless window.
- A cut anywhere leaves `stats.bin` naming a whole base. A file it doesn't name is `stats-<gen−1>` or `stats-<gen+1>`, and goes at the next boot after a Loaded read (5.4). Nothing lists the folder.

### 5.4 Reading at the boot

In `PlayHistory::begin()`, before `journal_->begin()` (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:119-120`):

1. `tagstore::settle(fs, kStatsNames, &check)`, names `{"/.player/stats.bin", "/.player/stats.tmp", "/.player/stats"}` (twins `stats.xl1`-`.xl9`). `check` is `StatsCheck`, a real TmpCheck (`lib/core/TagStore.h:314-323`): the frame and every section's CRC. A null check takes a lone tmp for torn and removes it (`lib/core/TagStore.cpp:454, 463`): after a cut between the remove of `stats.bin` and the rename, that tmp is the only copy. `StatsCheck` answers:
   - **Whole** for a frame whose checks hold, and also for one whose open says Major or UnknownRequired: that tmp is promoted, then read below as Newer and left alone (5.12). `IdxCheck` counts an Outdated `library.idx` as whole the same way (`lib/core/LibraryUpdate.cpp:39-61`). Answered Torn, a lone tmp of a newer stats firmware (a downgrade, or an older second player: O2) would be its whole history deleted.
   - **Unknown** for a read the card fails, records that are damaged (`File::damaged()`: never readable, but the tmp may be the only copy), or no memory for the check: the tmp is kept.
   - **Torn** only for a frame that fails its own checks.

   Settle `Unread` or `Kept`: the verdict is Unread.
2. Open `stats.bin` with `openRead(path, &cardFailed)` (`lib/core/TagStore.h:206-215`). A null with the card failed: Unread. A null otherwise is Absent only when `look()` says Gone, the card read and no `stats.bin` there (`:233-237`). Unknown (the file system's records of the way to it damaged) is Unread.
3. `Container::open`, each section's CRC checked as it is loaded (`Stream::crc()`, `lib/core/CardContainer.h:237`: one pass, not `checkCrc` and then a load). As built (the rework, 5.14's R4): the one pass loads HEAD and MARK into the fold and only checks HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD as they stream (nothing else of the file is kept in RAM), and every section's CRC is judged before any order (SectionCrc, then Shape for a HEAD under 128 B, then the rows' order), as `tools/statsref.py` reads. MARK's first 16 rows are in the glue's object; for a card whose MARK has more than 8 rows the boot's read carves a block of its rows and 8 more (the store's hook: PSRAM), kept for the session, and a carve that fails is a read with no memory (stats Off). Its `Why` (`lib/core/CardContainer.h:91-136`) decides. `open()` returns Short (`lib/core/CardContainer.cpp:89, 105`), Io, Magic, Major, HeaderBytes (`:102`), SectionCount (`:103`), FileBytes, HeaderCrc, Layout, UnknownRequired, Shape and Missing, a REQUIRED section that isn't there (`:165`). The load adds SectionCrc, Counts and the rows' order. Why's Missing is not the verdict Absent below:

| Why | Verdict | What follows |
|---|---|---|
| Ok, and the rows in order | Loaded | The fold goes on above its MARK |
| Io; FatRecords (`File::damaged()`, `lib/core/TagStore.h:143`) | Unread | At the boot, Io is read again after 100, 300 and 1,000 ms (`tagstore::kBootWaitsMs`, `lib/core/TagStore.h:349`); FatRecords once, as cardroot's rule has it: no read again changes it (`lib/core/CardRoot.cpp:31-36`). Still unread: stats Off this session; no fold, no save, **no trim** |
| Major, UnknownRequired | Newer | Stats Off this session; the file left as it is; no trim |
| Magic, Short, HeaderBytes, SectionCount, FileBytes, HeaderCrc, Layout, Missing (a required section absent), Shape, SectionCrc, Counts, any other Why, or a row out of order | Corrupt | Read once more after 100 ms. Still corrupt: renamed `stats.bad` (an older one removed first), then Absent. `stats.bad` never shares a chain (only the twins are names 2.12.6's rule knows, and it would one day free one that `stats.bad` holds): with another entry on `stats.bin`'s chain, `stats.bin` goes to a twin instead, and an older `stats.bad` on it too. A rename or remove the card fails: Unread |
| — (step 2: the card says there is no `stats.bin`) | Absent | A fresh start: MARK empty, so every slot on the card is folded |

4. Loaded: the base it names is opened with `openRead(path, &cardFailed)` and read whole, as the reference's `load()` does: its header, then its rows' CRC, order and count against baseRows, in one pass, no row kept (24 B a played track: 53 KB at 2,214, 467 KB at 19,478; as built, S1 C7, where this step had its header alone read). A card failure (cardFailed, Io), or rows whose CRC fails, is read again at `kBootWaitsMs`; damaged records once. If one persists, the verdict is Unread: stats Off this session, with no fold, no save and no trim. A base is lost (flags bit 1, baseGen 0) only when the card answers that it isn't there (`look()` Gone), or when its header reads whole with a CRC other than baseCrc. A base shorter than its header doesn't read whole: Unread, as one of any other length that fails its frame. Then the rows before it are lost and the totals stay, and on that boot no `stats-<n>.bin` is removed. A base whose header is the one named but whose rows fail their CRC or their order is a card fault too: Unread, never a lost base. `stats-<gen±1>.bin` are removed only after the named base checked whole.
5. The root's commitTime is kept for sinceDay (`lib/core/CardRoot.cpp:111-114` gains it in `cardroot::Root`).

A `stats.bin` (or a base) the card failed to read is never an absent one. Saving a log-only aggregate over it would erase the history the trims already removed (the queue's precedent: `src/app/QueueStore.cpp:181-186`). With MARK unknown, nothing is safe to trim.

**At a remount** (`remounted()`, 5.7): one read, no waits. The boot's waits are for the boot, where "nothing else is held up" (`lib/core/TagStore.h:345-349`); a remount runs on the loop (`src/main.cpp:3242`), perhaps minutes into the first track. The remount drops the folds at once (the records and any overflow); the read itself (its work memory, `stats.bin` and its base read whole) runs at the scan's first piece (`Glue::reserve()`), in a pass the write gate lets through, where Auto DJ's join isn't Working and no library update is asked, not in the card guard's step, which runs every pass whatever the join or the gate (`src/main.cpp:4043`; as built, the S1 review). It carves nothing to keep: a MARK with more rows than the object and the boot's block hold (another player wrote the card while it was out) is a read with no memory, the stats Off for the session (no fold, no save, no trim), never a MARK cut short, which the next save would write without those players' rows (5.14). Unread there: no fold, no save and no trim, and the read is tried again at the next remount. A read that leaves the stats off (Unread, Newer, no memory), here or at the boot, holds nothing but the object (and MARK's block, if the boot carved one). A `stats.bin` that reads corrupt there is not renamed on one read: Unread, left for the next boot's two. While the orderly end flushes (a remount inside `PlayHistory::flush()`, `src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:288-290`, within its 3 s, `src/app/PlayHistory.h:101`), or when the flush starts with a remount's read not made yet: no read at all; the folds are dropped and no save follows.

**No read after a library update** (the rework, 5.14): nothing of the stats is given back during one, so there is nothing to read again. (S1 as first built gave its resident blocks back during each update and read `stats.bin` again after it, a read the card failed made again the boot's waits apart: 6.7's history.)

### 5.5 Writing: the save, and its order against the trim

**When.**
- At the boot's scan's end, in `finishScan()` (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:318-347`): after the boot rules and before the trim loop (`:332`). Also on the path where `storeBoots` fails (`:323-326`): the fold is whole there, and a save is still right. No slot exists yet (append is Off before Ready, `:523`), so nothing can change under the save. As built (the rework, 5.14): when the fold took more than 40 records (96 until 2026-11-17: the overflow) and this save doesn't land (a step the card failed, no work memory, the card away), it is tried again at later passes the write gate lets through, `tagstore::kBootWaitsMs` (100, 300 and 1,000 ms) apart, four tries in all, as the boot's (a compaction allowed), the overflow held meanwhile; a slot written meanwhile goes into its last chunk's room, and a rotation's save in that window merges the overflow too. After the fourth try, a refusal, or the orderly end's flush, the session is behind (below) and the chunks go.
- In `rotate()` (`:411-463`), after `unread()` (`:423-433`) and before the `shared()` question (`:434`), so it covers the twin rename (`:439-441`) as well as the delete (`:437`). It saves when `unread()`'s pass over `plays.1.jnl` (`:349-375`) met a play slot the card's MARK doesn't cover, or, in a session whose scan was whole, couldn't read the file whole. The rotation goes on whatever the save does: plays are never held back for the stats.
  - With the boot's save done, a rotation saves nothing: the file it deletes is the one that save covered, and `plays.jnl`, holding this session's plays, is renamed, not deleted (`:437, 451`). The rotation's save runs at a second rotation in one session (6,553 more plays), or after a boot whose save failed or was skipped.
  - In the orderly end's flush too: `Outbox::flush()` can rotate (`writeOne()` at `:750`, `append()` at `:701`, `place()` at `:516`, `rotate()` at `:505`), and a power-off or restart always follows the flush (`src/main.cpp:278-281`). There the rotation's save runs when at least 1 s of `kFlushBudgetMs` (`src/app/PlayHistory.h:101`) is left (a rotation's save never compacts). Otherwise the plays in the deleted file are lost to the stats.
- `Hs`, the console's (the rotation's path), at a pass the write gate lets through once no play waits for its slot that the card may take: those are written first, one a pass, each folded live, so its save holds every play the gate held back (`statsglue::turnOf()`; S1's device round 1, run H: at the pass the gate opened, Hs had saved "nothing new" with 4 plays waiting for that gate). A play whose try the card failed, its 5 s not over, isn't waited for.
- **The records' save** (the rework, 5.14: `Kind::Records`), due once the records number 24 (`statsfold::kSaveAt`; 64 until 2026-11-17, 5.14's "Moved to internal RAM"), whether the boot's fold or a live slot brought them there, at the first pass the play log's write gate lets through while it is due, before that pass's slot (`statsglue::turnOf()`: a save due, then a slot, then `Hs`), so it holds exactly the records taken by then. It waits while the card is away and while a library update's fence is up, until the records reach 40 (`statsfold::kRecords`; 96 until then), when it runs whatever the fence (the Tracker's backlog and the plays that end during the fence's 13-30 s can reach it). It is refused while the orderly end flushes (its 3 s are the plays'; at most 39 records then wait for the next boot's fold). One that doesn't land is due again only once the records pass the count it was tried at (`recordsTried_`); any save that lands or is held resets that. Never a compaction.
- **Not at a library update** (the rework): nothing of the stats is given back, so nothing is saved first. (S1 as first built saved first at its give-back, at the update's memory check or its fence, waiting for the write gate and for the plays queued for their slots: 6.7's history.)

It is skipped, returning false:
- the boot's save, while the orderly end flushes (an "ending" flag set as `flush()` starts, before its remount at `src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:288-290`, where 5.4's no-read holds, and so before `outbox_->flush()` at `:307`; as built, S1 C9, where this said "before `:307`" alone): the 3 s budget is the plays' (`src/app/PlayHistory.h:101`), and `Outbox::flush` can reach `finishScan()` (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:743`). The ending flag holds back the boot's save and, since the rework, the records' (a rotation's has its 1 s rule); skipping them loses nothing (the next boot folds again);
- when the card is away, or another card is in the slot (`cardAway()`, `sectordisk::guard().foreign`): one card's stats never go onto another;
- when stats are Off, or the fold ran out of memory;
- every save of a session whose scan was partial (5.6);
- for the rest of a session that is **behind** its log (`Refused::Behind`, the rework: RAM lacks slots the log holds above the card's MARK, so a later save would raise MARK past them, unfolded): the 41st live record with the records full after a save that didn't land or was refused (B2 on the device, 2026-11-18: 5.14's "Its device runs"); a boot overflow whose save didn't land in its four tries, or was refused, or met the orderly end; a partial scan's overflow, or a fold out of memory's past 40 records (dropped at the fold's end); a held tmp lost before its promotion, or one no longer ours; a section of `stats.bin` damaged under the session (its CRC failing under the header RAM stands on: `Saved::Damaged`, `H` naming it). The next boot folds what the log still holds; a rotation meanwhile deletes those in `plays.1.jnl`, its own save refused by this rule, so they are lost (below). (S1 as first built also refused every save while its blocks were given back to a library update, and was behind after a give-back that left RAM short: 6.7's history.)
- when the card's `stats.bin` is no longer the copy RAM stands on. The glue keeps what it read at the boot (or a remount's read) or last wrote: no `stats.bin` (Absent, nothing saved yet), or its generation and header CRC. In step 1, after `settle()`, it reads `stats.bin`'s header and compares: a file where none was expected, none where one was, or another header is a change. A change refuses this save and every later one this session, `[listen]` says why, and the next boot reads the card's copy. The card guard's "same card" is its size and two sectors' CRCs (`src/storage/SectorDisk.h:22-35`; `src/app/PlayHistory.h:84-88`): a card that went into another player and came back passes it. Without this check this RAM would be saved over that player's newer `stats.bin` (its plays, and the base it compacted to, lost: the next boot would find its base gone and drop every per-track row), or over a `/.player` deleted on a PC.

With nothing new since the card's copy, it returns true and writes nothing.

**The steps.** The queue's commit (`src/app/QueueStore.cpp:473-500`), not `LibraryUpdate::save`'s (`lib/core/LibraryUpdate.cpp:400-401`), which removes the tmp after a failed `replace()`, possibly the only copy (`replace()` may already have removed the file: `lib/core/TagStore.cpp:493-495`).
1. A tmp held by an earlier save (step 5) is promoted first with `replace()`, as `QueueStore::settleTemp` does (`src/app/QueueStore.cpp:456-467`; `replaceFile` at `:465`): false, the tmp still held. As built (the S1 review), in a call of its own, `Store::promote()`, that the glue makes before it carves the save's work memory: the promotion's frames (`replace()`, a twin's rename) are then never under the save's (the loop's stack, 11). Only while its header is still the one written: another's (a player that had the card meanwhile) is left to `settle()`. The file then written takes the promoted one's generation + 1 (a held tmp promoted is a save that landed), and a remount's read promotes it first too (its `settle()` would drop it while `stats.bin` is there). Then `settle()` with the real check, the header check above, then `prepareTmp()`. A question the card fails, or a `settle()` that fails (a remove, or a rename to a twin): false, nothing done.
2. Compaction, when due (5.3): the new base written, closed, its size checked. A failure leaves the base as it was, and the save goes on. As built (the rework, 5.14's R4): due is decided on the merged TRKD's count, step 3's; the compaction runs between the counting and the writing pass, merging three sorted streams (the base, the card's TRKD and the records, the older side first on equal hashes) into `stats-<gen+1>.bin`; a read the card fails in it, or rows failing their CRC or order, is a compaction failed (the new base removed), and the writing pass reads TRKD again.
3. `stats.tmp` created and written through a `ContainerWriter` in 4 KB aligned pieces (`lib/core/TagStore.h:184-195`), then closed. As built (the rework): nothing of the card's rows is kept in RAM, so the file is a streaming merge of the card's `stats.bin` with the records, sorted in place for each section's key (a heap sort). A counting pass reads HEAD to TRKD, each section's CRC taken as it streams, merging HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD with the records by ⊕ (5.2) and counting the rows each keeps after its cut (HOUR's newest 840, DAYS' newest 400, RCNT's 32 days and then 4,096 rows by whole days, merged a second time when its count passes 4,096, to find the first day kept). The writing pass writes HEAD and MARK from RAM and the four sections merged again, each section's size known from the first pass; one whose merge gives another count is a save failed (step "rows"), the tmp removed. A read the card fails in either pass is a save failed, tried again; a section whose CRC fails under the header RAM stands on is `Saved::Damaged` (behind, above).
4. The tmp's size on the card must equal what was written (`QueueStore::tmpWhole`, `src/app/QueueStore.cpp:372-392`). Not: the tmp removed, false.
5. `replace()`. False: the tmp is kept, whole, and held; the next save promotes it first (step 1). A boot's `settle()` takes a whole tmp when `stats.bin` is gone, and drops one while `stats.bin` is there (`lib/core/TagStore.cpp:454, 463`): a held tmp not promoted before a restart is lost, and with it the plays of a `plays.1.jnl` a rotation deleted after that save (13). As built (the rework, 5.14): a held tmp holds every record, so the records are emptied and any overflow given back, and RAM's HEAD takes the plan's (its baseGen, baseRows, baseCrc and round), but the card's MARK in RAM (what `covered()` answers by) stays the one before until the promotion; the held tmp counts as something new, and the next save of any kind promotes it (`Store::promote()`: Promoted, the card's MARK then the held copy's; Behind, the tmp lost or no longer ours; Changed; Fault) and merges only the records taken since onto it.
6. True: the card's MARK is now the one written, and an old base is removed. As built (the rework): the records are emptied and any overflow given back.

The `ContainerWriter` is about 2.1 KB (`lib/core/CardContainer.h:281-313`: 64 section entries, two u32[64], a 256 B header). The loop's stack is 8 KB, with 2.5-3.8 KB left at its lowest (`docs/METADATA.md:6322`; `docs/AUTODJ.md:1802-1803`). So the writer and the save's buffers are in PSRAM, never on the loop's stack. As built (the rework, 5.14's R4): the work memory (`statsfile::kWorkBytes`: the writer, its plan, two containers, two row readers, the records' cursor and the merge's rows, then a 4 KB buffer and two of 2 KB) is 11,456 B on the device, carved for each read and save and given back in the same call, beside a 4,148 B `FatFile` for each file open (`FatFs::open()`'s `psramNew<FatFile>`): 19,752 B in three blocks for a save, 23,900 B in four for a compaction.

**The trim.**
- A log file goes when today's rule holds (every play acknowledged, at least one, a play last, no twin on its chain: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:292-297, 310-311, 332-344`) **and** every play slot in it is at or under the card's MARK for its (deviceId, epoch).
- After a save that succeeded, that MARK covers everything the scan read.
- After one that failed, or none (a partial scan, a changed `stats.bin`), it is the MARK read at the boot. So a full card still frees the files earlier boots folded.
- With stats on but no MARK known (Unread, Newer), nothing is trimmed. The log stays bounded: the rotation holds it to two files of 6,553 slots (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:61-64`).
- A fold that ran out of memory can't be saved; the trim then follows the MARK read at the boot, as after a failed save.
- With no stats (a `Folds` of null), the trim is today's.
- No ACKS, no trim, as today.

**Why not the worker.**
- No window: `finishScan()` is one synchronous call (1.1).
- The worker is busy with the post-sync rebuild just then (`docs/METADATA.md:1066-1068`).
- Its 7.5 KB stack is internal RAM, made for each burst of work (`src/app/CardWorker.h:38-61`).
- Folds while it wrote would need snapshots.

**Why not pieces across passes:** a state between the scan and Ready drops plays (1.1).

**What a failed save costs.** Nothing is lost: the card's MARK stays, and the next boot folds the same slots again. It can cost loop time: on a card with one free cluster each failed try after the first held the loop 5,295-5,398 ms, the boot's 5,430 ms (B2, 2026-11-18: FatFs's search for `stats.tmp`'s clusters reads the whole FAT; the store checks no free space first; 5.7's "Card full", 13's "A nearly full card"); with #63's hole hint 98-191 ms a try (visit B, 2026-11-19), the first allocation after a computer filled the card still 5,523 ms once a mount. The exceptions have one shape: a rotation deletes (or moves to a twin) a `plays.1.jnl` holding plays the card's MARK doesn't cover, and no save lands before the restart. Then those plays are in no file the scan reads. It happens when:
- the rotation's save failed (a full or failing card), or its tmp was held and not promoted before the restart (step 5);
- the stats are Off this session (Unread, Newer, no memory for the fold, the folds dropped while ending);
- this session's saves are refused (a partial scan, a changed `stats.bin`, behind: above);
- the rotation came in the end's flush with under 1 s of its budget left;
- (the rework, 5.14) a full log whose boot save fails all four of its tries: a full log's `plays.jnl` is at its cap, so the session's first play rotates, its save refused (behind), and `plays.1.jnl`'s uncovered plays are lost, up to 6,553 (statsref's `boot-overflow-full-rotation`: 128 at the fixtures' cap of 128; 0 when the second try lands). It needs a fault that lasts past the boot's waits (about 1.4 s) and clears before that play's end; one that lasts through the rotation loses them as S1 as first built did too. The owner's default: documented, not closed;
- (the rework) a session behind its log otherwise (above: the records full after a save that didn't land, a boot overflow not saved in a log that isn't full, a held tmp lost, a section damaged): a rotation then needs `plays.jnl`'s room in plays (statsref's `records-behind-rotation`: 64 at the cap of 64).

S1 as first built had two more, both its give-back's: a rotation while the blocks were given back, its own save unlanded (`update-rotation-unsaved`), and one after a take-back that left RAM behind (`update-behind-rotation`); both closed with the give-back (6.7's history).

13 lists it.

**What not keeping the data costs** (the rework, 5.14; 13). A section of `stats.bin` damaged after the boot's read can't be healed: every save reads it, so the session's saves stop at its first failure (behind), and the next boot reads the file Corrupt (5.4), renames it `stats.bad` and starts again from what the log still holds (HEAD's totals, HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD lost); S1 as first built wrote RAM's copy over it at the next save. Behind stops the live records and the saves for the session: a card that fills or fails mid-session shows fewer plays until the next boot. Each save reads `stats.bin` twice, and the records' save is a loop pause once each 24 plays (64 until 2026-11-17; 91-225 ms on the device with a 16-60 KB `stats.bin`, 0 underruns; about 5.3 s when it fails again on a card with one free cluster before #63, 98-191 ms with it: 5.7's "Card full"). (S1 as first built's "What a give-back costs the screen" went with the give-back: 6.7's history.)

### 5.6 The fold

**The live fold.**
- At `Outbox::done()`, only when `t.did == Did::Written`: after `append()` or `again()` said Ok, so synced (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:711-714`). It runs before `tracker_.pop()` (`:641-642`), so `p` is still the ring's oldest.
- Through a new `Outbox::Env::written(const Slot&, const Played&)`, beside `tried()` (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:486`). It has an empty default body, so test Envs compile unchanged.
- `done()` also runs for Dropped plays: Gone, Off, Nvs, Blocked, and given up after 3 tries (`:649-658, 676-679, 718-729`). Those are never folded.
- The fold takes `t.slot` exactly as encoded (`:702, 710`): `append()` fills its seq, epoch, deviceId and bootSeq (`:536-541`), and on a retry it is still the first try's slot. It is never dated again from `boardclock::now()` or `p.startMono`, so the next boot's fold of the same bytes gives the same result.
- From S2 (the lists and the map use ids; S1's fold is hash-keyed and asks for none): `p.file.path` names the file, and `LibraryIndex::findTrack()` gives its id (`lib/core/LibraryIndex.h:395-397`) when the index is readable; behind the fence it is not asked.
- RAM only: no card write and no NVS write for a play.
- As built (the rework, 5.14): the play is a record (20 B: its path hash, its time in seconds from 2026, 0 undated, its playedMs, a play, a skip or neither, its source, its round) among the glue's object's 40 (96 until 2026-11-17); while a boot's overflow waits for its save, in its last chunk's room. A live slot never carves: with the records full (a save that didn't land or was refused) the 41st isn't folded, and the session is behind (5.5). (S1 as first built folded each play into RCNT's and TRKD's rows in RAM, and kept a ring of 32 slots for the plays written while its blocks were given back to a library update: 6.7's history.)

**The boot's fold.**
- `Journal::Config` gains a `Folds*` (null: no stats, today's behaviour; 9.5's C4 lists its calls).
- `startScan()` (`:153-165`) calls `scanBegan(again)`. A scan begun again after a remount (`:167-174`) has the glue read `stats.bin` again, dropping the folds made before. Without that, every slot read before the remount would count twice.
- Each slot `decode` says Ok goes to `slot(s, file)`, after the damaged-slot skip (`:285`). Drop notes (kind 2) are skipped by the stats: their playedMs is a dropped count and their seq 0 (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:82`; `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:22-28`).
- A slot that counts is folded when its seq is above the MARK read at the boot for its (deviceId, epoch), from every deviceId (O2).
- **A partial scan.** Today the scan takes an open the card failed for a missing file (`fs_.open()` null, the next name: `:256-261`) and ends a file at a failed read (`:270-275, 302-306`). Raising MARK past slots it never read would lose them for good (once ACKS covers them, the trim deletes the file). So with folds the scan opens each log file with `openRead(name, &cardFailed)`; an open the card failed, or a read that failed, calls `Folds::partial()`. So does an open that finds no file with the card not at fault when `look()` then says Unknown, not Gone: FatFs's FR_INT_ERR, the file system's records on the way to the file damaged, where the file may well be there and a later disk check makes it readable (as built, the S1 review: one `f_stat` for each log name the open found nothing at, with folds only; 5.4 step 2 takes the same answer for `stats.bin` as Unread). A `/.player` whose directory chain is broken then keeps the saves off until a disk check. After a partial scan no save runs this session: the boot's, a rotation's and `Hs` all return false. The trim follows the MARK read at the boot, and keeps the file not read whole (as today's scan keeps a file it couldn't judge); the next boot folds again. A partial scan counts no gap (below): the slots it didn't read may hold those seqs. The same holds within a session: live folds raise MARK in RAM above an unread tail, and that MARK is never written.
- **Each slot once.** In one file, one device and epoch's seqs only rise, so a slot at or under the seq last folded for its row from that file is skipped. Across files, each row keeps the seq range each file read so far gave it, at most 10. A slot inside another file's range is skipped and counted in `twice`. Two files on one chain (a rename cut that `settle()` couldn't part: `:227-243`) give the same slots twice, and this keeps one. A conflict (one key, different bytes) keeps the first read; mStream's reader drops both (2.20.6 item 3), which a log with this seq rule can't produce. The reference (`tools/statsref.py`) keys each slot by (deviceId, epoch, seq): the first read kept, one met again from another file counted in `twice`. The ranges give the same on any log a device writes, and on every fixture: each fixture's log files keep one device and epoch's seqs in order, and `tools/test_statsref.py` checks that, and that the ranges choose the reference's slots, on every boot.
- **The gap.** For each ACKS row above the MARK read at the boot, the seqs it covers that no valid play slot of the log holds (a firmware without stats trimmed them) are added to HEAD's gap, and MARK rises to ACKS's seq: those seqs are gone for good (ACKS covers only seqs the software read from this card, and seqs only rise), and a MARK left under them would count them again at every boot. The rows are the ones the root's read keeps (`cardroot::Root::acks`: at most `msmf::Acks::kMax`, 16, the device's own first), as the journal's trim takes them; a row past them is counted at a boot that keeps it (this player's later, or another player's). `tools/statsref.py` takes every row it is given, and its fixtures never give more than 16 (the S1 review).
- **Order** comes from the slot, never from the scan: firstAt and lastAt are times; a play's round is the boot's round (below).
- **The cost** is CPU and RAM only: no read beyond the scan's own. As built (the rework, 5.14): a slot is appended as a record, nothing is looked up or merged while folding, so no index and no sorted insert. Each save sorts the records in place for each section's key (a heap sort: `std::sort`'s introsort recursed up to about 2 KB on the loop's stack under the save) and merges them, up to 17 sorted runs at a boot (the object's and 16 chunks'), with the card's rows as it streams (5.5). The rank (S2) reads `stats.bin` and takes a copy of the records (5.14, "The screens"). (S1 as first built kept TRKD's and RCNT's rows in RAM, in chunks of 16,392 B, with a transient open-addressing index during the boot's fold, binary searches and a sorted tail for live folds, and HOUR and DAYS carved at their caps: 6.7's history.)
- **Memory** (the rework, 5.14; as built since the move to internal RAM and fix A + G, 2026-11-17). The glue's object is internal DRAM's `.bss` (`s_glue` in app/ListeningStats, made with placement new in setup and never destroyed; not carved): HEAD, MARK's first 16 rows (28 B each: RAM's seq, the card's, and the one a held tmp holds) and the 40 records, 2,008 B on the device under a 2,048 B `static_assert` (until 2026-11-17 a PSRAM block of 3,200 B with 96 records, under 3,584 B). A card with more than 8 MARK rows has a block of its rows and 8 more from the boot's read (5.4), by plain `psramAlloc` (fix G), held for the session. The boot's fold carves, at the scan's first piece, its ranges' block (the per-file seq ranges, 80 B a MARK row, at most 10 files; the ACKS rows above MARK, 112 B each, at most 16, for the gap): 1,284 B for 16 MARK rows and no ACKS row above them, 3,076 B at most (4 B over and aligned to 8: the ACKS rows hold a u64, and PSRAM's heap gives 4-byte-aligned blocks); and, past 40 records, the overflow: one arena of 262,080 B (`statsfold::kArenaBytes`, 262,144 B carved), all 16 chunks' room (16,384 B, 819 records each), carved at the first chunk, its chunks packed in it; with no free block for the arena, chunks of 16,384 B one by one (carving 16,900 B each), the hold's caps given back (a full never-synced log, 13,106 slots: the arena, the heap's peak with the boot save's work memory 273,632 B on the host, MEASURED-HOST, where S1 as first built's fold peaked at 396,744 B; on the device the arena and its caps, 262,144 B and 81,276 B, c1-i1). Both are carved at the top of PSRAM's largest free block through Auto DJ's spacers keeping 64 KB free with the block carved (`kTopMarginBytes` in app/ListeningStats, Auto DJ's `kJoinMarginBytes` figure), the free block left above each closed by caps found by a heap walk (fix A), only in a pass where Auto DJ's join isn't Working and, before the scan's first piece, no library update is asked (otherwise the scan waits that pass). The caps are given back, and the arena, when the largest free block wouldn't then take a decoder's buffers (`statsglue::decoderRoom()`: the ranges' block asks at every take, the arena and its chunks while a track plays). The hold is given back bottom-up (5.14, "The hold's T"). The fold carves a chunk itself at the record past the 40, in the same piece of a pass that may carve (the scan reserves no look-ahead, `statsglue::kReserveAhead`: R6 had found the first piece's reserve of 128 slots carving one at every boot, which the rework review took out), so only a fold past the records holds chunks, a full log's, once a card. A card that goes away mid-scan ends the fold as a partial scan's, its blocks given back (`Glue::scanAway()`). The ranges' block goes at the fold's end (before the boot's save carves its work memory; `ready()` after a partial scan; a remount's `scanBegan(true)`), with the chunks no record needs; the rest when the boot's save lands or is held, or after its fourth try. A partial scan's overflow, or a fold out of memory's past 40 records, is dropped at the fold's end (behind); a fold out of memory within 40 records keeps them, never saved. HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD live in `stats.bin` alone. S2's chunked blocks (hash → id, the rank's snapshot, the played set: 6.5) can't be resident under the budget: they are carved for a rank's or a page's life and given back (13).

**The fold round.**
- The boot's fold gives its plays round `HEAD.round + 1`; live folds this session, `HEAD.round + 2`. The save writes the highest round used.
- Live plays the next boot folds again (not saved by a rotation) get that boot's round. It is higher than the round they had in RAM: the order holds, the number differs.
- So the proof (S1) compares counts byte for byte and rounds by order.
- 24 bits: 16.7 M rounds, saturating.

**The seq rule** (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:327-328`): `seq_ = max(NVS's seq, the card log's, MARK's, ACKS's)` for this device and epoch.
- Today a settings partition written back from a backup (the house's own end-of-check step: `docs/RELEASE-0.14.0-CHECKS.md:116-118`) puts NVS's seq behind.
- On a card whose log was trimmed, new plays then reuse seqs under ACKS: they read as acknowledged and are never sent.
- They are also under MARK, so the stats would skip them.
- 2.20.4 lets a device reserve seqs, so a higher start breaks no reader. Its own commit in S1.
- Two places state today's rule and change with it: METADATA 2.20.4's "At boot" sentence (`docs/METADATA.md:3112-3122`, C2) and the reference that writes the play log's goldens, `tools/playlog.py`'s `boot_seq()` (`:244-248`, used by `Device.boot` at `:531-545`; C5).

**The play log after S1**, against 0.14.0:
- the scan reads on a new epoch with no ACKS;
- the trim also needs the folds;
- the rotation may save first;
- the seq rule as above.

Slots, files, ACKS and the transfer's reading are unchanged. `test_play_log`'s 33 tests (`test/test_play_log/test_play_log.cpp:3064-3096`) pass with no `Folds`, with `atBoot`'s count from 5 to 6 (`:454-455`: the seq rule's new golden case, C5).

### 5.7 Card events

| Event | What happens |
|---|---|
| Remount during the scan | The scan begins again (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:167-174`); the folds are dropped (the records, any overflow and the ranges' block), and `stats.bin` is read again at the scan's first piece (one read, no waits, nothing carved to keep: a MARK past the room the boot carved is the stats off, 5.4), and a `stats.bin` unread at the boot is tried again there |
| Remount after Ready, before this boot's first slot | The scan begins again (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:171-173`; `src/app/PlayHistory.h:84-87`); `stats.bin` is read again (`scanBegan(again)` drops the folds, the read at the scan's first piece, one read, no waits) and the boot's save runs again (nothing new: it writes nothing) |
| Remount after the first slot | The live folds go on; the next save checks the card's copy first (5.5), so a card that was in another player meanwhile is never saved over |
| A remount inside the orderly end's flush (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:288-290`) | No read; the folds are dropped and no save follows (5.4) |
| A log file the boot couldn't read whole (an open or a read the card failed, or an open that found the file system's records on the way to it damaged: the S1 review) | A partial scan: the fold goes on, no save this session, the trim by the MARK read at the boot; the next boot folds again (5.6) |
| A library update | Nothing of the stats (the rework, 5.14: no give-back, no save first, no read after it). The boot's scan doesn't begin while an update is asked (the plays wait in the Tracker); once it has begun, an update asked waits in Asked while the scan holds its ranges' block or overflow chunks (`LibraryUpdate::Wait::Stats`), and Auto DJ's join doesn't begin meanwhile. The records' save waits while the fence is up, until the records reach 40 (5.5; 96 until 2026-11-17; B1's M2 on the device: due at 24 under `gb`'s fence, it ran after the fence came down). (S1 as first built gave its blocks back at the update's memory check or fence and read `stats.bin` again after it: 6.7's history) |
| The boot's save doesn't land with an overflow (past 40 records) | Tried again 100, 300 and 1,000 ms apart, four tries, the overflow held and a track's start, Auto DJ's join and an asked update waiting meanwhile; then behind: no save this session, the next boot folds what the log still holds (5.5) |
| Card pulled | No save, no trim. Plays not written were never folded |
| Another card in the slot | FatFs refuses writes (STA_PROTECT) and the guard restarts, or under the breaker it doesn't (`src/main.cpp:3199-3236`). The save checks `foreign` and `writesOff` and writes nothing. RAM is the first card's and is dropped at the restart |
| Card full | The save fails (5.5); the trim follows the old MARK. The records' save is tried again at each later record; with the records full the 41st puts the session behind (5.5). On the device (B2, 2026-11-18, the spare with one free cluster of 32 KB): the boot's save failed in 5,430 ms, then 17 records' tries from the 24th record to the 40th, none landed, all 39 slots written, behind, `Hs` refused; each failed try after the first held the loop 5,295-5,398 ms (FatFs's search for the tmp's clusters reads the whole FAT), and the store checks no free space first (`cardspace::freeBytes()`, src/storage/CardSpace, is the cheap count). With #63's hole hint (in S1 since 985f4a9; visit B, 2026-11-19, the same card and stops): each failed try 98-191 ms, and the boot's save, the first allocation after the computer's fill, failed in 5,523 ms (#63's known uncovered case); B2's failed saves left a 0 B `stats.tmp`, which the next boot's read removed (b3h-e), and visit B's left none (13's "A nearly full card") |
| Read-only (`writesOff`) | Save and trim fail; nothing lost |
| Reformatted, or `/.player` deleted | `stats.bin` is absent with the log: counting starts again (a player that stayed on through it saves nothing more this session: 5.5) |
| Only `stats.bin` deleted | Counting starts from what the log still holds; older history is gone |
| NVS erased | A new epoch, a new MARK row; the old epoch's slots fold once under their own row |

### 5.8 Every player on the card, each slot once

- The stats count every deviceId (O2). ACKS's 16 rows govern only the trim (`lib/core/CardManifest.h:159-175`).
- A card used by two players: each folds every slot above the card's MARK and saves one `stats.bin`. A player's last session, not yet saved, is folded by whichever player boots with the card next.
- Each slot once:
  - one atomic file holds the aggregate and its MARK;
  - MARK rows are kept for good (5.2);
  - the boot folds only above MARK, each slot once (5.6);
  - a scan begun again drops its folds; a partial scan saves nothing (5.6);
  - a save over a `stats.bin` that isn't the copy RAM stands on is refused (5.5);
  - live folds are RAM only, of Written slots, with fresh seqs (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:531-535`);
  - the trim and the rotation delete only what the card's MARK covers, or what is saved first;
  - a session behind its log (RAM lacking slots the log holds above the card's MARK: the records full after a save that didn't land, a boot overflow not saved, a held tmp lost, a section damaged) saves nothing more, so no MARK passes a slot not folded (5.5); a library update asks nothing of the stats (the rework);
  - seqs only rise (`docs/METADATA.md:3109-3131`; and 5.6's rule).
- Rare cases that undercount by design:
  - a slot given up on (3 card failures) whose bytes had landed, under a MARK raised later that session: never folded;
  - a firmware without stats (0.14.0) trims ACKed files it never folded (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:332-344`). The next stats boot sees ACKS above MARK with those seqs gone, and HEAD's gap says how many.

### 5.9 The clock and the time zone

- **Unset** (startedAt 0, clock 0: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:692-693`): HOUR, DAYS and RCNT need a day, so an undated play counts only in HEAD (in `undated` too) and in its TRKS row (firstAt 0 only while the row has no dated play, lastAt untouched, lastOrd its round: 5.2's merge).
- **Set during a play:** the slot is dated from its start (`lib/core/WallClock.cpp:167-180`); earlier slots stay 0. Dating them back from a later slot of the same boot (2.20.6 item 7) is possible later, only if the live and the boot's folds do it alike.
- **The RTC not believed** at the boot (`wallclock::Boot::Foreign` and the rest: `lib/core/WallClock.h:101-119`): as unset.
- **Corrections:** a `@time` moves the clock either way (`src/app/BoardClock.cpp:363-366`); the RTC drifts about 3.5 s a day (`docs/RELEASE-0.14.0-CHECKS.md:59`). A later today than a play's day is fine; counting days never go under 1.
- **Another device's** startedAt outside 2026-2099 is undated: the u24 hour and u16 day would overflow or go negative.
- **UTC now (O4).** Hours and days are UTC. Every function that turns them into labels or windows (lib/core `StatsView`, `StatsText`) takes `tzOffsetMin`, 0 until S6.
- **The time zone (S6).**
  - `@time` parses only field 0 today (`lib/core/HostLink.cpp:327-339`). Extra fields are ignored (`lib/core/HostLine.h:91-98`), so 0.14.0 takes a terminal that sends more.
  - S6 reads an optional field 1, minutes east of UTC (−720 to +840), into `HostLink::Out` (`lib/core/HostLink.h:109`), keeps it in NVS, and shifts the display.
  - Nothing stored changes: HOUR gives local days for at least the last 35 days, and a change of zone (DST, travel) moves the whole display at once and misfiles nothing.

### 5.10 NVS

- No key for the stats, and no write a play (`src/app/NvsLater.h:19-21`). `plays` is untouched (`lib/core/NvsLayout.h:210-236`).
- S6's `clock/tz` (an i16) is schema 7 (`kCurrent` at `lib/core/NvsLayout.h:77`), with a `migrate(6, 7)` that does nothing (`src/app/NvsSchema.cpp:42-53`): no key reads as UTC. Older firmware never reads it.
- The Bluetooth EQ claims the next schema too (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:1866-1869`); whichever lands second takes the next number (10).

### 5.11 What the terminal may do

- It never writes `/.player` (2.11: read only). The listener may delete it whole (2.11's Listener column, `docs/METADATA.md:1790`), and a format the listener confirms (`@format`) clears it too: the stats start again with the log. For caches "the device rebuilds" holds; for the stats it doesn't, and 2.11's row says so (C2).
- It never reads or writes the stats files. `device.txt` gains no key.
- Its sync's ACKS lets the next boot trim, after the fold and the save.
- Later (not v1): it may write mStream's per-track counts as a gen-named file of its own (spec 8.6). Not played yet would then mean never, and the counts would include other clients.

### 5.12 Compatibility

`stats.bin` is primary data, not a cache. `library.idx` answers a version change with one rebuild (`Load::Outdated`); copying that for the stats would lose, for good, every play the trims already removed. So:
- a reader keeps reading every major it ever read;
- a newer major is left alone (Off);
- a minor adds sections or longer rows only for what a save by an older firmware can drop (re-derived, or counted again from the log). A section a minor adds is written OPTIONAL (flags without `kSectionRequired`, `lib/core/CardContainer.h:42`), so an older reader skips it instead of reading the file as UnknownRequired and turning the stats Off; only major 1's own sections are REQUIRED (5.2). Anything that must not be lost needs a new major.

| Combination | Result |
|---|---|
| 0.14.0 on a card with `stats.bin` | Never reads it. Trims ACKed files without folding them: those plays are missed when the card comes back. HEAD's gap counts them |
| Stats firmware after a 0.14.0 session | Folds what the log still holds above MARK |
| A newer major on the card | Stats Off this session; no trim; the log rotates as ever |
| A newer minor | Read by its known sections and row prefixes (its added sections are OPTIONAL, so they are skipped); a save writes this minor (drops the additions: the rule above) |
| `stats.bin` corrupt | `stats.bad`; counting starts again from the log |
| The base lost or wrong | Per-track rows before it lost (shelves thinner); totals intact; flags bit 1 |
| `/.player` deleted | Starts again |
| Two players alternating, either firmware with stats | Each slot once (5.8) |
| A unit alternating 0.14.0 and stats firmware | Undercounts what 0.14.0 trims; no double count |

### 5.13 A worked example

Device A, epoch E. `stats.bin` says MARK (A, E) = 41. `plays.1.jnl` holds seqs 30-45, `plays.jnl` 46-48. ACKS (A, E) = 45.

| Seq | Slot | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 42 | Track X, durationMs 200,000, playedMs 120,000, completed, Auto DJ, startedAt 2026-11-08T21:40:00Z | Play (line 30,000). Day 311, hour 7,485, time 26,948,401 |
| 43 | Track Y, durationMs 240,000, playedMs 12,000, skipped | Skip (line 30,000) |
| 44 | Track Y, durationMs 40,000, playedMs 25,000, stopped | Play (line 20,000) |
| 45 | durationMs 0, playedMs 8,000, stopped | Neither (line 30,000): 8 s listened |
| 46-48 | Three plays | Plays |
| 30-41 | | Under MARK: not folded |

- Day: January to October 2026 are 304 days, so 8 November is day 311 (2026-01-01 is 0). Hour: 311 x 24 + 21. Time: 311 x 86,400 + 21 x 3,600 + 40 x 60 + 1.
- Y's row: plays 1, skips 1, firstAt and lastAt 44's time.
- MARK becomes 48 and the save writes it.
- The trim: `plays.1.jnl` (30-45) is under the new MARK and under ACKS: it goes. `plays.jnl` (46-48) is under MARK but over ACKS: it stays.
- The next boot finds nothing above 48: no save.

### 5.14 S1 rework: a fixed resident set (2026-11-12)

**Designed and reviewed; R1 built (the yardstick as a test, `test_autodj_join`'s `test_the_listening_stats_across_updates`, failing on purpose against S1 as built until R6, passing since), R2 built (the reference and its 34 sessions; `test_stats_store` fails two cases on purpose until R5), R3 built (`lib/core/StatsFold`, the rework's fold; S1 as built's moved to `StatsFoldS1` until R6), R4 built (`lib/core/StatsFile`, the streaming read and save; S1 as built's store moved to `StatsFileS1` until R6), R5 built (the glue and the firmware: `lib/core/StatsGlue`, `app/ListeningStats`, `PlayHistory`, `main.cpp`, `LibraryUpdate`'s `Wait::Stats`; `test_stats_store` runs every session through the glue), R6 built (the heap model: `test_autodj_join`'s `StatsRun` runs the firmware's glue, and the yardstick passes; S1 as built's fold and store deleted), R7 built (the docs: METADATA 3.11, ARCHITECTURE, the README's `H`, and this plan's 1, 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11, 12 and 13 describing the rework as built); the rework review applied (2026-11-16: its 17 findings, the build plan's "The rework review"); its gate met on the host (2026-11-16, at 39513f1: section 1's "The rework's gate"); the device re-run made (2026-11-17: main's PSRAM figures at every point but the FLAC row), and the resident set moved to internal RAM for it, 40 records (2026-11-17: "Moved to internal RAM", below); that move's re-run made (2026-11-17: "Its device re-run"); B1 found the hold's T on it (2026-11-17), fixed by A + G and the room's trigger, device-checked 2026-11-18 ("The hold's T", "Its device runs").** The owner's decision of 2026-11-12, "Rework S1": the stats keep a small fixed amount in memory, set aside once at the boot and never moved, and merge the rest from the card at each save. With nothing big resident, the give-back around library updates goes. Since R7, 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11 and 13 describe the rework as built (until then they described S1 as built, 414488c), and this section keeps the design, its review and each step's "As built"; where the code and this section disagree, the code wins and the step's "As built" says so. The design's review (2026-11-12, two lenses: the data's integrity and the memory) kept its core and its one resident block: every merge is ⊕, associative and order-free with saturation; HOUR's, DAYS's and RCNT's cuts give the same rows whether the card's copy was cut already or not (rows only grow, and statsref's `retain()` prunes in place); MARK never passes a slot not folded; the trim and the rotation go by the card's MARK. What it found wrong or missing is folded into the rules below, and each finding's outcome is listed at the end ("The review's findings").

**What the device found** (S1's device round 2 and main's control, 2026-11-12; MEASURED on the same board and spare card with one script: album 966, `gb` with 25.8 s of the heard track left, `D` and `Dc` after each update; logs in the session's scratch folder, `dev/round2` and `dev/control`). PSRAM's largest free block:

| Point | main (218822a) | S1 (414488c) |
|---|---|---|
| The boot's join, a build at the boot | 573,428 B | 516,084 B (-57,344) |
| Before any update (the Opus's 128 KB kept since run H) | 442,356 B | 385,012 B (-57,344) |
| After the MP3 update | 294,900 B | 294,900 B |
| After the Opus update (the fence at once, track 07 opening behind it: main's run Lo2) | 294,900 B | 188,404 B (-106,496: under 245,764 B where main is over it) |
| After the FLAC update (the FLAC held through it) | 188,404 B | 120,820 B (-67,584) |

The comparison found the mechanism and reproduced it in the heap model: S1's resident blocks (62,296 B on this card), carved in setup and carved again by good fit into the holes each rejoin leaves (the take-back's re-read), move where the long-lived blocks carved between updates land: the Opus decoder's, kept from its first open, and a FLAC's while it plays. The give-back frees the stats' blocks, not the ones they moved. E's deferred update (15,148 B short) is most likely the same cost from the boot (an inference: main can't repeat it without the card's earlier state).

**The yardstick: the multi-update model** (MEASURED-HOST, 2026-11-12; re-run after the review). The comparison's harness: `test_a_library_update_in_a_model_heap`'s model heap with 5 update steps of the 19,412-track library, the decoders' long-lived carves, a `Dc` (the join again) after each step or not, no memory-check give-back (run L had none), joined measured per step against main's run in the same history. The rule at each step is the mood plan's: joined at or over 245,764 B where main's is, and within 122,880 B of main's.
- **The decoders' carves are the device's now** (the review). The harness had asked for the Opus's carved sizes, 65,536 and 62,464 B, which `ModelHeap::carved()` rounds again (67,584 and 63,488 B), and for a FLAC one 65,536 B block and 16,384 B. The device asks for 65,307, 61,440 and 1,276 B for the Opus (`src/audio/OpusGenerator.cpp:50-52`; the logs' `[opus] buffers: 128023 B`), which carve 65,536, 62,464 and 1,312 B; and libFLAC (ESP8266Audio's, built with `FLAC__ALIGN_MALLOC_DATA` and the 64 Kbit bit reader of a non-ESP8266 build) for its private struct (about 6 KB), an 8,192 B bit reader (`bitreader.c:106, 274`), then each channel's output and residual, 16,400 and 16,415 B (`stream_decoder.c:1316-1325`): no block over about 16.9 KB, 82,072 B with headers in the model against the 82,120 B the control measured (`mainrun.log`: 429,340 B free while the FLAC played, 511,460 B after `qx`).
- **Both shapes are run**: the device's ("dev", the measure from here on) and the old one ("old", what the design was first drawn on), each over the wide set (every combination of the Opus kept never, before step 0, or after step 0-3; a FLAC held through no step or one of steps 0-4; a `Dc` after each step or not: 72 histories, 360 steps) and its named subsets: H1-H4 (the comparison's), H5-H8, K1-K4 and D (round 2's own sequence, below). The harness is `rework/r2/m.cpp` in the session's scratch folder (the review's copy with both shapes, the FatFiles and the shapes below), built with the host's g++ 11.2.0 against the worktree's native `libcore.a` (from 23:04, before f073406; it touches only `Fold` and `sizeof(Glue)`, as in the comparison); its outputs are `rework/r2/out*.txt`.

"The rework" in these tables is the design below: the block of 3,584 B at `PlayHistory::begin()`'s place; a records' save (its work memory and two FatFiles) carved and freed after each step's `Dc` and in each fence's pass; at the boot, the scan first, holding the ranges' block (3,072 B) at the top keeping 64 KB free, the Opus opening while it is held where the history opens it before step 0, then the boot's save (its work memory and FatFiles in one call) and the boot's join. Its wide-set figures equal those of the 3,584 B block alone with the Opus opening before the boot's join (the same order), in both shapes; with the old shape, those of the block alone. H5's step 4 (-110,592 B in dev) is main's own noise: 249,844 B, over the line, and any held block from 2,048 B on gives the same there.

**The model doesn't reproduce round 2** (the review). Round 2's sequence is history D: the Opus kept before the first update, the MP3 update, the Opus update, the FLAC playing through the third, a `Dc` after each. On the device S1 minus main was 0, -106,496 and -67,584 B at those three updates, main 294,900, 294,900 and 188,404 B. The model on D: 0, +36,864 and -155,648 B in the old shape (main 249,844, 249,844, 294,900 B); -69,632, 0 and +36,864 B in the device's (main 249,844, 249,844, 180,212 B). The design had matched round 2's steps against H2's steps 0, 2 and 3, a history with the FLAC through the fourth update: a fit chosen after the fact, now withdrawn. So R1 shows the rework within main's own variation in the model, not what the device will show: **the device re-run against main's figures, not R1, accepts the block's size and the transients' placement.**

**The wide set, and what it says about any fixed set.** One block carved at `PlayHistory::begin()`'s place and held (MEASURED-HOST):

| Held from setup | dev: mean, worst, failing of 360 | old: mean, worst, failing of 360 |

| 3,072 B | -950 B, -110,592 B, 0 | -364 B, -90,112 B, 2 |
| **3,584 B, the rework's block** (with its transients: the same) | **-933 B, -110,592 B, 0** | **-603 B, -90,112 B, 2** (both among the control's 6) |
| 4,096 B | -933 B, -110,592 B, 0 | -1,041 B, -118,784 B, 4 |
| 6,144 B | -5,308 B, -110,592 B, 0 | -1,667 B, -118,784 B, 4 |
