- **The place doesn't matter; the bytes do** (old shape, the design's run). 37,344 B gives the same figures carved first in setup, at `begin()`, at setup's end, after the boot's join, at the top of PSRAM, as one block or seven (H1-H4: -46,899 B mean, 7 of 20 failing). In setup every carve comes from the boot's one free block, so a block anywhere in it moves everything carved after it by its bytes. No placement makes a resident set free.
- **The cost is not a clean staircase, and its edges move with the decoders' shapes** (the review). In the old shape 4,096 B lost two steps more than 3,584 B (K1 and K2: a FLAC's one 64 KB block held through step 4), which was the design's reason for 3,584 B; in the device's shape 4,096 B equals 3,584 B, nothing from 2,048 to 6,144 B fails a step, and 1,024 B fails one that 2,048 B doesn't. What both shapes agree on: up to about 4 KB a set fails only steps main's own variation fails too, and from 8 KB it fails steps the control doesn't (old 21, dev 3; 12 KB: 31 and 11). So **3,584 B is a budget**, held by the `static_assert` and confirmed by the device re-run, not an edge the model found: it leaves room under the 4-6 KB the model can't tell from main's noise, and the rule for S2-S6 (nothing added to the block unless R1 still passes) rests on the corrected model.
- **A block carved and freed in one call leaves no trace.** A records' save (its work memory and two FatFiles, 19,192 B in three blocks) after each step's `Dc` and in each fence's pass, the boot's save's work memory and FatFiles, and a records' save at 96 in update 0's fence, before or after its build (the review's case, below): every figure equals the block's alone, in both shapes.
- **The update's wait for the scan is load-bearing** (the review). With the scan's blocks held through update 0's build, or carved in its fence and freed after its build (what dropping `mayScan()`'s wait for an asked update would allow), steps main passes fail: a full log's overflow held through the build 34 of 360 in both shapes (in the old one two FLAC carves fail besides); carved in the fence 48 (old) and 35 (dev); even the usual boot's work memory alone held through the build 35 and 15. R1's case runs the first two with the design's own scan blocks (the ranges' 3,072 B and 16 chunks, the boot's save's work memory and three FatFiles carved and freed at the scan's end; the runs above held the work memory with the chunks): held through the build 34 of 360 with the device's decoders and 24 with the old (two decoder blocks finding no room besides), carved in the fence 25 and 48 (MEASURED-HOST). Freed before the first update's memory check, as the waits below make it, they cost nothing more than the cases that follow. (As built, R6, with the firmware's blocks: held through the build, 13 of 65 steps fail and 62 of 360 (60 beyond the control's) with the device's decoders, 9 of 65 (7 beyond) and 31 of 360 (29) with the old; carved in the fence, 1 and 25, 12 (10) and 48 (45). MEASURED-HOST.)
- **A decoder opening under the boot's held blocks** (a tap on Play during the boot's scan; re-run in both shapes, wide set, steps failing of 360, old / dev). Main with the Opus opening before the boot's join fails none (old 0 / dev 0), and the 3,584 B block with that order 2 / 0, so the order alone costs nothing:

| Held across the scan's passes while the Opus opens | The join first, then the scan | The scan first, the join waiting |
|---|---|---|
| **The rework: the ranges' block (3,072 B), at the top keeping 64 KB** | 2 / 0 | **2 / 0** |
| The ranges' block by good fit | | 2 / 0 |
| The design before the review: the scan's work memory (10,896 B), at the top keeping 64 KB | 2 / 0 | 2 / **8** |
| ... at the top keeping nothing | | 2 / 0 |
| ... by good fit | 3 / **8** | 3 / 0 |
| A full log's overflow with the ranges (3,072 B and 16 chunks of 16,384 B), at the top keeping 64 KB | 2 / 0 | 2 / **29** (all 12 histories with the Opus before step 0 fail steps 0 and 1: 180,212 B against 249,844 B) |
| ... with the work memory (10,896 B and 16 chunks), at the top keeping 64 KB | 2 / **10** | 2 / 1 |
| ... keeping nothing | 2 / 3 | 2 / 5 |
| ... by good fit | 1 / 3 | 3 / 4 |
| **... the Opus opening only after it is given back (a track's start waiting: the rework)** | 2 / 0 (the work memory's) | **2 / 0** (the ranges') |

  In the old shape none of these fails a step the block alone doesn't (the design's finding). In the device's the scan's 10,896 B held across passes failed 8 in two placements of five, and the ranges' 3,072 B none in any: so the scan holds only its ranges (below), and the boot's save carves its work memory in its own call. A full log's overflow (about 270 KB) is another matter: with the Opus opening while it is held it fails 0 to 29 steps in the device's shape, by the order of the scan and the join and by a few KB of the scan's first block, and no placement tried makes it free; with the Opus opening after it, none. So **a track's start waits while the boot's fold holds overflow chunks** (below): about 1.4 s and its save for a full log, once a card (the first stats boot on a card 0.14.0 never synced, or the first after a 0.14.0 player's long use). The top keeping 64 KB stays (the review's reason; good fit is no better). Run I on the device measures the overflow with a tap on Play during it. (As built, R6, MEASURED-HOST, the firmware's blocks: the Opus opening under the scan's blocks, a full log's (the ranges' 1,284 B and 16 chunks) or a usual boot's (the ranges and the one chunk its first piece reserves), the scan first, fails no step beyond the control's in either shape, named or wide; with the device's decoders its boot's join is main's with the Opus opening before the boot's join (466,932 B: the order's own cost, against 589,812 B after it), with the old shapes 16,384 B under that (450,548 B, in each of the 12 histories of 72 with the Opus at the boot). The raw blocks of the table had failed 29: the ranges' 3,072 B, and the boot's save's blocks carved while all the scan's were held, where the glue gives the ranges' block back first and the chunks while its work memory is held. So with the device's decoders the start's wait is not load-bearing in the model; it is kept, the owner's default.) (The rework review, MEASURED-HOST: R6 had measured it with the ranges' block at 1,284 B alone and the Opus opening after the scan's last piece. Opening after the full log's first piece or its 50th gives the same figures; but with the root's ACKS 16 rows above MARK, the ranges' block at its largest (3,076 B, a card a 0.14.0 player synced before its first stats boot), the Opus opening under a full log's overflow fails 10 of 65 steps (H2, H5, K3, K4 and D at steps 0 and 1: 180,212 B against main's 249,844 B) and the boot's join 401,396 B against main's with the Opus first 466,932 B; 29 of 360 in the wide set (mean -9,455 B, worst -180,224 B), and with the old shapes none beyond the control's. So the start's wait is load-bearing at the ranges' block's largest, and the rework with it (that full log, a start waiting) fails none.)
- **The boot's join must not place around a held overflow**: with a full log's overflow (the work memory and 16 chunks, 281 KB) held and the Opus open, the join can't place in 12 of the 72 histories (all 12 with the Opus kept before step 0) and the boot's joined is 155,636 B against 589,812 B (both shapes). So the join waits while the scan's blocks are held, and the hold is short (below).

**Moved to internal RAM (2026-11-17; the owner's decision, "Move it").** The rework's device re-run (2026-11-17, the spare card, the control's script with main's control in the same sequence; logs `dev/rework` and `dev/control2`) matched main's PSRAM figures exactly at every point but one: after the library update made while a FLAC plays, the largest free block was 106,484 B against main's 188,404 B, the same again in a second run. The one standing PSRAM difference was free 3,268-3,276 B lower: the glue's object (3,200 B and its heap header). It shifts the holes, so Auto DJ's 76,944 B kept block no longer fits its usual one ("2 of 3 kept" where main keeps 3 of 3). The heap model had predicted no cost for that history (D), so only the device judges PSRAM, and the owner chose to take the stats out of it:
- **The object is internal DRAM's `.bss`** (`app/ListeningStats.cpp`'s `s_glue`, made with placement new in `ListeningStats::begin()`, never destroyed), not a carve from the internal heap. It is never in the heap, so it never splits a free block another internal carve would have taken (the card worker's 7.5 KB stack at each burst of jobs, A2DP's buffers, a track's 4.1 KB of decoder buffers), as a carve in setup would, and it can't fail. It does shrink the DRAM heap above `.bss` by its size (the internal-RAM review): that heap runs from `_heap_start`, `.dram0.bss`'s end, to 0x3FFE0000 (79,288 B in the review's build, 2,096 B less than S0's), and `heap_caps_malloc()` tries the internal heaps in address order, DRAM's before the D/IRAM ones above it, so a carve that fit at that heap's end on main can go to the next internal heap instead. No free block is split, but carves can shift between heaps (where Bluedroid's link buffers, the card worker's stack and a track's decoder buffers land), the same class of effect as the FLAC row in PSRAM, which `ram=` and `min=` alone wouldn't show, so the re-run recorded the internal `largest=` and the card worker's lowest beside main's: on the speaker one `[heap]` `largest=` moved by more than the object's 2 KB, boot 2's at `library` and `dance` (63K against main's 67K, equal again at `ui`), and one `[rate]` line's largest block (`lo2`'s Opus table copy, 47,092 B against main's 51,188 B; the other `[rate]` lines 2,048 B under), and nothing failed; the worker's lowest while a step ran (a sampled figure) moved by 1,912-2,320 B with the headphones linked (below, "Its device re-run"). Its cost is the same at every boot, card or none, and shows in the build's RAM figure (`.dram0.bss` +1,992 B; 2,096 B over S0's with the rework's 96 B of statics and the review's 8 B, `Ht`'s hash).
- **MARK's block stays in PSRAM, by plain `psramAlloc` since fix G** (2026-11-17, below; from the internal-RAM review until then at the top of its largest free block through `topAlloc()`, which would have held a free block T above it for the session: B1; the move had put it on the internal heap). A card with more than 8 MARK rows gets it: its rows and 8 more, 28 B a row (476 B at 9 rows, 560 B at 12), through `statsglue::Memory::keep`, the firmware's `psramAlloc` and `psramFree`, carved once by the boot's read in setup and held for the session. Its size is the card's, with no bound (MARK's rows are never dropped: each (deviceId, epoch) folded, so each player and each NVS erase, adds one), and internal RAM's margin has none to give: on the internal heap a 9-row card's block (476 B and its header) would have put the walk under G5's 42 KB at METADATA 3.8's figures (the budget bullet), and a card of hundreds of rows would have taken tens of KB in setup, after the audio's stage, before the A2DP link's buffers, the card worker's stack and the decoders' buffers. So internal RAM holds the object alone, whatever the card. A card of 8 rows or fewer (the spare card has 2) carves none; a card past 8 rows holds that block for the session where the heap puts it (above the read's work memory and FatFile unless a hole fits it), main having no such block. With no PSRAM for it the read is `NoMemory`, and the stats are off. (Internal instead would need a cap counted in the budget, `static_assert(sizeof(statsglue::Glue) + cap + 16 <= 2048)`: 40 B, under the smallest block (9 rows and 8 more: 476 B), so the object would have to shrink first. The owner's call, settled 2026-11-19: PSRAM; section 1, call 2.)
- **Nothing of the stats is held in PSRAM between calls but the boot's scan's blocks** (and MARK's block on a card past 8 rows, above). **Not "so PSRAM is main's"** (B1, below): the blocks held across passes left a free block T above them for the hold, and a full log's chunks gaps between them, where a long-lived carve made meanwhile (the first frame's canvas, a queue) pinned the largest free block split for the session; fix A closes T with caps and puts the overflow in one arena. The ranges' block (1,284-3,076 B) is given back at the fold's end: the boot's save, `ready()` after a partial scan, `scanAway()`, or a remount's `scanBegan(true)`. A fold past the records holds overflow chunks until the boot's save lands or is held; after `[plays] ready`, only while that save is tried again (100, 300 and 1,000 ms apart), then behind. Every other PSRAM block the stats carve is given back in its call: each read's, save's and look's work memory (`statsfile::kWorkBytes`, 11,456 B), the FatFiles of the files it opens (`FatFs::open()`'s `psramNew<FatFile>`, 4,148 B) and the FILINFOs of its questions (`FatFs::look()`, `stat()`). **The audit** (2026-11-17), every PSRAM call of the stats' code:
  - `app/ListeningStats.cpp`: `psramLargest()` and `psramFreeBytes()` (questions, for `topAlloc()`'s spacers); `topAlloc()` (its spacers given back in the call; then the ranges' block and the overflow's arena, the scan's); `Memory::alloc` and `release` (`psramAlloc`, `psramFree`: the work memory, in `Glue::readNow()`, `commit()`, `track()`, `lookFile()` and `lookDays()`'s `lookRows()`, given back in each). `Memory::keep` is plain `psramAlloc` since fix G (MARK's block, held for the session where the heap puts it). Since fix A, `topAlloc()` and `topRelease()` are an `autodj::TopHold`: the spacers, the block (the ranges' block, the overflow's arena; no room for the arena, its chunks one by one, carved as before the caps), then `heap_caps_walk(MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)` (a question under the heap's lock) and the caps (`psramAlloc`, held with the block and freed after it, bottom-up; given back early, `TopHold::uncap()`, when the overflow falls back to chunks); the boot's first take also asks `heap_caps_get_info()`, and a take no free block can hold asks `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block()` and carves nothing.
  - `lib/core`'s `StatsFold`, `StatsFile` and `StatsGlue` carve only through those hooks: `Fold::carveChunk()` by `top`, the store's MARK block by `keep`, the rest placement new into the work memory. Their default hooks (`malloc`) are the host's.
  - `PlayHistory`'s own PSRAM (the read buffer, the Tracker, the Journal, the Outbox) is the play log's, main's since 0.14.0.
  - The console's looks that read the card (`H`'s card part, `Hd`, `Ht`) carve a save's work memory and a FatFile, but as moved they ran at once on the loop, at no pass the write gate chose: typed in a start's, a jump's or a join's window, their blocks could be held while the decode task carves a track's long-lived blocks (the first Opus's 128,023 B of buffers kept for the boot, a FLAC's six blocks for its track), and PSRAM would differ from main's for the session or the track (the internal-RAM review). Since the review they wait as `Hs` does: `command()` notes the look (`H`'s RAM part, `Hm`, and a reply that needs no card print at once), and `PlayHistory::loop()` makes it in `Hs`'s turn (`turnOf(saveDue, slotsDue, hsWaiting || lookWaiting)`, `Hs` first), one a pass, at a pass the write gate lets through after a save due and the slots due, and never behind the library update's fence (no save carves there either). Their depth from `PlayHistory::loop` (`-fstack-usage`, FatFs's own frames not counted): `Hd`'s open 1,296 B, its line with printf's own about 2,096 B; `Ht`'s open 1,248 B; `H`'s card part's open 848 B (each look's lines, printf's own with them, under `Hd`'s); all under the boot's save's line (2,208 B) and the rotation's save (2,464 B).
- **The budget is internal RAM's margin** (11's Internal RAM row): **2,048 B**, the `static_assert` on `sizeof(statsglue::Glue)` (the PSRAM budget was 3,584 B), set against G5's floors (docs/OPUS.md: steady 50 KB or more, the session's lowest 42 KB or more, the headphones linked) at the lowest figures then held with a link (METADATA 3.8's check, 2026-11-04, on another firmware). At those figures 3,200 B would have put the walk and the steady figure under (42,268 B; 50,048 B). What the device loses is the build's `.dram0.bss` over main's, not the object alone (the internal-RAM review: as moved, this bullet counted 2,048 B and said it kept G5's floors): 2,096 B over S0's (the object's 1,992 B, the rework's 96 B of statics, the review's 8 B), and `ram=` truncates to the KB (`internalFree / 1024`). So, from those figures, `ram=52K` with a link and the worker gone (53,248-54,271 B) was expected at 51,152-52,175 B: G5's steady 50 KB (51,200 B) with 0-1 KB to spare, 48 B short at the reading's low end. A walk on the card worker under a Bluetooth play, 45,468 B, at 43,372 B, 364 B over 42 KB (43,008 B). A whole Rescan's scan under a Bluetooth play (`min=43K`, 44,032-45,055 B, that check's lowest) at 41,936-42,959 B, under 42 KB: the session's floor not shown kept. **Measured** with the headphones linked (the re-run, below, main's firmware beside the move): steady `ram=` 56K (57,344-58,367 B; main 58K), the walk's lowest 49,288 B (main 51,304 B), the Rescan's 49,124 B (main 51,000 B): both floors hold with about 6 KB to spare, on main too. The expectation was 5,169-7,215 B low, in its base, not its cost: main reads about 6 KB over 3.8's figures now, and the move costs 1,860-2,192 B against main at every figure read to the byte but one sampled (2,320 B). So on that run (link first, a real stream) G5's session floor was not the owner's call; in the play-before-link order it was (main 89188f4 itself read 35,916 B there on 2026-11-19), and the owner settled it that day on the merged build's real-stream figure, 50,764 B, silent mode's 42,808 B beside it (section 1; "Its device runs").
- **So the object holds 40 records, not 96** (`statsfold::kRecords`; `tools/statsref.py`'s `RECORDS`), **and their save is due at 24** (`kSaveAt`, `SAVE_AT`). The design's 2:3 is kept roughly: 16 records of slack for a fence, a backlog or a card that fails, where there were 32. The cost: `stats.bin` is written each 24 plays (about 1.5 h of music) instead of each 64, and a boot whose log holds more than 40 slots above the card's MARK overflows into a chunk (a 0.14.0 card's first stats boot, as before, or a session whose saves failed). Hm keeps 4 log keys, not 8. The object is 2,008 B on the device since fix A (`xtensa-esp32-elf-g++`'s `sizeof`; 1,992 B as moved): the Fold 1,544 B (HEAD 112, MARK's 16 rows 448, the 40 records 800, its state 184, the arena's pointer in it), the store 56, two `Opened` 112, the last save's report 60, Hm's 4 keys 96, the save's ask 32, `Memory` 24 (with `keep` and `topRelease`), the counters and flags; 40 B under its budget. Every rule of this section stands; only the counts move. - **The internal-RAM review** (2026-11-17, on f64c3f7: the internal heap, its budget, PSRAM's byte-for-byte rule). Five findings, each checked against the code, the link map and the device's logs, and fixed (the bullets above amended):
  1. and 5. MARK's block on the internal heap, its size the card's with no bound, outside the 2,048 B budget: a 9-row card would have put the walk under 42 KB at METADATA 3.8's figures. Fixed the first way the review gave: `Memory::keep` is `topAlloc()` (PSRAM's top); `StatsRun` carves it in the model heap again (a new assertion on `markHeld`, failing first with the block outside the model). The other way, internal with a cap inside the budget, leaves 40 B, no block's room: the owner's call (settled 2026-11-19: PSRAM; section 1).
  2. The budget counted the object alone, and the re-run measured no link: the budget bullet and 11's row recounted with the build's statics and `ram=`'s truncation; the headphones' runs added to the re-run; the comments (`app/ListeningStats.cpp`'s `static_assert`) reworded.
  3. "It splits no free block" said more than it can: reworded (it shrinks the DRAM heap, which can move a carve to the next internal heap); the internal `largest=` and the worker's lowest added to the re-run.
  4. The console's looks carved PSRAM at once on the loop: deferred to `Hs`'s turn (the audit, above). The deferral costs 8 B of `.bss` (`Ht`'s hash).

**The hold's T (B1, 2026-11-17) and its fix, A + G** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-17: "Fix it first", fix A and G of the investigation; built, host-gated and device-checked: 2026-11-18 on 94cdf0c and d4a5357, the spare card, main 218822a's figures as the control: "Its device runs", below).
- **What the device found** (B1: the move's c3f1f09 on the spare card, 3 MARK rows, main's runs on the same board as the control; MEASURED, checked by an adversarial verdict against the raw logs). Free PSRAM was main's to the byte at every point, but the largest free block was split on the boots whose first frame (`[ui] up`) or a queue started from the console came inside the boot's hold. The largest free block 3 s after Auto DJ's join, an MP3 playing: main 638,964 B; the move **589,812 B** when the first frame and a start came before the boot's fold ended (I-3m and I-3m2, the same to the byte, though one folded 2 slots and wrote and the other folded none: 49,152 B, three 16 KB classes, under main's at the join; after `qx` 483,316 B against 524,276 B, 40,960 B under), and **442,356 B** after a full-log boot (I-1: 196,608 B, twelve classes, counted against main's I-3 and I-0 runs, not a main run on the same full log with `qp966`: the verdict's open question 3). The boots whose fold ended before the first frame (I-2, idle1-5) had `D`'s free and largest main's to the byte (659,348/638,964 B); their join kept 4,992 B higher than main I-0's (`free at least` 4,992 B lower), 4,784 B against main I-3's, whose joins overlapped main's own scan where the move's waits for the stats' hold (not a stats effect). So the move failed this section's acceptance (one class) on those boots. Nothing failed outright (no alarm, no failed allocation, no underrun); the cost is margin, for the session: a growing library update after an I-1-like boot is deferred to the next boot (`LibraryUpdate::roomToBuild()` wants 17/16 of the table, about 660 KB at 19.4k tracks), after an I-3m-like one it passes with about 48 KB less (about 22.2k tracks at most, about 23.6k on main).
- **Why** (the verdict's mechanism). `topAlloc()` carved a held block under Auto DJ's spacers keeping 64 KB free: the block at the low end of R, what the spacers left of the largest free block (about 66.5 KB at the boot), then the spacers given back. That left a free block T of about 57-59 KB physically above the held block, at PSRAM's top. TLSF's good fit sends any carve between about 1.3 KB and T's size made during the hold into T: the first frame's cover canvas (`NowPlayingPage`: a 352 B `M5Canvas` object and its 19,208 B sprite, carved 19,456 B, never freed), a queue (`QueueModel`, about 2.3 KB with the undo). When the hold ended the freed block merged downward, not past that pin. On a full log each of the 16 chunks was carved the same way, with gaps of 1-17 KB between them. `pmin=63K` was that window, not only the spacers. MARK's block, held for the session through `topAlloc()` since the internal-RAM review, would have left a T for the whole session on a card past 8 rows (never run: the spare card has 3 rows).
- **Fix A: close T** (`lib/core/AutoDjJoin`: `capRequest()`, `closeAbove()`, `autodj::TopHold`; `app/ListeningStats.cpp`: `topAlloc()`, `topRelease()`, the heap walk; `statsglue::Memory::topRelease`):
  - The spacers keep the margin **with the block carved**: `takeSpacers(bytes, 64 KB + carvedAtMost(bytes))`, as the join counts its own blocks. With 64 KB alone the arena's 262 KB, carved under its spacer, would have taken free PSRAM to about 16-29 KB for that instant (the spec's challengers' model); now the lowest free during a take is 67-78 KB in the model (`test_the_caps_keep_the_margin`).
  - The block, then the spacers given back, then **a heap walk** (`heap_caps_walk(MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)`: ESP-IDF 5.5's TLSF, light poisoning; the walker reports a block by its TLSF pointer, malloc's less the poisoning's 8 B, and its TLSF block size, the 12 B of poisoning and the class rounding in) finds the free block right above it, and **caps close it**: each the size class floor of what is left less 12 B, so that TLSF maps it to that block's own free list, whose head that block is, and carves the floor from it; each must come back at the address the walk gave (anything else, another task's carve first or another block heading the list, is freed at once: Missed, no retry, the rest left open); a walk after each. Three caps at most close any free block to 4 MB, and what is left is 0 B or a 12 B block no request can take (`capcheck.py`; `test_caps_close_any_free_block` builds every free size from 12 B to 64 KB exactly and 2,000 to 1 MB, its counts capcheck's).
  - Caps only when the block sits on the spacers' top (the free block right under it holds the last spacer's address): a block a smaller free block took (a hole) gets none, so the hole's rest stays where main's carves go (a carve that needed the whole hole goes elsewhere, so that layout isn't main's byte for byte, at most the block's footprint off: the merge's PSRAM audit's model, 2026-11-19; since #64 a freed 4 KB stdio buffer can leave such a hole; b3h-e's ranges' take is the one on the device so far). Never under the margin (64 KB and 1 KB) free: checked against the first walk's free block, and again before every cap after the first, which also stops when the block above is larger than T less the caps' footprints (another task freed the block right above it between two caps: merged in, it would head its list at the walk's address and the next cap would take it whole; Left, the caps held kept, `test_a_block_freed_into_t_between_caps`). A take no free block can hold is NoBlock without a carve (`largest()` first, exact: TLSF serves no request over it), so the firmware's failed-allocation hook prints no `[heap] FAILED` line for the arena a cut-up heap can't hold (`test_no_block_asks_no_carve`).
  - **Given back bottom-up**: a top block is freed, the block first and then its caps in carve order, only once every top block taken after it is given back. `commit()` gives the ranges' block back before the save lands; while the overflow's arena (carved after it, under it) is held, it waits, and the arena's give-back frees the arena, its caps, the ranges' block and its caps. So no free block ever opens above a held one.
  - **The overflow in one arena**: `statsfold::kArenaBytes`, 262,080 B (262,144 B carved, not 270,336 B), all 16 chunks' room, carved at the first chunk through the same hook (one block, one T, capped; no gaps), the chunks packed in it, given back whole when no chunk is in use (`Fold::held()` and `peak()` report its bytes). No room for it (no free block of 262 KB: the yardstick's scan after update 0 had none in 6 of 13 histories; mid-session, the spec's open question 5): NoBlock with no carve, and each chunk a carve of its own (`test_no_memory`, a chunk's carve failing in turn too), carved as fe61db9 carved them: `topAlloc()` gives the hold's caps back at the first chunk (`TopHold::uncap()`: T above the ranges' block open again), each chunk under spacers keeping 64 KB alone, nothing closed (`TopHold::take(..., close false)`: Plain); the first chunk is the hold's second entry (the ranges' block, given back by `commit()`, waits behind it), the rest past its two entries (Full) are freed at once when given back. So that hold is fe61db9's, PSRAM and layout (the review found the first build, which kept the ranges' caps and capped the first chunk, losing a full log fe61db9 folded: `test_the_arena_s_fallback_as_before`, the review's heap and 120 random ones, fe61db9's hooks saving a full log in 71: the firmware's loses none, the first build's 3).
  - **A decoder's room** (the recheck, 2026-11-18: hazard 5 for a track that follows one playing). That track opens during the hold, never held (main.cpp's `OutputHold` holds a start from a pause, a stop or a wait; a gap would be worse), its decoder's blocks carved while the hold's blocks and caps are held: GAPLESS.md 2.1's one decoder at a time gives the old one's blocks back first, but an MP3 gives back none (its decoder is the pinned block) and the session's first Opus carves 128,023 B (`OpusGenerator::allocBuffers()`: the page, the packet and a frame, kept for the session). The caps keep 64 KB and 1 KB free, not those blocks. The case: a remount whose re-read folds an overflow (a session behind), a track boundary inside its hold, the hold then the ranges' block with its T capped (about 71 KB mid-session: the spacers' margin counts the block) and the arena with its T' (about 78 KB), the largest free block what is left under them. In the model (Y5d: an MP3 playing, the session's first Opus opening after the re-read's first piece or its last, the session's blocks 0-224 KB more than the model's, the holes taken and not; 60 cases) 5e1d576's hold left the Opus no room in 36, 21 of them where fe61db9's hooks opened it, the first at 48 KB less free (the Opus after the first piece, where the arena holds every chunk's room at once and fe61db9 held one chunk). So a take asks room for the largest decoder's blocks when one may open before the block is given back (the trigger below; as first built, any take while a track played or a start waited) (`statsglue::kDecoderRoomBytes`, the Opus's 128,023 B; `TopHold::take(..., room, yield)`): with the block and every cap held, the largest free block must still take them (their carve, a thirty-second and 1 KB over: 133,047 B); short of that, every cap the hold holds is given back, the newest block's first (Room: T open again, as fe61db9 left it), and the arena, still short, is given back too (NoRoom: no block, so the fold carves its chunks one by one as before the caps, a piece at a time, as fe61db9 did). Now 16 of the 60 short, none where fe61db9's opened (fe61db9's own 19: its 16 chunks held too when the Opus opens after the last piece); main's layout at the model's own session in all four ways (the room there with every cap held: nothing given back) and in 26 of the 60; the arena given back in 40. A boot has the room (about 700 KB free): the boot's cases are unchanged. What it costs: where the room gives the caps or the arena back, that hold's layout is fe61db9's (Y5b, a FLAC playing, its holes taken: 498,588 B after the hold against main's 506,684 B, the Dc's join main's 491,508 B; a rule that can't know the next track keeps the Opus's room for a FLAC's too), and from 112 KB less free the re-read's chunks one by one find no room for all 16 (15 of the 60, each where fe61db9's didn't either, where 5e1d576's arena folded the log whole and left the Opus no room): the Opus first, the session already behind. The flag costs no RAM (1 B in `.bss`'s fill). **The trigger** (the final review, 2026-11-18). As first built every take asked while a track played or a start waited (`ListeningStats::pass()`), and the review found that wrong both ways. A start waiting at the arena's take can't open a decoder while the arena is held: `OutputHold` holds every start that isn't from Playing while overflow chunks are held, PlayGate's `statsHold` keeps it waiting, and the arena and its chunks are given back in one call (with the ranges' block waiting behind them). So the room asked for it could only give the caps back (Room) or the arena (NoRoom) for nothing. And while the ranges' block alone is held (before the overflow's first chunk: a usual log, or a full log's first 40 records) `OutputHold` holds no start, so a Play from a stop or a pause after a take that asked nothing opened its decoder with T capped and the room never checked. Now the ranges' block asks at every take, whatever the player (it never yields: at worst its T open again, as fe61db9 left it), and the arena and a chunk only while a track plays (`s_trackPlaying`, the pass's `playing`; a chunk's take gives the caps back first, so its room changes nothing). Since the review's second round the rule is `statsglue::decoderRoom(bytes, trackPlaying)`, which the firmware's `topAlloc()` and the host's model both call: the cases below test the rule the firmware runs (only the flag's line in `pass()` stays outside them). The arena is given back mid-scan only by its own NoRoom, which gives the ranges' caps back first, and its chunks then hold every start. Else it goes at the fold's end with the ranges' block (`Fold::endBoot()`'s `giveChunks()` and `fallBehind()` run inside `Glue::endBoot()`, which gives the ranges' block back in the same call), or later (the boot's save, or behind), the ranges' block waiting for it. Nothing else asks again when the player changes in the hold: a start while the arena or a chunk is held waits, and a track playing at their take was asked for. In the model (U14): one free block of 480 KB, the ranges' block closed, then the arena with a start waiting: kept and capped (Closed), the largest free block 83,956 B (the start can't open yet), and once both are given back the Opus opens; 94cdf0c's rule gave the arena back (NoRoom). Through the glue a full log folds in the arena with the start waiting, one chunk at a time with a track playing, both saved. U15: one free block of 130-320 KB in 64 B steps, the player stopped, the ranges' request 1,284 or 3,076 B: the take gives its caps back (Room) in 1,024 heaps of each (138,560-204,032 B; 140,416-205,888 B), and the session's first Opus opens wherever fe61db9's hooks opened it; the take without the room (94cdf0c's for a stopped player) left it short in 960 and 962 of those heaps (138,560-199,936 B; 133,120-201,792 B), the packet's 61,440 B the one short in 928 and 930, the frame's 1,276 B in 32. Two of the 3,076 B heaps (133,120 and 133,184 B) leave the Opus short with the room asked too: the take leaves T open there (Margin), but its spacers keep the margin with the block carved (fix A), so the block sits 4 KB lower than under fe61db9's (at 61,444 B, not 65,540 B), and the packet finds no block; fix A's placement, not the trigger's (pinned in U15 since the review's second round: 2 at 3,076 B, none at 1,284 B, so a change that widens it fails the case; with the model's spacers keeping 8 KB more it read 101 at 1,284 B). U16 (`test_the_room_s_rule`): `decoderRoom()` itself; every ranges' request (the fold's need, 16 B to 512 KB in 16 B steps, and 4 B) asks 128,023 B with a track playing, a start waiting or the player stopped, the arena and a chunk only with a track playing. The room is checked once, at the take: main's carves later in the hold that shrink the largest free block aren't seen (as before).
  - **The line** (one a take, owed: printed at the next pass, never inside the walk or the scan's frames): `[listen] the hold's top block (<which>): <bytes> B at 0x<p>; the <T> B free above it closed by <n> caps (<bytes> B) (<carved> B carved; <walks> walks in <us> us, the longest <us> us, <blocks> blocks)`, or why T was left open (in use above it: a probe or a FatFile is 4,224 B; no spacers; in a hole; the margin; a cap that came back elsewhere, both addresses; no PSRAM for a cap; the walk didn't meet it; the caps' most or the block above grown between caps; carved as before the caps, the arena's fallback). `<which>` by the take's size: `the ranges'`, `the overflow's arena` (262,080 B), `an overflow chunk (no room for the arena)` (16,384 B; the ranges' request is the fold's need + 4 B, need a multiple of 16, so never either). A take that walked nothing (no spacers, a chunk carved as before the caps) says `(not walked)`, not `0 B carved`. No free block for it: `NO PSRAM for its <bytes> B (no free block takes it; ...)` (asked of the largest free block first: no carve fails). The fallback's first chunk adds `the hold's <n> caps given back first`. Three take lines at most between passes (the ranges' block, the arena or the arena's refusal and the first chunk), none overwritten (a fourth is counted, `not told`); the fallback's chunks past the hold's two blocks one line once they stop coming: `[listen] the hold's top blocks: <n> more overflow chunks (no room for the arena) carved as before the caps past the hold's two blocks: not tracked, nothing closed, each freed when given back`. The boot's first take adds PSRAM's block count (`heap_caps_get_info()`'s `total_blocks`) and one `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block()` walk's time, the yardstick for the walks'. Since the recheck: a take is Closed only when the caps' bytes are T's plus 4 (the 12 B rest aside); another task's block at the rest's head between a cap and its walk is Left (`... the rest LEFT OPEN (..., or another task's block took the rest's head)`), not Closed. A Lost take (its walk didn't meet the block) says `(the block not met; 1 walk in <us> us, ...)`, not `0 B carved`. The decoder's room: `the <T> B free above it LEFT OPEN: with the hold's caps held no free block takes a decoder's 128023 B (<why>): the hold's <m> caps given back, its <n> among them` (Room; `<why>` `a start or a track playing` for the ranges' block, `a track playing` for the arena; a block that closed nothing itself: `with the hold's caps held ...: the hold's <m> caps given back, none of them its own`; with none held, what the take did stands), and for the arena `[listen] the hold's top block (the overflow's arena): its 262080 B GIVEN BACK at once: with it held no free block takes a decoder's 128023 B (a track playing), the hold's <m> caps given back first; its chunks one by one, as before the caps` (NoRoom; the first chunk's line follows, carved as before the caps).
  - **The walk's cost**: it runs under the PSRAM heap's lock (`multi_heap_walk` takes `xPortEnterCriticalTimeout`: this core's interrupts masked to level 3; a PSRAM heap call on the other core, Bluetooth's, the decode task's, the card worker's, spins with its own masked until it ends), as every `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block()` already does (two a spacer in `takeSpacers()`). A boot's take walks 3 times (walk, cap, walk, cap, walk), a full log's two takes up to 8. The callback compares and copies words on the caller's frame, nothing else; it gives up at 8,192 blocks before it meets the block (Lost). Flash code (`heap_caps_walk`, `multi_heap_walk`, `tlsf_walk_pool`); no IRAM.
- **Fix G: MARK's block by plain `psramAlloc`**, where any session block goes (at a top it would hold its cap for the session). In the model a 12-row and a 40-row card are main's line against a control that replays the read's carves (the work memory, the FatFile, the block kept), and their boot's join is bare main's (573,428 B both). The investigation's replay model, with the device-matching small holes, put a 40-row block (1,344 B) above the read's work memory and FatFile with a 15.7 KB hole under it for the session, two classes under bare main: the C++ model doesn't show it, and no card past 8 rows has run on the device. If it shows there, the read can carve the block again once its work memory is given back and move MARK's rows (`Fold::markBlock()`).
- **What it costs**: PSRAM during the hold only (0.6-4.9 s at a boot; a full log's 4.6 s and 4.9 s measured, c1-i1 and s1mv-a1: "Its device runs"): in the model's boot heap the ranges' block's caps take T's 63,468 B (2 caps) and, with a full log, the arena's T 73,720 B more (3 caps): about 400 KB held at a full log's peak against about 272 KB before (free about 439 KB meanwhile; before, more free but its largest split by the chunks' gaps). Any overflow, one chunk or 16, carves the whole arena and its caps: about 400 KB held for the hold where a one-chunk overflow (41-859 records: a 0.14.0 card's first stats boot, a session whose saves failed) held about 18 KB before (one 16,900 B chunk and the 1,316 B ranges' block); the fold's `[listen]` line (`the overflow N B at the peak`) and `H`'s RAM line report the arena's 262,080 B. Owner's call: an arena sized to what the scan can still need (settled 2026-11-19: the whole 16 chunks' room stays; section 1). No room for the arena: the chunks as before the caps, the hold's caps given back (fe61db9's PSRAM for that hold). A decoder's room (above, the recheck; since the final review the ranges' block's at every take, the arena's and a chunk's while a track plays): the caps given back, and the arena, when the largest free block wouldn't take the Opus's buffers with them held (fe61db9's PSRAM and layout for that hold; at a boot the room is there and nothing is given back, at the model's mid-session remount with an MP3 playing too, but with a FLAC playing and the holes taken the arena is given back: Y5b). A start waits while the overflow is held, Auto DJ's join and an asked library update while the scan's blocks are (unchanged), and the first frame (19.6 KB), the scan's and the save's FatFiles and work memory, a queue and the card worker's walk fit, and so does a track that follows one playing, its decoder opening in the hold, wherever fe61db9's hold left it room (Y5d: an MP3's next track the session's first Opus; before the recheck short in 21 of 60 such cases where fe61db9's opened it).
