**The resident set.** One block, the glue's object (`statsglue::Glue`), carved once with `psramNew` in `PlayHistory::begin()` at its place now (setup, after Auto DJ's glue) and never freed until the restart, the stats off included. **At most 3,584 B** on the device, a `static_assert` on `sizeof(statsglue::Glue)` in the core2 build: a budget (above), not a figure the model pins. It goes to PSRAM through `psramNew` (the IDF's 4 KB always-internal limit is `malloc`'s, not this carve's). Nothing else of the stats is held between saves, but MARK's extra block below. *Since 2026-11-17 the object is internal RAM's `.bss`, at most 2,048 B, with 40 records, and MARK's block PSRAM's, by plain `psramAlloc` since fix G (until then at the top of its largest free block: above, "Moved to internal RAM" and its internal-RAM review).*

| What | Bytes (on the device) | Why it is there |
|---|---|---|
| The glue's and the store's state: the copy RAM stands on (generation and header CRC), the last save's report, the read's verdict, the ending flag, Hm's 8 log keys, the counters, the Fold's state | at most 1,280 B (as built 2,576 B without the ring, `xtensa-esp32-elf` sizes: ACKS's 16 rows (400 B), a second `Opened` (48 B), 8 of the 16 log keys (24 B each), the give-back's state, and most of the Fold's 1,432 B, its chunk pointers and positions, go; ACKS's rows are the boot fold's, in the ranges' block). As built, R5: 544 B beside the Fold's 2,656 B, the glue's object 3,200 B on the device (`xtensa-esp-elf-g++`'s `sizeof`; 3,368 B on the 64-bit host): the store 56, the save's ask 32, the boot's read and the last read (`Opened`, 56 B each: the second kept, for `H` and the remount's verdict), the last save's report 60, Hm's 8 log keys 192, the counters and flags; the root's ACKS held by reference (the root doesn't change in a boot), its rows above MARK copied into the ranges' block by the boot's fold | As now |
| HEAD (112 B in RAM) and the session's round | in it | Every slot's totals at its fold; the screens' lifetime figures |
| MARK's first 16 rows | 448 B (as built, R3: 28 B a row, the file's 24 and the seq a held tmp holds, which `covered()` answers by once it is promoted, as statsref's `_promote`; the table had 384 B, so the state's row has 64 B less) | `covered()` for the trim and the rotation, `markOf()` for the seq rule, the fold's "above MARK". A card with more than 8 rows (each NVS erase or player adds one) carves one block more at the boot's read, its rows + 8, in setup right after the object, for the session. More new (deviceId, epoch) pairs than that in one boot is the fold out of memory (no save, as now). **A remount's read** (before the boot's first slot, the only time MARK is read again) whose MARK has more rows than the 16 and that block hold (another player wrote the card while it was out) is a read with no memory: the stats off for the session (5.4: no fold, no save, no trim), never a new carve and never a MARK cut short, which the next save would write without those players' rows (the next boot would fold their slots twice) |
| The records: 96 of 20 B | 1,920 B | One a slot that counts, folded since the last save that landed: its pathHash (8 B), its UTC time in seconds from 2026 (4 B; 0 undated), its playedMs (4 B), its kind (a play, a skip, neither), its source, boot or live (its round); 3 B reserved |

What leaves RAM: HOUR (6,720 B) and DAYS (6,400 B), RCNT's and TRKD's chunks (16,900 B each, 3-6 and a spare), the boot's indexes, the ring: 62,296-194,968 B as built. They live in `stats.bin` alone, and each save merges them there. Nothing is added to the object later (S2-S6) unless it comes out of the records or the state, or R1's case still passes with it and the device re-run confirms it.

**Placement of what isn't resident.**
- A block the stats hold across passes (the boot's scan's ranges' block, the overflow's chunks) is carved at the top of the largest free block through the join's spacers (`autodj::takeSpacers()`), **keeping 64 KB free** as the join does (Auto DJ's `kJoinMarginBytes`; as built, R5, app/ListeningStats' own `kTopMarginBytes`, the same 64 KB: Auto DJ's constant is its file's own) (the review: the spacers are held only inside the carve's call, but another task's block must still fit meanwhile: a track start's 4 KB MP3 probe, a seek's 8 KB, the Opus packet buffer's 60 KB, the card worker's 4-16 KB, and the scan's carves come exactly when a tap on Play may; `src/app/AutoDj.cpp:121-138` found spacers over the whole block took free PSRAM to 10 KB). In the model the margin changed no figure of the rework's (the review's run, and the ranges' block here); with a full log's overflow it moved which steps fail (above). **Wrong as a property** (B1): the margin is what leaves T above the block, and the model then had no first frame and no queue in the hold; fix A (above) keeps the margin with the block carved and closes T with caps.
- A block carved and given back in one call (a save's or a read's work memory and FatFiles) goes by good fit; in the model it leaves no trace.
- **The scan holds only its ranges** (the model above): the ranges (80 B a MARK row) and ACKS's rows above MARK (112 B each, at most 16), 3,072 B with MARK's 16 rows, sized from MARK's rows at the scan's first piece. The boot's save carves its own work memory and FatFiles in its call, after the fold has dropped the ranges, as every other save does. (As built, R5: carved 4 B over and aligned to 8 by the glue, since ACKS's rows hold a u64 and PSRAM's heap gives 4-byte-aligned blocks: 1,284 B for 16 MARK rows and no ACKS row above MARK, 3,076 B at most; given back at the fold's end, the boot's save before it carves, `ready()` after a partial scan, or a remount's `scanBegan(true)`.) (The design had held the 10,896 B work memory from the scan's first piece to its save: in the device's decoder shape that cost 8 steps of 360 in two of five placements.)
- **The waits, in this order** (the review; S1 as built had the first two):
  - The boot's scan doesn't **begin** while a library update is asked: `ListeningStats::mayScan()`'s wait for an asked update stays (only its `givenBack()` half goes). So the scan's blocks are never carved behind an update's memory check or while its build runs (the give-back review had found the fold at 347-446 KB during a build), and the plays wait in the Tracker meanwhile, as built.
  - Once the scan **has begun**, a library update asked waits in Asked until `finishScan()` and the boot's save have given the scan's blocks back (`LibraryUpdate::Wait::Writes` becomes `Wait::Stats`, from `PlayHistory::statsScanHolds()`, like the join's `Block`): about 0.1-0.2 s for a usual boot, about 1.4 s and its save for a full log, the boot's waits more when that save doesn't land (below). The scan then doesn't wait for the update (`mayScan()`'s update wait applies only before its first piece): both waiting would deadlock. So the scan's blocks are never held across a fence, and the boot's save never runs during a build. (As built, R5: `Glue::scanWaits(updateAsked, joinWorking)` says the scan's wait, `ScanWait::Update` only while the fold hasn't begun, the boot's to begin or a remount's read to make, and none with the stats off; `Glue::scanHolds()` is the ranges' block or overflow chunks held; `Wait::Stats` sits where `Wait::Writes` did, after the safe point and Auto DJ's pick, before the worker's task and the memory check.)
  - Auto DJ's `stepJoin` doesn't begin while the scan's blocks are held (a `Block` reason: 6.8's rule for a stats rank or walk, brought forward), and the scan's pieces wait while the join is Working, as now: the join can't place around a full log's overflow (above). (As built, R5: `AutoDj::Env::statsHold`, `PlayHistory::statsScanHolds()` read each pass beside Build and Reads, not a stored `Block`; `AutoDj::joinSettled()` went with the take-back it served.)
  - **A track's start waits while the boot's fold holds overflow chunks** (the model above: a decoder's blocks carved while they are held land in the region the next update needs). A start the listener asks (a tap on Play, the headphones' play key, a pick) is held in the player's Waiting state, as one waiting for the headphones is, until the chunks are given back: about 1.4 s and the save for a full log, the boot's waits more when that save fails (below). Nothing starts by itself at a boot (`src/app/QueueStore.h:46-47`), so nothing playing is cut. A remount's scan again before the first slot (rare) holds the chunks while a track may play: its next track's open isn't held (a gap would be worse), and the hold keeps that decoder's room itself (the recheck, 5.14's "A decoder's room": modelled as Y5d, an MP3 playing and the session's first Opus next; the caps given back, and the arena, when the largest free block wouldn't take the Opus's buffers with them held; the Opus then short only where fe61db9's hold left it no room either, and from 112 KB less free than the model's session the re-read's chunks short too). (As built, R5: main.cpp's `OutputHold`, the headphones' Hold, holds too while `PlayHistory::overflowHeld()` and the player isn't Playing, so a start from a pause, a stop or a wait waits and a track following one playing doesn't; `stepStatsHold()`, before `stepPlayGate()`, releases the wait once the chunks are given back, or leaves it to PlayGate when the headphones are then what it waits for; PlayGate leaves a wait the stats hold alone meanwhile.) (As built, R6 found: the scan's first piece reserves 128 slots, `PlayHistory::kScanSlotsPerPass` through `ListeningStats::mayScan()` and `Fold::reserve()`, and 0 + 128 records pass the object's 96, so every boot's scan carves one overflow chunk (16,384 B at the top) at its first piece, whatever the log holds, and gives it back at the fold's end: `overflowHeld()` is true for the whole scan, and a start waits during every boot's scan, for as long as it lasts (0.1-0.2 s a usual boot), not once a card as the owner's default (1) has it. The model finds the chunk costs nothing (the rework's figures include it), and letting the start through instead nothing with the device's decoders (above). Left as built: the owner's call, to reserve only what the log holds or keep the hold.) (As built, the rework review: the firmware's reserve asks no look-ahead (`statsglue::kReserveAhead`, 0): the fold carves a chunk itself at the 97th record, in the same piece of a pass that may carve, so only a fold past the records holds chunks, a full log's, once a card, as the owner's default (1) has it, and a usual boot's start isn't held. The hold moved into lib/core's PlayGate (`In::statsHold`, `PlayHistory::overflowHeld()`; `stepStatsHold()` and `statsHeldStart` gone): a wait for the headphones begins as ever, its burst overlapping the hold, and PlayGate releases it once the hold is over, with its line and "Now playing on"; a wait on the speaker, or with the headphones connected, is the stats' alone (`Do::Held`, once), started when they let go (`Do::Start`); nothing is released, given up or cancelled meanwhile but by the listener's cancel. What the listener sees: the play button's spinner; Now Playing's "Waiting for SPYDRONE" strip, its [Play on speaker] and [Cancel], and the Library's, Queue's and Auto DJ's "Waiting for" toasts only for a wait for the headphones (`AppState::headphonesWait()`: Waiting, Bluetooth the output, them not connected). A card that goes away mid-scan, which stops the scan until a remount (writes off: until a restart), ends the fold at once as a partial scan's, its blocks given back (`Glue::scanAway()`), so nothing waits on them for the session.)

**The saves: streaming merges.** A save reads the card's `stats.bin` and merges it with the records (and, at a boot, the overflow); nothing waits in RAM but the records.
- **When.**
  - **The boot's**, in `finishScan()` before the trim, as now (5.5), when the fold is dirty: it took new slots, or the gap raised MARK (`StatsFile.cpp:687-690` returns `Saved::Nothing` otherwise; statsref's `new` is the same, the gap included). A compaction due with nothing new waits for a boot with something new, as statsref's does (the design had said "or a compaction is due", which neither does).
  - **The records'** (new, `Kind::Records`). **Due** once the records number 64 or more, whether the boot's fold or a live slot brought them there (one trigger, the same in statsref: the review found the design's two wordings saved 70 before a boot's first live slot in the firmware and 71 after it in the reference). It runs at the first pass the play log's write gate lets through while it is due, before that pass's slot (`turnOf()`: the save, then the slot), so it holds exactly the records taken by then. It waits while a library update's fence is up and while the card is away; it is refused while the orderly end flushes (the flush's 3 s are the plays'; its plays only take records). One that doesn't land is tried again at the pass after each later live record. While it waits for a fence the slots go on (the Tracker's backlog, up to its 32 when a run of tracks of 7 s or less kept the gate shut, and the plays that end in the fence's 13-30 s): **when the records reach 96 with the save still waiting for the fence, it runs then, whatever the fence**, as the rotation's does (a carve and a free in one call; in the model a save in update 0's fence, before or after its build, leaves every figure as it was). The session is **behind** (`Refused::Behind`: no more records, no save, `Hs`'s too; the next boot folds the log above the card's MARK, which the trim keeps) only at 96 records after a save that was asked failed or was refused (the card away, a changed `stats.bin`, the end): a fence alone never makes it (the review: the design's "32 records cover its 13-30 s" didn't hold with the backlog written during the fence, 64 + 31 and two skips).
  - `Hs`, as now (the plays waiting for their slots first: `turnOf()`).
  - **The rotation's**, as now (5.5): it saves only when `plays.1.jnl` holds plays the card's MARK doesn't cover, which now needs a boot whose save didn't land or a session whose saves fail; it runs whatever the fence; in the end's flush only with 1 s of its budget left, as now.
  - **Not at the orderly end** (the records' save refused while ending; at most 95 records wait for the next boot, 63 and the Tracker's 32, inside its records), and **not at a library update**: no give-back, no save first.
- **What it reads and writes** (`statsfile::Store::save`; 5.5's commit kept: the held tmp promoted first, `settle()`, the card's copy checked against the one RAM stands on, the tmp, its size, `replace()`):
  1. The records sorted in place for each section's key in turn: by hour, by day, by (day, hash), by hash.
  2. **Whether a compaction is due** (the boot's save alone, 5.3) is decided on the **merged** TRKD's row count, as statsref's `len(st.trkd)` of its merged aggregate and as built `p.rows[kTrkd]`: a counting merge of the card's TRKD with the records' distinct hashes that have a play or a skip (a slot that is neither makes no row), made first. (The review: counting the records beside the card's rows overcounts every record whose hash has a row already: 1,300 rows and 100 plays of tracks among them merge to 1,300 rows, 31,200 B, not due; counted, 1,400, 33,600 B, due, and the bytes differ from the reference's.) When due: the base, the card's TRKD and the records merged, three sorted streams, into `stats-<gen+1>.bin`; the new `stats.bin` then has an empty TRKD. (As built, R4: that counting merge is step 3's own count of TRKD, so the compaction runs between the counting pass and the writing pass; the card's TRKD and the records merge first, then with the base, the older side first on equal hashes. A read the card fails in it, or rows whose CRC or order fail, the old base's or `stats.bin`'s, are a compaction that failed: the new base removed, the save goes on uncompacted, and the writing pass reads TRKD again.)
  3. A counting pass: the card's HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD read in order, each section's CRC taken as it streams, merged with the records by ⊕ (5.2), counting the rows each keeps after its cut: HOUR's newest 840, DAYS's newest 400, RCNT's 32 days (from the earlier of today and the newest dated day) and then its 4,096 rows (a day goes whole while the rows from it on number more than 4,096: the count alone decides it). (As built, R4: HEAD's and MARK's CRCs are taken in this pass too, though RAM writes them, so a damaged one is seen as any section's; HOUR's and DAYS' cuts are the first rows of the merge dropped in the writing pass, RCNT's the first day kept; when RCNT's count from its 32 days passes 4,096, RCNT is merged a second time to find the first day from which at most 4,096 rows are left (rare: more than 128 tracks a day for 32 days).)
  4. The writing pass: HEAD and MARK from RAM, then HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD merged again from the card (their CRCs again) and the records, through the `ContainerWriter` in 4 KB pieces, each section's size known from step 3. (As built, R4: a section whose merge gives other than step 3's count is a save that failed, step "rows", the tmp removed, never a file whose sections run into each other.)
  5. What it comes to:
     - **Landed** (`Saved::Wrote`): the records emptied, any overflow given back; MARK in RAM, the card's, is the plan's.
     - **Held** (`replace()` failed, the tmp whole and kept): the tmp holds every record, so the records are emptied and any overflow given back, and RAM's HEAD takes the plan's (its baseGen, baseRows, baseCrc and round), as at a landing; but the card's MARK in RAM (what `covered()` answers by) stays the one before until the promotion is seen, which only keeps more of the log. The held tmp counts as something new: the next save of any kind (the records', the rotation's, `Hs`) promotes it first (`Store::promote()`, its own call, as now) and merges only the records taken since, onto the held copy: `Held::Promoted`, or `Held::Gone` with the card's header the held copy's. `Held::Gone` with the card's header the copy before (the tmp lost: a PC, another player's `settle()`), or a tmp no longer ours: the session is behind and nothing is merged; the log above the card's MARK keeps those slots for the next boot. (As built, R4: `Store::promote()` settles the gone case itself, its answers `Held::Promoted` (the card's MARK then the held copy's: `Fold::promoted()`), `Behind` (lost, or no longer ours: `Fold::fallBehind()`, the save `Saved::Behind`, step "held tmp lost"), `Changed` (gone with another copy there) or `Fault`; the held tmp is something new, so it is promoted whether or not records came since, and with none the save is `Saved::Nothing`.) (The review: as built, Held left the fold dirty and RAM's whole aggregate was written over the promoted copy, which was harmless; merging the records again onto the held copy would count each of them twice in HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD, HEAD once, under a MARK that covers them, so no boot would mend it; and after a held compaction RAM's HEAD would still name the old base, so the next save would write a `stats.bin` naming it with a TRKD of only the newer records, the old delta left in a `stats-<gen+1>.bin` the next boot removes as named by nothing.)
     - **Didn't land** (a step failed, a read the card failed in either pass): the tmp removed, tried again as above; a card fault is never a lost row.
     - **A section whose CRC fails with the header unchanged** (the card's copy damaged under the session, not a read the card failed): the session's saves stop at once (`Refused::Behind`, `H` naming the section), not tried again at every record. The next boot reads the file as Corrupt (5.4, unchanged) and renames it `stats.bad`: HEAD's totals, HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD are lost to the stats, which start again from what the log still holds. As built, RAM's copy healed it at the next save (only the header was compared). A cost of not keeping the data (13). (As built, R4: the store's `Saved::Damaged`, `SaveReport::section` the section's type (0: `stats.bin`'s frame failing under the header RAM stands on), the Fold behind, the tmp removed in either pass; every later save `Saved::Behind`. A read the card fails in either pass is `Saved::Failed`, step "read", tried again; the glue maps both to `Refused::Behind` and the retry, R5.)
- **Its work memory** (the review): `statsfile::kWorkBytes` as now, 10,896 B on the device as on the host (`xtensa-esp32-elf` `sizeof(Scratch)` 2,696 B: the writer, the plan, a `Container`, a `Stream`), with a second `Stream` for the compaction's third input and the buffers as 4 KB for the writer and two of 2 KB for the reads; **plus a `FatFile` for each file open**, which `FatFs::open()` carves with `psramNew<FatFile>` (`src/storage/CardFat.cpp:115, 132`): 4,148 B, a FIL with its own 4,096 B window (`CONFIG_FATFS_SECTOR_4096`, `CONFIG_FATFS_PER_FILE_CACHE`; Auto DJ's "the file 4148 B"). A save reads `stats.bin` while it writes `stats.tmp`, two open, which as built it never had; a compaction three (the base, `stats.bin`'s TRKD, the new base). So **a save carves 19,192 B (10,896 + 2 × 4,148) in three blocks and a compaction about 23.4 KB in four**, each given back in the same call; in the model they leave no trace. (R4 may instead keep the FatFiles inside the work memory, opened with `FatFile::openRead()` on a caller-owned `FatFile`, `src/storage/CardFat.h:80-85`, through a `tagstore::Fs` call that opens into caller memory: each save then one block.) **As built, R4:** `statsfile::kWorkBytes` is **11,456 B on the device** (`xtensa-esp32-elf-g++`'s `sizeof(Scratch)` 3,264 B: the writer 2,112, the plan 144, two `Container`s of 272 B, `stats.bin`'s frame and a base's, two row readers (`CardRows`, a `Stream` each) of 84 B, a cursor of 48 B and the merge's four rows of 24 B) and 11,488 B on the host, then the buffers above (4 KB and two of 2 KB): a save carves 19,752 B in three blocks and a compaction 23,900 B in four, 560 B more than the figures above, which R1's model holds at 10,896 B (R6 runs it with the real figure). The FatFiles stay `FatFs::open()`'s own carves: that option wasn't taken. MEASURED-HOST: a save opens at most two files at once, a compaction over a base three.
- **Card bytes and time** (ESTIMATED; round 2's `Hs` wrote 15,472-15,544 B in 61-131 ms): it reads `stats.bin`'s HOUR to TRKD twice and writes `stats.bin` once. Typical 15-44 KB: 30-90 KB read, about 0.08-0.2 s on the loop. Ceiling about 160 KB (RCNT 49 KB, TRKD 96 KB before the forced compaction): about 320 KB read and 160 KB written, 0.35-0.7 s. A boot's compaction adds the base's read and write, as now (0.8-1.6 s at 19,478 played, only at a safe point). The records' save comes once in 64 plays (about 4 h of music); the boot's as now; a rotation's rarely. (MEASURED-HOST, R4's `test_stats_file`: after a full log's boot, a `stats.bin` of 133,616 B; the next save, compacting it, read 270,420 B of the card: every section in the counting pass, TRKD again in the compaction, HOUR to RCNT again in the writing pass.) (MEASURED since, on the device: a full log's boot save 143,672-143,872 B in 2,487-2,689 ms; the next boot's compaction 1,336-1,442 ms at a 19,074-19,079-row base; the records' saves 91-502 ms: "Its device runs" and "Its device re-run".)
- **The bytes equal `tools/statsref.py`'s, given the same save moments.** Every merge rule is order-free (⊕) and the cuts are the reference's, so a merge of the card's rows with the records writes what the reference's whole aggregate writes, given the same slots folded at each save. The reference keeps its save; its sessions take the firmware's moments (the review: three of them it couldn't express):
  - `RECORDS = 96` and `SAVE_AT = 64` beside the log's constants (and `BOOT_TRIES = 4`).
  - A records' save at the player's first `play` or `hs` step after the records reach 64, whether the boot's fold or a live play brought them there, before that step's own slot or `Hs`; one that doesn't land (failed or refused), tried again at the first such step after a later record, as the glue's `recordsTried_` has it (the rework review: statsref had tried it at every later step, so a save at 96 under a fence that failed was tried again at the next step where the glue went behind; `records-save-fenced-fails` pins the glue's rule). (As built, R2: a `fence`, `unfence`, `update` or `remount` step asks none: the fence may go up in the pass that wrote the 64th slot, which `records-save-fenced-backlog` takes.)
  - `fence` and `unfence` steps (an update whose fence covers the plays between them; `update` stays, a fence over no play): a records' save due while fenced runs at the first step after the unfence, before that step's slot, or, after its slot, at the step that brings the records to 96 under the fence, whatever the fence. `Hs` under a fence is refused (`fenced`, the glue's rule).
  - While ending, a records' save is refused (the flush's plays only take records); the rotation's keeps its 1 s rule.
  - Behind at 96 only after a save asked failed or was refused: the 97th live slot finds the records full, isn't folded, and the session is behind; the records it held then count for nothing (0: no save takes them).
  - A held tmp (`replaceFails` on a save: as built, replace() fails at `stats.bin`'s remove, so `stats.bin` stays the copy before, or with no `stats.bin` at the tmp's rename): the records emptied, HEAD the plan's, the card's MARK the old one until the next save promotes it; the held tmp counts as something new, so the next save of any kind promotes it, then writes only if records came since; the tmp lost before it (`dropTmp`: as built, the sessions' `delete` step of `stats.tmp`), or a tmp whose header isn't the one written: behind. The read settles `stats.tmp` first, as `tagstore::settle()` does: beside `stats.bin` a leftover, removed (a held tmp a reboot finds); alone, promoted when it reads whole, else removed.
  - A boot whose overflow's save doesn't land: its tries (the boot's and three more, below) counted against `saveFails` (as built: true, every try fails; a number n, the first n), after the trim; only a try that failed is made again, and behind after the fourth, or at once for any other outcome (a partial scan's overflow too).
  - A section damaged under the session (`damage`, its first byte flipped): every later save that has something new refused, behind; the next boot's read Corrupt.
  - `RING` and the give-back's state go.

  `test_stats_store` drives the firmware's glue through every session and compares the card byte for byte after each step, as now (the sessions: the build plan's R2).

**The boot's fold.** MARK, the trim and each slot once stay exactly as now (5.5, 5.6): the scan reads the newest file first; a slot above the card's MARK for its (deviceId, epoch) is folded, one inside another file's range counted in `twice`; ACKS's rows above MARK make the gap; MARK in RAM is the highest folded per (deviceId, epoch), and only a save that lands, in `finishScan()` before the trim, writes it; a partial scan saves nothing. A fold that can't finish in what it holds is a fold out of memory, which is never saved (as now): MARK never passes a slot not folded. What changes is where the folded slots wait:
- **The usual boot** (the last session's records, at most 95 of this device while its saves land, and what other players wrote since) folds into the records. The ranges (80 B a MARK row) and ACKS's rows above MARK (112 B each, at most 16) go in the ranges' block, 3,072 B with MARK's 16 rows, carved at the top at the scan's first piece (a pass that may carve: no update asked, the join not Working) and given back at the fold's end. That is the scan's one transient across its passes (9 for 1,028 slots on the device; as built, with one overflow chunk beside it, the first piece's reserve: R6's "As built"); the boot's save then carves its work memory (10,896 B) and its two or three FatFiles in its own call.
- **Past 96 records** (the first stats boot on a card 0.14.0 never synced; a 0.14.0 player's sessions; a session whose saves failed): the rest go into overflow chunks of 16,384 B (819 records, carving 16,900 B), carved at the top as the fold needs them, in passes where the join isn't Working: at most 16 for a full log (13,106 slots), 270,400 B, about 273 KB held with the ranges, about 294 KB for a moment at the boot's save (its work memory and three FatFiles beside the chunks; the ranges dropped first). As built the fold peaked at 396,744 B (rows and indexes). The boot's save sorts each chunk in place and merges them with the card's sections as up to 17 sorted runs (a cursor each, in its work memory).
  - **Landed** (or Held: the tmp holds them): every chunk given back at once; nothing behind.
  - **Didn't land** (a step failed, a read the card failed; as built, R5, also no work memory, or the card away: statsref's sessions know only a step failed, and a card back within the waits mends it): the overflow stays at the top and the save is tried again at later passes the write gate lets through, `tagstore::kBootWaitsMs` apart (100, 300 and 1,000 ms: four tries in all, the take-back's rule as built), the join, an asked library update and a track's start waiting meanwhile (about 1.4 s and the saves); a slot written meanwhile (from a track started before the first chunk) goes in the last chunk's room, and a rotation's save in that window merges the overflow too. Given back when one lands; else after the fourth try, at once if the orderly end begins, the card's `stats.bin` changed or the stats go off: then the session is behind, and the next boot folds again. (The review: as designed, a full log's overflow was given back at the first failure, the session behind; a full log's `plays.jnl` is at its 6,553-slot cap, so its very next append rotates (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:561-563`), the rotation's save refused, and `plays.1.jnl`'s up to 6,553 plays lost to the stats after one transient fault. The tries mend a fault that clears within the boot's waits; one that lasts past them still loses those plays at the first play, where as built RAM kept the fold and the rotation's save could still land: 13's hole, said with its number.)
  - Why not hold it until the first play's rotation, as the review proposed: a track's start waits while the overflow is held (above), so no play would come to rotate. Letting starts through instead puts a decoder under about 270 KB (0-29 steps of 360 in the model), and the join can't place around a full overflow with the Opus open (above), so it would wait up to a track, Auto DJ owing its next pick meanwhile; held until update 0 is asked with the decoders opening in it, the model fails a full log's 12 of 360 steps in the old shape (two FLAC carves failing besides) and 19 in the device's, a 300-slot overflow's 12 and 27. The owner's call (13): fold `plays.1.jnl` again above the card's MARK in windows of 96 records, each with a save, before the rotation deletes it; or hold the overflow until that rotation and let a start through under it.
- **Whether a decoder can open during it:** under the ranges' block, yes: a tap on Play (or the headphones' play key) can come while it is held, about 0.1-0.2 s, and in the model that costs nothing (with PSRAM short, as on a remount, its caps could: its take asks a decoder's room, 5.14). Under the overflow's chunks, no: a track's start waits while they are held (above), since a decoder opening under them costs steps in the device's shape wherever they sit.
- **A remount** before the first slot begins the scan again (the records and the overflow dropped; `stats.bin` read again at the scan's first piece, one read, no waits, HEAD and MARK loaded, MARK's rule above); after the first slot, the live records go on and the next save checks the card's copy first, as now.

**The boot's read** (5.4's verdicts and waits unchanged): `stats.bin` read whole in one pass, each section's CRC and order checked as it streams, HEAD and MARK loaded into the object, HOUR to TRKD not kept; the base read whole as now. A remount's read the same, one read, no waits. No read after a library update. (As built, R4: every section's CRC is judged before any order, as statsref's read: `SectionCrc`, then `Shape` for a HEAD under 128 B, then `Order`. MARK's block is the store's: `statsfile::Store(fs, alloc, release)` carves it through that hook (the firmware's: PSRAM) at the boot's read, the one with a wait hook, when the card's MARK has more than 8 rows (`Fold::markBlockBytes()`: its rows and 8 more), `Opened::marks` and `markBytes` saying so, keeps it for the session and gives it back when the store goes; a carve that fails is `Read::NoMemory`. A remount's read carves nothing: MARK past `markCap()` is `Read::NoMemory`.)

**The screens (S2-S3).** The rank on the card worker already reads the base; it reads `stats.bin`'s RCNT and TRKD with it and takes a copy of the records (at most 1,920 B, carved with its other blocks), in place of S1's RAM snapshot. The hub's week (4.2: HOUR summed by UTC day, DAYS's Auto DJ counts for 7 days) comes from each read and save as a summary of 8 days of 12 B (96 B) inside the state's 1,280 B, the records added when shown. S6's local days need the hours (192 HOUR rows, 1,536 B): they don't fit the block, so S6 takes them from the rank's pass on the worker, or grows the block with R1's case run again and the device's figures. *As decided (2026-11-20, later; call 2, "Lifetime line, no memory"):* no week is held, in the state or anywhere. Each visit's ask reads the 7 days (DAYS ⊕ the records' DAYS rows) into its snapshot (9.7). S3's copy of the records is 2,080 B: 40 × (TRKD 24 + RCNT 12 + DAYS 16), 960 B for TRKD alone in the lean ask. S2 as built copies 1,440 B (TRKD and RCNT).

**What goes and what stays.**
- **Goes:** the give-back and the take-back (`Fold::giveBack()`, `Glue::giveBack()` and `takeBack()`, the re-read after an update and its four tries, `Refused::GivenBack`, the held copy's notes, `GaveBack` and `TookBack`); the ring of 32 (2,560 B); the update's waits for the stats' saves (`Glue::updateWaits()`, and `PlayHistory::slotsQueued()` with it; `statsSaveWaits()` becomes `statsScanHolds()`, above); the stats' hook in `LibraryUpdate::setGiveBack()` and their call in `enterFence()`, and their bytes in `UpdateEnv::alsoFreed`; `mayScan()`'s `givenBack()` wait; RCNT and TRKD chunked in RAM, HOUR's and DAYS's blocks, the spare and `keepSpare()`, `reserveLoad()` and the load's rows, the boot's indexes, RCNT's cap at the fold's end, kChunkBytes; the heap model's give-back shapes; statsref's `RING`, its give-back state and the 9 `update-*` sessions; `H`'s GIVEN BACK line. 5.5's and 13's give-back costs go with them.
- **Stays:** `stats.bin`'s and the base's formats byte for byte (S0's contract); the boot's read and its verdicts; the fold's rules (rounds, ranges, the gap, partial scans, the seq rule C5); the save's guards and commit (the ending flag, the card away, a changed `stats.bin`, the held tmp); compaction's rule (5.3), on the merged count, now of three streams; the rotation's save; `Hs` and `turnOf()`; `Refused::Behind`, with new causes; **`mayScan()`'s wait for an asked update, before the scan's first piece** (the review: it is what keeps the scan's blocks out of an update's memory check and build); `H`, `Hd`, `Hm`, `Ht` (reading the card's sections where they read RAM's tables); the trim by the card's MARK.

**The rotation-loss hole.** Its two give-back causes close (`update-rotation-unsaved`, `update-behind-rotation`). The hole stays for 5.5's other causes (a rotation over plays the card's MARK doesn't cover, no save landing before the restart) and gains these, each where S1 as built kept the plays in RAM and a later save could still have landed:
- **A full log whose boot save fails all four tries**: the session's first play rotates (a full log's `plays.jnl` is at its cap) and loses `plays.1.jnl`'s uncovered plays, up to 6,553. It needs a fault that lasts past the boot's waits (about 1.4 s) and clears before that play's end; a fault that lasts through the rotation loses them as built too. statsref's `boot-overflow-full-rotation` asserts the count.
- **A log that isn't full, whose boot save didn't land with an overflow**: behind; a rotation needs `plays.jnl`'s room in plays, up to 6,553.
- **A session whose saves fail or are refused** until the records reach 96: behind; the uncovered plays of every `plays.1.jnl` a rotation took out, before it (its save failing) or after, are lost: at most the 95 records before, `plays.jnl`'s room after (the rework review: a rotation before behind loses plays too, which S1 as built kept in RAM for a later save; a boot whose save failed with 96 records or fewer, `plays.jnl` near its cap and the first rotation's save failing, is the realistic case).
- **A held tmp lost before the next save promotes it**, or a section damaged under the session (below): behind, the same.
statsref asserts each count exactly (R2; as built: `boot-overflow-full-rotation` 128 at the log's cap 128, its `-retry` twin 0, `records-behind-rotation` 64 at the cap 64, `records-fail-rotations` 77 at the cap 16 (the rework review: every save failing from the first play to the 97th, five rotations before behind); `held-lost` and `boot-overflow-unsaved` go behind with no rotation after, 0; `section-damaged` loses 4, not to a rotation but to the next boot's Corrupt, which counts again from a log a trim had taken 1-4 from). Closing the hole for good means folding the log again above the card's MARK, in windows of 96 records with a save each, before a rotation deletes `plays.1.jnl` (no overflow transient, and no behind): about 100 saves for a full log and a second reader of the log; the owner's call (13).

**What not keeping the data costs** (beside the hole above; 13):
- A section of `stats.bin` damaged after the boot's read can't be healed: every save reads it, so the session's saves stop at its first failure (behind), and the next boot renames the file `stats.bad` and starts again from what the log still holds (HEAD's totals, HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD lost). As built, RAM's copy was written over it at the next save.
- Behind stops the live records and saves for the session: a card that fills or fails mid-session shows fewer plays until the next boot.
- Each save reads `stats.bin` twice (above); the records' save is a new loop pause each 64 plays (13; each 24 as built, 91-225 ms on the device, about 5.3 s when it fails again on a card with one free cluster before #63, 98-191 ms with it: "Its device runs").

**Considered and left out.** HOUR and DAYS resident (13,120 B), the work memory resident (10,896 B) and a delta of 256 rows: each over the 8 KB where both shapes fail steps the control doesn't. A block of 4-6 KB with 128 records (a save each 96 plays): in the device's shape it fails nothing more than 3,584 B, in the old shape two steps more (4 of 360); the owner's choice if fewer saves matter more, once the device re-run has measured 3,584 B. A live overflow chunk when the records fill with saves failing: a block held in a failing session; behind instead. Holding a failed boot's overflow until the rotation, or until a save lands (the review's first answer to the full log's hole): the join can't place around it and the model fails steps (above). Internal RAM: 42 KB the lowest with Bluetooth (at design time; measured since in the play-before-link order, 2026-11-19: 35,916 B on main 89188f4, and on the merged build 42,808 B in silent mode and 50,764 B on a real stream: "Its device runs").

**The review's findings** (2026-11-12; each checked in the code or the model in `rework/r2`, the design revised where it held):
1. A held tmp's records merged twice, and a held compaction's delta lost (integrity): **applied** (step 5's Held, above; R2, R4, R5). Checked: `StatsFile.cpp:779-791` leaves the fold dirty and skips `fold.saved()` on Held; `promoteHeld()` sets `expect_` to the held copy. A tmp no longer ours is handled as a lost one.
2. A full log's overflow given back at the first failure, its first play rotating (integrity): **applied in part.** Checked: `PlayLog.cpp:561-563` rotates at the cap. The overflow now stays through the boot's waits, four tries, and the rotation's save merges it within them; holding it until the rotation was measured and left to the owner (a start would have to go through under it, the join can't place around it, the model fails steps). The hole's text says the one play (above, 13); R2 gains the full log's session with its count.
3. statsref can't express the fence's wait, the end's refusal and the boot's 64-or-more (integrity): **applied** (one trigger in both texts; statsref's `fence`/`unfence`, the end's refusal; R2's new sessions).
4. and 10. Dropping `mayScan()`'s update wait lets the scan's blocks into an update's check and build (integrity, memory): **applied.** Checked: `src/app/ListeningStats.cpp:161-185` gives "nothing is carved while the build runs" as the wait's second reason; in the model the held-through and carved-in-the-fence shapes fail steps main passes (above). The wait stays before the scan's first piece, and the update waits for the scan's blocks once it has begun; not both at once, which would deadlock.
5. The compaction's due test before the merged count exists (integrity): **applied** (step 2; R4's edge at 1,365 and 1,366 merged rows).
6. The boot's save's trigger worded unlike the code and statsref (integrity): **applied** (the gap is new; a compaction due waits for something new).
7. The Tracker's backlog written during a fence reaching 96 (integrity): **applied** (the save at 96 whatever the fence; behind only after a save asked failed or was refused; R2's `records-save-fenced-backlog`). In the model that save, before or after update 0's build, leaves every figure as it was.
8. A section damaged under the session never healed (integrity): **applied** (behind at once, `H` naming it; 13).
9. and 16. A remount's MARK larger than the object and its block (integrity, memory): **applied as 9's rule**: a read with no memory, the stats off for the session (5.4). 16's other wording (the fold out of memory, the trim by the card's MARK) is not taken: with MARK not held whole the trim couldn't answer by the card's MARK, and 5.4 already has the verdict for a read without its memory.
11. The model's decoder carves aren't the device's (memory): **applied** (both shapes run; the device's is the measure; 3,584 B now a budget, not the staircase's edge). Re-run with them, the scan's 10,896 B work memory held across its passes cost 8 steps of 360 in two of five placements, so the scan now holds only its ranges (3,072 B: none in any placement); and a full log's overflow with the Opus opening during it fails 0-29 steps by order and placement, none with the Opus after it, so a track's start waits while overflow chunks are held (above; 13). Checked: `OpusGenerator.cpp:50-52`, `OggOpus.h:217-235`; libFLAC's `stream_decoder.c:1316-1325`, `memory.c:52-58`, `bitreader.c:104-106, 274`; the control's 82,120 B.
12. The model doesn't reproduce round 2 on its own sequence (memory): **applied** (history D in the tables and in R1; the H2 fit withdrawn; the device re-run decides).
13. The spacers' keepFree (memory): **applied** (64 KB, `kJoinMarginBytes`, for every top carve; no figure of the rework's moves; with a full log's overflow and the Opus it moves which steps fail, and keeping nothing is no better).
14. The device re-run's tolerance (memory): **applied** (the class of main's own figure, per row: 16,384 B at the boot's join; 8,192 B from 262,144 to 524,287 B; 4,096 B from 131,072 to 262,143 B: `ModelHeap::largest()`'s rounding and TLSF's).
15. The save's FatFiles left out of its work memory (memory): **applied** (19,192 B a save, about 23.4 KB a compaction; modelled; 10,896 B on both). Checked: `CardFat.cpp:115, 132`, ff.h's `buf[FF_MAX_SS]` with `CONFIG_FATFS_SECTOR_4096` and `CONFIG_FATFS_PER_FILE_CACHE` in the core2 build's sdkconfig.
17. The records' save's stack unchecked (memory): **applied** (R5's commit message and the gate sum `turnOf()` → the records' save → `Store::save`'s counting and writing passes against 2,548 B).
The review's other findings held as written: the block goes to PSRAM through `psramNew`; the 1,280 B state is plausible (`xtensa` sizes); no new internal RAM; P = 19,478 doesn't touch the resident set.

**The build plan** (each commit with its host tests; Git Bash for the tests, PowerShell with MSYSTEM unset for builds; no device until the gate):
- **R1, the yardstick as a test.** `test_autodj_join` gains `test_the_listening_stats_across_updates`: the multi-update model with the device's decoder requests (the Opus's 65,307, 61,440 and 1,276 B in that order; a FLAC's private struct, 8,192 B, then 16,400 and 16,415 B a channel, its sum checked against the control's 82,120 B), 5 steps, H1-H8, K1-K4 and D, a `Dc` after each where the history has it, joined per step against main's run in the same history, the rule per step, and the boot's join equal to main's. Against S1 as built (`StatsRun` with the give-back) it fails on purpose, listing D's step 0, H2's 0, H3's 3-4, H5's 0, H6's 2, K3's 0 and K4's 0 (the device's shape), every figure pinned (the S1 review's precedent: `pio test -e native` exits 1 on that case alone). It also runs, and must fail, the shapes the waits exist for: a full log's overflow held through update 0's build, carved in its fence, and with the Opus opening under it, the scan first. The old decoder shape and the wide set (72 histories) build with `-DSTATS_OLD_DECODERS` and `-DSTATS_WIDE`, outside the native suite (about 75 s a variant): their rule is that every step the stats fail is one main fails with its queue block 2,048 B longer, run beside it, and their figures go in the commit message.
  **As built (R1, MEASURED-HOST).** `libraryUpdates()` takes an update history (`Updates`: the Opus kept from a point on, a FLAC through one update, a `Dc` after each or not, the queue's block longer for the control; no memory check's give-back) and the rework's design as raw blocks (`Rework`: the 3,584 B object at `begin()`'s place; a records' save, 10,896 B and two FatFiles, carved and freed in each fence's pass and after each `Dc`; the scan's blocks at the top through the spacers keeping 64 KB; the boot's save at the scan's end), as `rework/r2/m.cpp` had them, whose figures the case reproduces (S1 as built, the control, the Opus under the overflow). Each history runs six ways: main, the control, S1 as built, and the three shapes the waits exist for. In the suite's build (the device's decoders, the 13 named histories; 68-76 s on this host, `test_autodj_join` 1 min 43 s to 2 min 3 s in all) every joined is pinned, 5 updates of each of the six; the Opus's asks sum to 128,023 B and the FLAC's take 82,072 B of free PSRAM, 48 B under the control's 82,120 B (within its private struct's 128 B class step); the control fails none. S1 as built fails D's step 0, H2's 0, H3's 3-4, H5's 0, H6's 2, K3's 0 and K4's 0, **and the boot's join** (507,892 B against main's 589,812 B: its load's chunks carved in setup), and the case ends in `TEST_FAIL_MESSAGE` listing them: `pio test -e native` exits 1 on that case alone (pio shows `test_autodj_join` ERRORED, 28 Unity cases, 1 failed) until R6, so 9.5's Done-when isn't met meanwhile. The shapes the waits exist for run with the design's own scan blocks (the ranges and 16 chunks of 16,384 B; the boot's save's work memory and three FatFiles at the scan's end), not the work memory with the chunks as the design's run had them for the first two; with the device's decoders they fail 6 of the 65 steps (held through update 0's build: H2, H5, K3, K4 and D at step 0, H3 at 3), 1 (carved in its fence: H6's step 2) and 10 (the Opus under it, the scan first: H2, H5, K3, K4 and D at steps 0 and 1). The variants build with `PLATFORMIO_BUILD_FLAGS` set to `-DSTATS_WIDE`, `-DSTATS_OLD_DECODERS` or both and `PLATFORMIO_BUILD_DIR` a folder of their own under `.pio`, and run the case alone (the file's header has the line): 94-97 s for the old shapes' named histories and 408-443 s for each wide set, each with its build. S1 as built breaks their rule in each, and the boot's join with it: 47 of the 360 steps with the device's decoders (45 beyond the control's 2), 7 of 65 with the old shapes' named histories (5 beyond the control's 2: H3's 3-4, H4's 2, H6's 2, D's 2), 36 of 360 with the old shapes' wide set (32 beyond the control's 6). The shapes the waits exist for, of 360 with the device's decoders and the old: held through the build 34 and 24 (with the old shapes two decoder blocks find no room besides), carved in the fence 25 and 48, the Opus under it 29 and 2; the old shapes' 2 are the control's own (K3's step 2, K4's 4), as the design's run found, so that shape is asked to fail only with the device's decoders.
- **R2, the reference** (`tools/statsref.py`, `tools/card_fixtures.py`, the fixtures): `RECORDS`, `SAVE_AT`, the records' save's one trigger, `fence`/`unfence`, the end's refusal, Held and its promotion, the boot overflow's tries, the new causes of behind, a damaged section; `RING` and the give-back's state gone; `update` inert. Sessions: `records-save` (70 plays: the save before the 65th's slot, a reboot folds 6), `records-save-fails` (fails from the 64th: tried at each play; at 96, behind; a reboot folds every one once), `records-save-fenced` (63 records, a fence over 3 plays, the save with 66 at the step after the unfence), `records-save-fenced-backlog` (64 records, a fence, the backlog and two skips: the save at 96 in the fence, nothing behind), `records-save-ending` (the 64th play written in the end's flush: no save; the next boot folds them once), `boot-unsaved-70` (a boot whose save doesn't land with 70 records: the records' save before the first live slot, holding 70), `records-save-held` (`replace()` fails at a records' save; the next save promotes it and merges only the newer records: no play counted twice), `boot-compaction-held` (the same at a compacting boot's save: `stats.bin` names the new base, its TRKD the newer records, the old delta in the base), `held-lost` (the tmp removed before it is promoted: behind; the next boot folds them once), `records-behind-rotation` (behind, then a rotation over `plays.1.jnl` holding uncovered plays: the count lost, exact), `boot-overflow` (300 unsaved slots: one save, nothing behind), `boot-overflow-retry` (its save fails once and lands at the second try: nothing behind), `boot-overflow-unsaved` (four tries fail: behind, `Hs` refused, the next boot folds them once), `boot-overflow-full-rotation` (a full log, four tries failing, the first play rotating: its lost count, and 0 when the second try lands), `section-damaged` (TRKD's CRC damaged after the boot: the next save refused, behind; the next boot's Corrupt), `update-nothing` (plays, an update, plays: no stats byte moved by it). The 9 `update-*` sessions go. `card_fixtures.py --check` (the regenerated list), `test_statsref`, `test_playlog`.
  **As built (R2).** `tools/statsref.py`: `RECORDS`, `SAVE_AT` and `BOOT_TRIES`; `Session.records` and `behind` (`fall_behind()` drops the records: no save takes them), `Session.live()` refusing the 97th, `Session.save(held=True)` (the bytes to `stats.tmp`; `stats.bin`, the old base, the card's MARK and the base it names kept), and the read's settle of `stats.tmp`; the sessions' save gives its outcome (`wrote`, `nothing`, `held`, `failed`, or the refusal: `off`, `ending`, `budget`, `fenced`, `partial`, `behind`, `changed`), a held tmp is promoted first (`_promote`: promoted, gone with `stats.bin` the held copy's header, else behind or changed), the boot overflow's tries come after the trim, and the records' save runs at `play` and `hs` steps and at 96 under a fence; `RING`, the give-back's state, `giveback`/`takeback` and the 9 `update-*` sessions are gone. 34 sessions, 276 steps: the 15 kept write every file byte for byte as before (`test_stats_store` at S1 still passes the cases that run them), and 19 new: the list above, `boot-overflow-full-rotation` in two (its `-retry` twin is the second try landing), and two that R2's mutation check asked for, `boot-overflow-partial` (a partial scan past the records: no save asked, none made again, behind at once) and `held-promoted-covers` (a held `Hs` with no `stats.bin`, promoted by the next with nothing more to write: its MARK covers a rotation after, no save asked); `records-save-fenced` also has an `Hs` under the fence (refused). 660 card fixture files (663, 120 gone, 117 new); `sessions.json` 238,822 B; `index.json` and the scenarios' files unchanged. Where it departs from the list (and from the bullets under "The bytes equal", amended there): a batch step `plays` (each play's fields in columns, the card stored once after the last; one stored `plays.jnl` per play of these 70-300-play runs would have added 2-3 MB) and the facts each player's step gives (`records` and `behind` after it; `recordsSave`, `rotationSave`, `hsSave`, `bootSave`, `tries`, `held`), which R5's runner reads; `records-save-ending`'s flush writes the 64th and the 65th, so the refusal shows (the save due at the 65th); the 0.14.0 histories of `boot-unsaved-70` and the `boot-overflow*` sessions are their starting files (A's NVS given), not steps; `boot-overflow-full-rotation`'s full log is two files at the cap 128 (256 slots, 128 lost), not 6,553; `records-behind-rotation` runs at the cap 64 (64 lost); `section-damaged` at the cap 4, ACKS trimming 1-4 before the damage (4 lost to the Corrupt boot). `test_statsref` 64 OK (59: the give-back's case replaced by `test_the_records_save`, `test_behind`, `test_held`, `test_the_boot_overflow`, `test_the_read_settles_a_tmp` and `test_the_records_in_a_session`; the take-back's read is the remount's), `test_playlog` 32 OK, `card_fixtures.py --check` 660 files. A mutation check of the new rules (15 changes to `statsref.py`, each alone, the file restored after): all 15 caught by `test_statsref`, 3 of them only once the two added sessions and the fenced `Hs` were there. Until R5, `test_stats_store` fails two cases on purpose, with `test_autodj_join`'s R1 case: `test_every_session` (S1's runner stops at R2's first `plays` step) and `test_an_updates_give_back` (its `update-*` sessions are gone); its other 19 cases pass (21 in all), and `test_stats_fold` (20) and `test_stats_file` (13, the index's boots) do.
- **R3, `lib/core/StatsFold`:** the records and the overflow chunks (through a placement hook, the firmware's at the top), HEAD and MARK in the object, the ranges and ACKS's rows in caller memory, sorted views of the records for each key, the merge cursors, Held's records emptied with the card's MARK kept; Chunked, Capped, Index, the spare, `giveBack()` and the load's rows go. `test_stats_fold` rewritten: the records merged with a reference table equal statsref's rows for every index boot; the full log's peak (the ranges and 16 chunks, at most 273 KB held); the 64/95/96 boundaries; MARK's 16 rows and the extra block; a fold out of memory never saved.
  **As built (R3).** `statsfold::Fold` is the rework's fold. In the object: 96 records of 20 B (the slot's path hash, its time as `timeOf()` gives it, 0 undated, its playedMs, a play, a skip or neither, its source, boot or live), HEAD, and MARK's 16 rows, 28 B each (RAM's seq, the card's, and the one a held tmp holds). It is 2,656 B on the device (`xtensa-esp32-elf-g++`'s `sizeof`: the records 1,920, MARK 448, HEAD and the rest 288) and 2,752 B on the 64-bit host. The fold carves nothing but the boot's overflow: past 96 records, chunks of 16,384 B (819 records) through the allocator hook, at most 16 (96 + 16 × 819 = 13,200, over a full log's 13,106). `reserve()` carves them before each scan piece, a fold carves its own without it, and the fold's end gives back any no record needs. A live slot never carves. With the records full and no chunk's room it is the 97th: not folded, and the session behind (`fallBehind()`: the records dropped, the chunks given back, `covered()` as it was). While a boot's overflow waits for its save, a live slot goes in its last chunk's room. Two blocks are the caller's memory: MARK's block for a card with more than 8 rows (`markBlockBytes(rows)`: those rows and 8 more) and the ranges' block (`bootBytes(acks)`: 80 B a MARK row and 112 B an ACKS row above MARK, 3,072 B with 16 of each). A remount's read whose MARK passes `markCap()` loads none of it (`loadMark()` false, no memory). The save's side:
  - `plan()`: HEAD's round and sinceDay, the generation, MARK sorted; false after a partial scan, out of memory or behind. `markRows()` gives MARK as the file has it.
  - `saved()`; `held()` (the records emptied and the chunks given back, HEAD and the generation the plan's, the card's MARK kept); `promoted()` (the card's MARK the held tmp's).
  - `Fold::Cursor` (48 B on the device): the records in a section's key order, a key's records ⊕'d into one row as the file has it. Each run (the object's records, each chunk) is sorted in place, by time for HOUR and DAYS (one sort serves both), by day then hash for RCNT, by hash for TRKD, and up to 17 runs are merged. (As built, R5: a heap sort, not `std::sort`, whose introsort recurses up to 2 x log2(n) frames of 112 B in the core2 build, 2 KB for a chunk's 819 records, on the loop's stack; MARK's sort too. Equal keys' records ⊕ in any order, so the rows are the same.)
  - `compareRows()` and `addRow()`: each section's order and ⊕ on the file's rows. With the cursor they are what R4's streaming merge needs.
  - `due()`: the records' save is due (64 or more, not behind).

  A partial scan's overflow, or a fold out of memory's, is dropped at the fold's end: no save may take it, so the session is behind (statsref's `boot-overflow-partial`). Where it departs from the bullet above:
  - S1 as built's fold (Chunked, Capped, the indexes, the spare, `giveBack()`, the load's rows) is moved unchanged to `lib/core/StatsFoldS1` as `statsfold::s1::Fold`, not deleted. `StatsFile`, `StatsGlue`, `app/ListeningStats` and their suites (`test_stats_file`, `test_stats_store`, `test_autodj_join`'s S1 run) use it under that name until R4 and R5 move them; only the type's name changed in them. lib/core builds as one library for every native suite and for the firmware, so a fold they couldn't compile against would break every suite. R5 deletes it. (As built, R5: kept until R6. `test_autodj_join`'s S1 as built's run, R1's yardstick, builds on it; nothing else does, and the firmware doesn't link it. Deleted in R6, with that run.)
  - MARK's row is 28 B, not 24: it keeps the held tmp's seq, because statsref's `_promote` gives the card the held copy's MARK, which RAM's has passed once a live play folds after the held save. So MARK's 16 rows take 448 B, and the state's row in the table above has 64 B less: the glue's and the store's own state must fit in 928 B for the 3,584 B `static_assert` (R5).
  - The records sort by time for HOUR and DAYS alike, not by hour and then by day (step 1 above).

  `test_stats_fold` was rewritten (19 cases). What it checks:
  - Every index boot (34; 25 saves, 4 bases, 22 trims, 2 past the records). stats.bin's HEAD and MARK are loaded into the fold and its other sections kept as a reference table. The records merge with it through the cursors: a two-stream merge by `compareRows()` and `addRow()`, statsref's `retain()` after, a compaction on the merged count. The result is byte for byte statsref's.
  - Random sessions against a port of statsref's whole aggregate: 86 saves byte for byte, 10 of them held then promoted, 9 boots past the records.
  - The 64/95/96 boundaries and the 97th live slot; the overflow's chunks at 96, 97, 915, 916, 1,734 and 1,735 records.
  - Held: the card's MARK kept, then the held tmp's once promoted; a held compaction's next TRKD holds the newer rows alone, over the new base. A lost tmp leaves the session behind.
  - An overflow kept through failed tries with a live slot in its room, then landing, or dropped.
  - MARK's 16 rows, the extra block, and a remount's read past it.
  - No memory at each carve: the fold stops and nothing is saved, never a wrong file.
  - The full log: 16 chunks, 262,144 B held, 273,472 B as the device carves them with the 3,072 B ranges' block (MEASURED-HOST: 0.13 µs a slot; the save's merge 27 ms with the test's own reader and writer).

  A mutation check of `StatsFold.cpp` (each change alone, the file restored after): 23 deliberate bugs, all 23 caught by `test_stats_fold`. They were the cursor keeping a key's first record, the live round, HOUR keyed by the day, RCNT taking skips or keyed by the day alone, DAYS' skips, TRKD of plays alone, the runs sorted once for every order, the cursor stopping at a record it doesn't take, `held()` moving or keeping, `promoted()` and `saved()` leaving out their part, sinceDay from a skip, twice in one file, MARK written with the card's seq, `covered()` by RAM's MARK, MARK's room a row too many, `reserve()` one short, the 97th folded into a chunk, a partial scan's or an out-of-memory fold's overflow kept, and the gap ignoring held seqs. Until R4 and R5, `test_stats_file` (13) passes as before, and `test_stats_store` and `test_autodj_join` fail only their cases that fail on purpose (R2's two, R1's one).
- **R4, `lib/core/StatsFile`:** the read (HEAD and MARK loaded, the rest checked as it streams, the base whole), the streaming save (the merged count for the compaction's due test, the counting pass, the writing pass, a three-stream compaction), the frame, a section's CRC failing with the header unchanged told from a card fault. `test_stats_file`: each cut at its edge (HOUR 840 and 841 rows, DAYS 400 and 401, RCNT's 4,096 with a day across it, its 32 days with the clock unset and a day ahead), the compaction's due edge (1,365 merged rows not due, 1,366 due, with records whose hashes have rows already), a CRC or a read failing in either pass (the tmp gone, the card's file untouched), `replace()` failing at a records' save and at a compacting boot's save then the save that promotes it (the card byte for byte against statsref's session where that save landed once), the index's boots byte for byte against statsref.
  **As built (R4).** `statsfile::Store` is the rework's store, over `statsfold::Fold`; nothing of it is carved but MARK's block (above, "The boot's read"), everything else in the caller's work memory. What it is:
  - The read: `settle()`, then `stats.bin` read whole in one pass (`Container::open`, then each section streamed, its CRC taken; HEAD and MARK into the Fold, HOUR to TRKD only checked by `statsfold::compareRows()`), the base read whole (its CRC, order and count), its neighbours removed; the verdicts, waits, `stats.bad` and its chains as C7 built them.
  - `CardRows`: one section's rows front to back by their first minor's prefix, the CRC taken and the order checked as they pass; `end()` gives Ok, Io (the card failed a read), SectionCrc or Order. The save's merges read the card through it, a `Fold::Cursor` beside it: by `compareRows()`, equal keys ⊕'d by `addRow()`, the card's row the older.
  - The save, in this order: the store's refusals (`Off`, `Changed`, a partial scan or a fold out of memory `Off`, a held tmp lost `Behind`, the session behind `Behind`, a held tmp's question failing `Failed`), nothing new `Nothing`, no work memory `NoMemory`; then `settle()`, the card's copy, `prepareTmp()`; `stats.bin`'s frame (its header the one RAM stands on, else `Changed`; a frame that fails under it, damaged); the counting pass; a compaction when asked and due; the writing pass; the tmp's size; `replace()`: `Wrote` (`Fold::saved()`), or `Held` (`Fold::held()`).
  - New: `Saved::Behind` and `Saved::Damaged`; `SaveReport::section` (the damaged one's type) and `rows[]` (the file's rows as written); `Opened::marks` and `markBytes`; `Store::Held` is `None`, `Promoted`, `Behind`, `Changed` or `Fault`; `Store(fs, alloc, release)` and `markBytes()`.
  - S1 as built's store is moved unchanged to `lib/core/StatsFileS1` as `statsfile::s1::Store`, with its own `Saved`, `SaveReport`, `Scratch` and `kWorkBytes` (the names, the frame, `StatsCheck`, `Read`, `Opened` and `SaveAsk` are shared, `using`'d into it): `StatsGlue`, `app/ListeningStats`, `test_stats_store` and `test_autodj_join`'s S1 run name it so, as R3 did with the fold. R5 deletes it. (As built, R5: kept until R6, for `test_autodj_join`'s S1 run alone, as the fold; the firmware doesn't link it. Deleted in R6.)

  `test_stats_file` was rewritten (19 cases). What it checks:
  - Every index boot (34): 25 saves and 4 bases byte for byte, 2 neighbours removed, 1 `stats.bad`, 22 trims, 2 boots past the records (the overflow's chunks merged as runs); at most 3 files open at once.
  - Every session of `sessions.json` the store can run alone: 32 of 34 (the two with a twin's link are left to R5's runner), 250 steps, 179 saves (4 held, 6 behind or damaged). Each save at the moment the session's facts give (the glue's refusals, `ending`, `budget` and `fenced`, aren't asked of the store), each play's slot made from its fields and folded live, the log's files as the journal left them. After each step the card is byte for byte the session's, each save's outcome statsref's, and the records and behind statsref's.
  - `records-save-held` and `boot-compaction-held` against themselves with every save landing: the same card at the end.
  - The edges, byte for byte against the merge in miniature (the cursor, `addRow()`) and a port of statsref's `retain()`: HOUR 839 + 1 and 840 + 1 rows (a held hour, an hour older than every row), DAYS 399 + 1 and 400 + 1; RCNT 4,095 + 1 (4,096 kept), 4,096 + 1 (the oldest day, 50 rows, goes: 4,047), an oldest day of one row with 4,096 from the next day on (that row goes, 4,096 kept), 4,096 and a row there (4,096), one day of 4,097 (none left); RCNT's 32 days with the clock on day 310 (from 271), on day 300 with DAYS a day ahead (from 269, the rows ahead kept) and unset (all). The compaction's due edge over a base of 6,000 rows: 1,365 merged rows (30 records of TRKD's tracks, 65 new) not due, 1,366 due, three files open; a TRKD sharing 10 hashes with the base.
  - A failure in either pass, over three saves (`worked-example`'s, `compaction`'s, the due edge's three streams): each of `stats.bin`'s questions failing once (38, 38 and 66 of them): failed, the tmp gone, `stats.bin` and the base untouched, the same save again giving the whole save's card; in the compaction's read of TRKD, the compaction failed and the save went on (16); at `replace()`'s question, the tmp held (1 each). Each section's first byte flipped in each of the save's reads: `Damaged` naming that section (8, 8 and 5, some in the writing pass), the session behind, no tmp, the card as it was, every later save `Behind`; or the save landed (a read the byte isn't passed in, or the compaction's). A byte flipped on the card: `Damaged`, then the next boot's read `Corrupt`.
  - MARK's block: none for 8 rows; 9 and 40 rows carved once (their rows and 8), 8 new pairs folding into it and a ninth out of memory, a save writing MARK whole from it; a remount's read past 16 rows `NoMemory` with nothing carved; a boot's carve failing `NoMemory`.
  - The C7 cases kept on the new store: `StatsCheck`, a lone major-2 tmp, the boot's reads again, `stats.bad`'s chains, the base check, the card's copy, the commit's steps (Held then promoted, now merging only the newer record onto the held copy; a held tmp lost, no longer ours, promoted by another, or replaced by another copy: `Behind`, `Behind`, `Wrote`, `Changed`), a fold out of memory, a cut at every step of a save three ways, compaction, the writer in the caller's memory (a full log's 16 chunks: nothing carved by the save, 4 KB pieces).

  - The saves: `Kind::Boot`, `Rotation`, `Hs` and `Records` (new); `Kind::GiveBack` and `Refused::GivenBack` gone. Each save is `refusal()` then `commit()` or `refuse()`, called from the save's own frame (one frame fewer under the store's save). The order: off; ending (the boot's and the records' in the end's flush); budget (a rotation's there with under 1 s left); the card away; partial; behind (the Fold's: the 97th record, a boot overflow's save not landing, a held tmp lost, a section damaged). Hs: fenced, off, booting (the boot's scan, or its overflow's tries), partial, behind, the card away. The store's `Saved::Behind` and `Saved::Damaged` are `Refused::Behind` with the report kept, so the line and `H` name the damaged section. The report is made straight in the glue's `last_` (no copy of it on `commit()`'s frame).

  - The overflow's tries (`retryDue()`, `retry()`): after the boot's try in `finishScan()`, records past 96 whose save didn't land (failed, no work memory, the card away) are tried again `tagstore::kBootWaitsMs` apart, `kBootTries` (4) tries in all, as Kind::Boot (a compaction allowed); after the fourth, a refusal, or the end's flush during the waits, the session is behind and the chunks go.

  The loop's stack (`-fstack-usage`, the core2 build's flags; FatFs's own frames not counted, as before): the records' save, `PlayHistory::loop` 192 → `ListeningStats::runSave` 32 → `Glue::saveNow` 32 → `recordsSave` 32 → `commit` 48 → `Store::save` 304 → `replace` 64 → `toTwin` 144 → `FatFs::rename` 640: 1,488 B (its sort, `Store::save` → `write` 144 → `pass` 96 → the cursor 32 → `sortRuns` 112 → the heap's `__adjust_heap` 80 (`__pop_heap` and `__push_heap` inlined in it) → the comparator 32: 1,136 B, which said 1,264 B until the rework's gate: the 64 B `__pop_heap` and 48 B `__push_heap` frames it had counted are TagStore's compaction's heap's, and the 96 B `__adjust_heap` is MARK's sort's, `Store::save` → `Fold::plan` 192 → `sortMark` 96 → 96: 1,024 B; its merge's read, `pass` → `Merge::next`, `CardRows::next`, `Stream::read`, `ReadWatch::read` 32 each → `FatFile::read` 48: 1,056 B). A boot overflow's try the same 1,488 B (its compaction, `compact` 240 → `FatFs::openRead` 48 → 32 → `FatFile::openRead` 336, is shallower than `replace()`'s path). The rotation's save, `PlayHistory::loop` 192 → `writeOne` 320 → `append`, `put`, `place` 32 each → `place`'s body 384 → `rotate` 240 → `Glue::save` 32 → `commit` 48 → `Store::save` 304 → `replace` 64 → `toTwin` 144 → `FatFs::rename` 640: 2,464 B (S1 as built's 2,448 B; 512 B over the rotation's own deepest, 1,952 B: 11's budget, at its edge), 2,736 B in the end's flush. The boot's save, `loop` 192 → `scan` 192 → `finishScan` 80 → `save` 32 → `commit` 48 → `Store::save` 304 → 848: 1,696 B. Hs 1,456 B. A remount's read, `loop` 192 → `mayScan` 32 → `reserve` 32 → `settle` 96 → `readNow` 32 → `Store::read` 256 → `promoteHeld` 80 → `replace` → `toTwin` → `rename`: 1,568 B. The save's line, `commit` → `ListeningStats::saved` 32 → `logSaved` 240 → printf: 1,584 B before printf's own from the rotation's. From `loop()`'s own 592 B, a records' save is 2,080 B and a rotation's 3,056 B. (The rework review: printf's own is about 1,392 B more, `Print::printf` 80, `vprintf` 144, `vsnprintf` 64, `_vsnprintf_r` 144, `_svfprintf_r` 800 and `_dtoa_r` 160 for the ms, so that line under a rotation's save, about 2,976 B from `PlayHistory::loop`, was the stats' deepest, past the rotation's own deepest, its `onOp` printf at about 2,592 B, not the rename's 1,952 B. A rotation's line is now owed to the next pass, `PlayHistory::loop` 192 → `owedLine` 32 → `logSaved` 240 → printf: 1,856 B; the records' save's line 2,000 B and the boot's 2,208 B before `_dtoa_r`'s callees; so the deepest stays the rotation's save's rename, 2,464 B, under the rotation's own.) Before the heap sort, `std::sort`'s introsort was 112 B a level, up to 2 x log2(n) levels: about 1.5 KB for 96 records and 2 KB for a chunk under `Store::save`'s pass.

Each history runs fifteen ways: main; the control (main's queue 2,048 B longer); main with a start at the boot opening the Opus before the boot's join (the order's own cost: the boot's join 466,932 B in the histories with the Opus at the boot, against 589,812 B); the rework in eight ways, a usual boot (70 records) with the scan first or the join first, a records' save at 96 in update 0's fence before or after its build, a full log (16 chunks) with the scan first or the join first, its boot's save failing all four tries (behind), and the scan after update 0 (asked before its first piece), each with the records' save of 64 plays in every fence's pass and after every update; and four shapes with a wait left out. The rule is R1's, the boot's join against main's with the Opus first where a start opened it before the boot's join. The rework's eight ways give the same figures in every history of every set: the suite's build (the device's decoders, 13 histories) 0 of 65 failing, mean -1,890 B, worst -110,592 B (H5's step 4, main's own noise), the boot's join main's 589,812 B; the wide set 0 of 360, mean -933 B, worst -110,592 B (the design's 3,584 B block alone, above); with the old shapes 2 of 65 and 2 of 360, the control's own (wide: mean -603 B, worst -90,112 B, the design's). The control fails 0, 2, 2 and 6 (dev named, old named, dev wide, old wide); main with the Opus first 0 in each. (The rework review: a start asked during a scan that comes first opens the Opus before the boot's join, not after it as R6's runs had it: the chunks given back by the boot's save, main.cpp's `stepPlayGate()` starts it in the next pass before `stepAutoDj()` begins the join. Neither group fails a step beyond the control's.
