**If 3a-4 fails** (a held snapshot moves a figure that main's doesn't), the fallback must remove what it failed on: a long-lived carve made while the snapshot is held. That is a decoder's open (a track's start, a skip, the gapless next's open, a Play from a shelf's bar) or Auto DJ's join (`Dc`, or "Auto DJ from here" on a shelf's sheet). So the fallback, still under A, frees the snapshot before each of them: `statsRelease()` from the player's open and the join's begin. The page it was on is attached again at 0 rows with Try again, and nothing asks by itself. It is checked by its own run: 3a-4's script with the fallback built, against main, by 3a-4's rule. The per-page hold (freed at each `leave()`, asked at each `enter()`) is not a fallback by itself: a shelf's Play still opens a decoder under it, so it is offered only with a 3a-4 run of its own. The figures and the choice go to the owner.

*As run (2026-11-21):* 3a-4 failed, and the figures and the fallback go to the owner ("S3a: the device pass", below: its call 1). The fallback is in the heap model only. The firmware doesn't have it, and its own run is owed if it is chosen.

**As decided (2026-11-21): the snapshot in the Dance tab's block.** The owner chose another hold, "Borrow Dance's block" (section 1): the snapshot is lent the Dance tab's 4,096 B tracker scratch, which `DanceMode` carves at the boot in every build, main's included, and keeps for the session, idle while the tab is down. Nothing is carved for the snapshot, so it can't shift a carve made while it is held, and neither the fallback nor the moves accepted is built. The heap model's borrowed way (V9) gives the visits alone's figures to the byte in every history and build, and run 3a-4 is owed again on the device (the run table). Its costs: the stats are tied to the Dance module (a `static_assert` holds the scratch to `kBound`, and the loan is keyed on the block, not on the dancer being ready); opening the Dance tab drops the lists, which recount when a stats page shows again; a console ask while the tab is up is refused; and a library past 18,432 albums gets no snapshot, where a larger block would change main's PSRAM from the boot.
- **Free + recount** (the fallback above, plus a recount by itself after each track's open or Auto DJ's join) was the owner's first pick that day, and is not built: "Borrow Dance's block" replaced it, since it gives main's PSRAM with no free hooks in playback, the queue, Auto DJ or the fence and no blank page. Its costs as its read-only spec found them (at 4540adc; INFERRED where not run): six free points (the decoder's request, a Play's queue replace, the gapless next's early open behind a held word, Auto DJ's join begin, its index file's open and its root's first carve); a fence-order blocker (`queueStore.remapBegin()` runs before `lists->drop()` in `enterFence()`, `src/main.cpp:3464`, `:3480` at V10); carves outside the decision left unhooked (the queue's growth, its save buffer, Waves' job); and a blank page for about 3.4-4.4 s after each start, skip or Play, about 5-6 s around a gapless change and longer after a join (the waits and the capped hold).
- **Also considered** (a ranking of the advisors' options, read-only): a static 4 KB in RTC slow memory (the same PSRAM result, but 4 KB of new resident memory, the letter of what "A: nothing held" ruled out, and its speed unmeasured); internal RAM while a page shows (about half of G5's 7.5 KB margin); the in-heap placements (worse in the model: "A top placement", below).

*As built (S3a, 2026-11-21; MODELLED: 3250036, `StatsRun::page.fallback`).* The fallback is in the heap model at 3,860 and 4,096 B: the snapshot is freed from the model's decoder opens (`carveInto()`: a FLAC's, an Opus's) and the join's begin (`joinNow()`: the `Dc`), and isn't carved again until the next visit. Every build asserts that:
- every figure (the boot's join and the five updates') is the visits alone's to the byte;
- the visits' grades are the same;
- each kept snapshot is freed once, none out of the largest free block, and no ask leaves a trace;
- the 4,096 B way's frees and holds are the 3,860 B way's.

Its cost is how often a page would lose its lists:

| Build | Snapshots kept | Freed by the fallback (FLAC / Opus / join) | Histories it fired in | A history, mean (most) | Kept to the fence or the end | Points held, mean (A4's) | Held at its step's figure (A4's) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Suite (13 histories) | 68 | 39 (3 / 0 / 36) | 11 | 3.00 (5) | 29 | 1.19 (3.46) | 32 (68) |
| Wide (72) | 351 | 192 (28 / 24 / 140) | 70 | 2.67 (6) | 159 | 1.41 (3.44) | 187 (354; 355 at 4,096 B) |
| Old decoders (13) | 72 | 42 (3 / 0 / 39) | 11 | 3.23 (5) | 30 | 1.18 (3.47) | 33 (72) |

A point is one the model lets a snapshot live through: each decoder's open, the FLAC's end, the `Dc`, each records' save, and the step's figure. Where a `Dc` follows each update (H2-H4, H6-H8, K1, K4, D), every update's snapshot goes at that `Dc`. The boot's visit's, carved after the boot's join, is kept to update 0's fence in all of them but H6, whose FLAC opens before that fence and frees it. Only the histories with no `Dc` keep an update's snapshot to the next fence: H1 and H5 lose none, K2 and K3 one each, at a FLAC's open. So the suite's 29 kept to the fence or the end are those four histories' 21 (H1 6, H5 6, K2 5, K3 4) and the boot's visit's in the other 8.

**Why A4 moves figures** (traced through the model heap, printed): every lasting move goes through a long-lived carve made while the snapshot is held, which are the carves the fallback frees it before.
- **3,860 B, D's update 4** (262,132 B against 270,324 B). The snapshot's 3,904 B block comes out of a 10,216 B free block that lies on top of the rejoin's region.
  - At the `Dc`, with nothing held, that region and the block merge into 364,536 B. With the snapshot there, the merge stops under it at 354,316 B, reported 352,244 B, a class under.
  - The `Dc` sizes its spacer from the report: 217,024 B against 225,216 B. Its blocks sit 8,192 B lower.
  - The free block under them, the largest at the figure, is 268,296 B against 276,488 B. That is under its class floor (270,336 B), so it is reported a class less.
- **3,860 B, the worst wide step** ("opus 2 flac 2 dc 0", update 4: 249,844 B against 270,324 B).
  - Update 2's FLAC opens before its fence with update 1's snapshot held, and its 6,000 B struct lands 3,908 B higher.
  - Update 2's visit then sees a largest free block 3,908 B smaller (94,244 B against 98,152 B), its take asks another spacer, and the runs place blocks differently from there.
  - At update 4's Live, Thumbs' 110,592 B pool lands at the bottom of what is, with nothing held, the largest free block.
- **4,096 B, the worst wide step of all** ("opus 3 flac 3 dc 1", update 4: 249,844 B against 327,668 B).
  - The same FLAC shift comes first.
  - Then update 3's Opus opens with the snapshot held. Its 65,307 B buffer, kept for the session, goes to a 71,688 B free block high in PSRAM; with nothing held it comes out of the 364,536 B largest.
  - Update 4's build then puts its 64 KB string chunks high, and the largest falls from 334,348 B to 251,892 B.
- **Old decoders.** 3,860 B moves no figure, and 4,096 B moves 6 of 78. Traced, "opus 3 flac 3 dc 1" moves by 4,096 B through free alone: at the `Dc` free is 4,228 B less, so the margin caps the spacer 4,096 B shorter.
- **In every trace** the first carve to land elsewhere is the records' save's 11,488 B work memory, in the snapshot's hole and given back in its call. It leaves no lasting difference.

**A top placement** (printed only, not the rule's) is worse. Here the snapshot is carved through the hold as the grades are, under the spacers with caps closing the block above it.

| Way | Suite moved | Suite failing | Wide moved | Wide failing | Old decoders moved | Old decoders failing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Top, 3,860 B | 14 of 78 (all under, at most −8,192 B) | 13 of 65 | 42 of 432 (33 under, at most −20,480 B) | 28 of 360 | 15 of 78 | 15 |
| Top, 4,096 B | 22 of 78 | 15 | 84 of 432 (72 under, at most −86,016 B) | 45 (44 not the control's) | 25 of 78 | 15 |

On the top the snapshot comes out of the largest free block and lowers it at once. In the suite at 3,860 B it landed on the top 13 times, in a hole 63 times, and was refused twice; wide, 31, 379 and 22.

**S3a: effort.** 5-6.5 (a guess; the checkers' round added the played-set-only rank, the heap sort, `phaseOf()`, the Rank lane's waits in the gate and the cap):
- V1-V3: 1.75-2.25;
- V4: 1.25-1.75;
- V5: 0.75-1;
- the review: 0.25;
- the device runs: 1-1.25.

**As built (S3a, 2026-11-21)**, on `claude/listening-stats-s3` from main de4f52f. Not pushed. Not on a device when written; device-checked since ("S3a: the device pass", below, with its fix b16a189). The commits, each with its host tests:
- **V1** 700ce6d `lib/core/StatsRank`, with `tools/statsref.py`'s `totals()`, `days7()` and `played40()`, three new rank scenarios and `stats/rank.json`.
- **V2** b07da2a `lib/core/StatsMap`.
- **V3** f7cd731 `lib/core/StatsVisit` and `StatsFold`'s two getters; 9f8c224, the check's fixes.
- **V4** dbd3283 `ScanScheduler::yieldNow()`; 443b755, the u40 set's one-way error said to hold only while the set lost no hash; 47543bb, the firmware (`src/app/StatsLists`, `ListeningStats`, `CardTasks`, `PlayHistory`, `main.cpp`); 6ea01b0, the two checkers' fixes.
- **V5** 09ac8dd, the heap model's S3 half; fc5bf26, the check's fixes; 3250036, the 3a-4 fallback in the model, A4's moves traced, and a top placement for comparison.
- **V6** 93d38f6 "Code: what the review found"; cf72100, the check of its fixes.
- **V7** these docs: ARCHITECTURE's Listening stats bullet and diagram, the README's console table and Layout, and this plan's "As built" notes (section 1, 4.4, 6.5, 6.8, 7.10, 9.3, 9.7, 11, 13).
- **The device pass's fix:** b16a189 `statsvisit::elapsedMs()` ("S3a: the device pass", below).
- **The owner's calls** (2026-11-21, later): V8 a4853d1, V9 ec3f13c, V8's review 747a368, and V10 these docs ("S3a: the owner's calls as built", below).

The tables' and the API's departures are in the notes beside them: the snapshot, the grades, the fit, the API, the phase, `.bss`, the snapshot's life, the lit hold, the tests, Done when, and "If 3a-4 fails". The rest, by commit:

**V1:**
- DAYS is read before RCNT, and both are read with the clock unset too (6.8's "As built").
- `Report::recordRows` counts the records' rows read: TRKD's, RCNT's and DAYS'. `Report::daysRows` counts DAYS'.
- Each day's field saturates at its DAYS row's width, as a save's ⊕ does: plays at 65,535, Auto DJ's plays and skips at 255, listenedMs at a u32's top. `statsrank::Day` is 12 B in the report (84 B for the 7 days), packed to 10 B (70 B) in the snapshot.
- `Played40::at(i)` is the 40-bit key.

**V2:**
- `seal()` sifts by hand over the chunked rows; they have no random-access iterator for `std::make_heap`. Its sift-down stops at the held row's place, where libstdc++'s goes on to a leaf and back up. After the dedup the sealed set equals the insertion sort's byte for byte.
- `Wanted::region()` and `bytesFor()` sit beside `rowsCap`.
- A `begin(index, nullptr, ...)` overload: an untyped null was ambiguous between the two sets.
- On the host, 696 rows take 279 µs against the insertion sort's 2,476 µs.

**V3:**
- `write(ranker, wanted, report, ctx, snap, bytes)`. A `statsvisit::Context` carries what isn't the rank's: the key, the albums, the ask's time, `sinceDay`, the grade, Most played's window (a re-ask passes the held one's), the version and the live round. `bytes` is the carved block's, and `write()` refuses, writing nothing, when `bytesFor(albums)` is more.
- `notPlayed(map, index, snap)` checks the index against the map and the snapshot's key. `albumAt(k)` gives the album's place in `albumsAZ()`, not its id: the page maps it.
- `layout(grade, head, rows, foldersBytes, albums, dated)`.
- `Fold::sinceDayNow(nowMs = 0)`. With the board's clock it is the day the first save would set (the earliest dated play's, at most today, else today). Without, it is HEAD's sinceDay, else the earliest dated play's day.

**V4:**
- `sizeof(StatsLists::Ask)` is 1,600 B (the grades' note).
- `CardTasks` hands over `yieldNow()` after the turn's pass. This pass's input reaches the turn through `Gates::input`, so a tap's or a console line's own pass never takes. The lane's other waits arrive a pass late, and a take they catch is given back the next pass. `yieldNow()` is `List` while `listHolds()`, else `yieldOf()`: not Step, Updating or Battery.
- **A visit's u40 set that lost a hash** to its room lands with the grade written Lists (no Not played yet). The lean ask's ends `Room` (No room).
- **A library index changed** before the walk, or under it, puts the ask back to be asked again over the new one. After three re-asks it ends Cancelled.
- **A Full visit whose `notPlayed()` is refused** (the snapshot's key not the index's) still writes grade Full, with no bits; its landing's note says why. S3b keys Not played yet on `hasNotPlayed()`, not on `grade() == Full`.
- **The carve's line** says whether the snapshot came out of the largest free block by a heap walk (`statshold::largestFree()`: that block's own range before the carve), beside the reported class floors. (V8 replaced it with the loan's line, and the heap walk went with the carve.)
- **The fence's `[lib]` line** counts the snapshot's bytes, and main.cpp's `alsoFreed` adds `statsHeldBytes()`. (Since V8 both count 0 for it: the snapshot is lent.)
- **Each change of the pages' phase** says a line: "[listen] the stats pages' phase: <phase> (version N, at T ms): …".
- **The image** was 24,432 B over main's after V4 (6ea01b0, V1-V4); Done when's note has the final figure.

**V5:**
- The model's head is 1,600 B (`kS3AskHead`, since V6 `statsvisit::kAskHeadBytes`).
- **The yardstick's library** (`synth::userShape(19412)`) has 2,562 folders and 1,785 albums, so its snapshot is 3,872 B, not the spare's 2,478 folders and 1,706 albums. Full and Lean are 848 B over the spare's column with the same rows: 140,224 and 131,872 B at P = 19,478. The reduced room uses the spare's parts.
- **A4's steps** include the visit's take (`Hu`'s ask), as run 3a-4 does. A snapshot is also carved after the boot's join and held to update 0's fence. The S3 asks come right after each Live and rejoin.
- `StatsRun::Page{bytes, Carve::Plain}` is A4's; `Carve::None` is `Hv`'s.

**A4 in the model** (MODELLED; V5, with 3250036's counts):
- **The step rule.** The held snapshot passes it at 512, 3,860 and 4,096 B. In the suite 0 of 65 steps fail (the control 0); wide, 0 of 360 (the control 2); with the old decoders, 0 of 65 (the control 2). It never comes out of the largest free block.
- **The figures.** It moves figures the visits alone don't, out of the boot's join and five updates each history:

  | Snapshot | Suite (78) | Wide (432) | Old decoders (78) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 512 B | 2, both over | 15: 2 under (at most −20,480 B), 13 over | 0 |
  | 3,860 B | 1: D's update 4, 262,132 B against 270,324 B | 12: 3 under (at most −20,480 B), 9 over | 0 |
  | 4,096 B | 3, each −8,192 B | 24: 12 under (at most −77,824 B), 12 over | 6 |

- **The baseline.** The visits alone, with nothing held, give the rework's figures to the byte in all 13 named histories and all 72 wide.
- **The prediction.** By run 3a-4's rule (main's to the byte) the model predicts the run fails where a decoder opens or a `Dc` joins while a snapshot is held. The fallback gives the visits alone's figures everywhere ("If 3a-4 fails", "As built").

**The review** (V6, 93d38f6: six lenses, 13 findings, each checked; none rejected, 12 fixed in code, one recorded here):
1. **A row's time, and the phase after a give-back**: a docs finding, recorded in the snapshot's and the phase's notes.
2. **A re-ask's `write()` over a served snapshot** unserved it with no version move. Now the version moves there (the API's note).
3. **The 30 s worker give-up** took the block again in the same pass: take, 30 s queued, give up, take, the block held nearly all the time.
   - Now the give-up sets `kStalled`, and the visit is held in Asked 30 s more, holding nothing, then taken again. There is no count and no end.
   - Its lines: the put-back's "…: taken again 30 s from now", the waits' "the card worker (no step of this ask's in 30 s: taken again 30 s after)", and the take's "the card worker's give-up".
   - `kStalled` is cleared at the take, at a new ask, at the fence's re-ask and by an S2 command.
   - `CardWorker::mayStart()` (the task there, or its spawn due after `kRetryMs`, 5 s) lets `StatsLists::start()` skip the records' copy and the opens while a spawn was refused.
4. **A page's ask behind a console ask** no longer clears the outcome, so a console ask stopped on the card worker keeps its result dropped.
5. **The orderly end** frees the snapshot with no plays pending too (`PlayHistory::flush()` calls `StatsLists::ending(0)`).
6. **`sayTake()`**: every take that ends without landing says its figures (the lit hold's note).
7. **The take line's turn time** leaves out its own console lines: "(stats.bin's directory N us; its lines' N us left out)".
8. **`Hk`** is paced by the TX buffer's room (9.3's note). `Serial.availableForWrite()` gives at most 1,016 B for the 2 KB ring. The parts are 1-3 the header, 4-9 the lists, 10 Not played yet's albums.
9. **`statsvisit::kAskHeadBytes`**, with a `static_assert(sizeof(Ask) == …)` in `StatsLists::begin()` (`Ask` is private), read by the model, which asserts the 1,600 B its pins assume. The table's "moves byte for byte" is now held at build time.
10. **A held snapshot through the memory check** (the tests' note).
11. **The landing's "not counted" note** no longer blames a short set for a refused `notPlayed()`.
12. **`kFake` only when `Hu`'s ask carved the snapshot.** `Hu` over a page's snapshot leaves it the page's (`Hu0`: "isn't Hu's: kept"). A page's visit over `Hu`'s takes it over, and the fence doesn't ask `Hu` again for it.
13. **The key check's comments.** `write()`'s short-block refusal and `finishVisit()`'s NoRoom are backstops: `turnVisit()` carves a short snapshot again, `finishVisit()` checks the albums, and `StatsLists::loop()` checks the key.

**The check of the fixes** (cf72100) found two things, both fixed:
- **A blocker: finding 3's fix livelocked.** `PlayHistory::loop()` calls `lists.loop()` before the turn with the same `nowMs`. In the pass where the stall's 30 s ran out, `loop()` moved the wait's start to that pass, and the turn's gate then held the ask again, for good. Now the start stays the give-up's until the take. The rules moved to `lib/core/StatsVisit.h` (`kStallMs`, `stallHolds()`, `waitStartMoves()`), host-tested (the tests' note).
- **A nit.** `freeSnap()` returns its line's µs, so a short snapshot's re-carve line is left out of the take's turn time too.

The stall has had no device run: it needs a card worker that takes no step for 30 s.

**S3a: the device pass (2026-11-21; the owner's "Yes, device pass"; MEASURED).** The Core2 with the spare card only (`tools/synthcard.py`'s library, seed 1: 19,417 tracks, 1,706 albums, 2,478 folders; its `stats.bin` over the base `stats-00000002.bin`, 19,079 rows, at generation 10 at the pass's first boot and 17 at the lists' check; P = 19,075 played hashes, 19,165 u40 rows), Auto DJ on, silent mode (`z`) after every boot but in run 3a-7. S3a ran at 852d982 (v0.14.0-179-g852d982, ELF f7b7c327) for run 3a-6's first try, then at b16a189 (v0.14.0-180-gb16a189, ELF 9f8fae0e; the fix below) for 3a-6 again and every run after. Main de4f52f's CI build (v0.14.0-163-gde4f52f, ELF e631b311; its image's sha256 checked against the artifact's SHA256SUMS) is the control, in the same scripts with S3a's asks left out. Each image was written at 0x0 alone and checked (its md5, version and ELF) before its runs. NVS was never written, the music card was never in the board, and nothing on the never-sent list was sent. Logs are in the session's scratch folder (`dev/s3a`). The scripts were reviewed before use, and all 32 of the review's findings were applied: a jump to the entry already current prints no "now at", so the scripts wait on the console's echo; "No folders" has a space; the scripts' own swipes print "a touch on the glass", so the check for a person's touch accepts that line only beside "[touch] down ... scripted"; `uiv` wakes only a screen that is Off, so each unswiped lit ask starts from a fresh wake (the swiped asks' `uiv` came without a wait for Off, on a bright or dim screen); each run has one boot; and the G5 runs wait for `out=bt(connected)`.

**The defect the pass found, fixed in b16a189** ("Code: a wait's start ahead of the pass's now (S3a's device pass, run 3a-6)"). Run 3a-6 on 852d982 printed "[listen] the stats pages' phase: working (version 1, at 3929 ms): Hv in Asked, held 4294967294 ms by the boot's scan". A console ask, and `Hu`'s re-ask at a fence, stamps its wait's start (`queuedMs_`) with `millis()` after the loop's pass took its `nowMs`. `loop()`'s held time and `phaseAt()`'s waited time then subtracted a start ahead of now, and the u32 wrapped. For every wait but the boot's scan's, the phase would have read Waiting at once instead of from 3 s. A page's ask comes after `StatsLists::loop()` in the pass, so it doesn't meet it. The fix is `statsvisit::elapsedMs()` (0 for a start ahead, wrap-safe), used by `loop()`, `phaseAt()` and `stallHolds()`, with `test_stats_visit`'s new `test_elapsed_ms_with_a_start_ahead_of_now`. Host: `test_stats_visit` 9 of 9, `test_scan_scheduler` 30 of 30. `pio run -e core2 -e core2-dio`: both build, RAM 61,504 B as before, flash +8 B. On b16a189 the same line reads "held 0 ms".

**3a-1, lit asks (default (a)): past 3 s with the cap off.** 60 asks: idle, with an MP3 (Long Silence's track 1) and with a FLAC (its track 4); each lit, lit and swiped, with the cap on and lit, with the cap on and swiped, and dark; each `Hv` (Full), `Hv1` (Lists forced), `Hn` (Lean) and `Hn1` (No folders forced). Every lit ask was lit at the take, the rank and the walk, with no "[screen] … -> off" between its send and its end. Each ask took once and nothing was given back; with the cap on none was capped, each visit's holds under 3 s. The dark control ran with the cap off only: no dark ask ran with the cap on. Each take's hold, in ms, with the rank's ms in brackets (past 3 s a visit's own summed line, "the visit's holds … ms: past the 3 s cap", reads 45-51 ms more for Full and Lists and 1 ms more for Lean and No folders, up to 6,155 ms):

| Each take's hold, ms (the rank's) | Idle: Full | Lists | Lean | No folders | MP3: Full | Lists | Lean | No folders | FLAC: Full | Lists | Lean | No folders |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lit, the cap off (priority 0) | 3,587 (2,539) | 3,233 | 2,695 | 2,843 | 6,104 (4,478) | 5,268 | 4,438 | 4,648 | 5,037 (3,616) | 4,273 | 3,634 | 3,792 |
| Lit and swiped, the cap off | 3,737 | 3,282 | 2,681 | 2,877 | 6,045 | 5,280 | 4,563 | 4,763 | 4,861 | 4,268 | 3,548 | 3,781 |
| The cap on, lit (priority 1) | 1,672 (862) | 1,407 | 1,207 | 1,281 | 2,370 (1,292) | 1,888 | 1,832 | 1,881 | 1,982 (1,079) | 1,603 | 1,521 | 1,617 |
| The cap on, swiped | 1,669 | 1,415 | 1,207 | 1,280 | 2,379 | 1,900 | 1,826 | 1,894 | 2,006 | 1,590 | 1,535 | 1,605 |
| Dark, the cap off (priority 1, the control) | 1,541 (742) | 1,285 | 1,133 | 1,194 | 2,160 (1,087) | 1,681 | 1,694 | 1,729 | 1,841 (927) | 1,412 | 1,390 | 1,453 |

- **The walk:** lit, against the plan's "up to 4 ms a pass", the slices averaged 4.6-7.4 ms a pass, and the longest pass was 4.9-6.2 ms idle and 9.1-15.5 ms with an MP3 or a FLAC playing (Full: 103-124 passes, the longest 5.7-6.2 ms idle, 11.2-13.8 ms with the MP3 and 10.1-10.3 ms with the FLAC); dark or with the cap on, Full's 42-50 passes, the longest up to 24.9 ms (the MP3, `Hv` with the cap on, lit).
- **The rule's outcome:** lit visits pass 3,000 ms with the cap off: Full and Lists in every state, Lean and No folders with a track playing. By the lit hold's "After run 3a-1", `kLitCap` goes on for S3b, and the figures, with the cap's, go to the owner, who may overrule. With the cap on every hold is under 2.4 s (at most 2,379 ms), 74-219 ms (6.5-13.5 %) over the dark control. That control ran with the cap off. A dark ask ranks at priority 1 and walks at the dark's pace either way, and its holds (at most 2,160 ms) stay under the cap's 3 s, so the comparison stands by that reasoning (INFERRED), not by a dark run with the cap on.
- **The swipes** (`uis160,200,160,60,300` about every 450 ms for 5 s, not the table's 300 ms, the first before the ask): each swiped ask's phase went Working, Waiting, Working, Idle, and its waits said "input (the Rank lane's)", then "a list moving". No block was taken while they went on. The take came 547-641 ms after the last scripted touch when the list didn't move, and 2,297-2,422 ms after it when the last fling set the list gliding (the Rank lane's list-moving wait, then the 0.5 s quiet). No Unread.
- **`[ui] slow pass`:** 3 lines (35.9-37.1 ms), each the wake's redraw frame from `uiv`, before the ask was sent; none from the stats' work. The `[stats] track=` lines (178, `s`'s among them): underruns=0 and breaks=0 throughout.

**3a-2, the loop work: PASS** (3a-1's lines; each piece under 35 ms). The turn is at most 10,350 µs in 3a-1's lines (`stats.bin`'s directory at most 8,920 µs, an MP3 playing), and 20,074 µs the first after a boot (3a-6; its directory 17,774 µs). The hand-off is at most 6,824 µs. `finishRank()` is at most 28,056 µs with an MP3 playing (the seal at most 16,611 µs; 10,648-10,800 µs idle). `resolve()` is mostly 0.6-0.7 ms and at most 5,590 µs (an MP3 playing, `Hv` swiped), and `notPlayed()` at most 1,192 µs. Across the pass the seal reached 16,982 µs (s3a-hv) and `notPlayed()` 2,026 µs (s3a-hv2). The seal takes 279 µs on the host and 10.6-17.0 ms on the device.

**3a-3, the grades after updates: PASS.** After the boot: `Hw` took 286,060 B (it walked 19,417 tracks and matched all 19,075 played hashes; not played yet 1 of 1,706), `Hv` took Full and `Hn` Lean. After each update's rejoin and `Dc` (C1 stopped, C2 with an MP3, C3 with a FLAC), `Hv` took Full (130,800 B) and `Hn` Lean (122,448 B); nothing was refused.
- **The snapshot:** every one carved elsewhere than L, the largest free block equal before and after its carve (557,044, 319,476, 393,204 and 401,396 B).
- **T:** 66,700 B after the boot, and 8,184, 12,284 and 9,884 B after the updates, 2 caps each.
- **`D`:** at each take's peak a dip (180,212 B after C1 for `Hv`, say), and after each ask back to the `D` before it.
- **Not played yet:** `Hv`, `Hn` and `Hw` all 1 of 1,706.
- **The fit's near cells** weren't met: 3a-3's asks came after each `Dc`, at L 319,476 B or more, above the fit table's near rows (286,708 B and under), which stay the model's. 3a-3's first `gb` came 17 s after the walk, after the tag-scan rest was done (its View closed; the fence gave back the work memory), so its history is the waited one's.
- **The 286,708 B point** did come up on the device, in main's own history on the spare: in main-c and in 3a-5's pair (both builds), where the first update's fence cut the rest and it was carved again after it, L read 286,708 B after C1's rejoin (free 124,052 B under the waited history's: the rest's View and work memory) and from C3's rejoin on. No ask was made there. 9.6's 286,708 B likely came from the same history (INFERRED). An ask in that window would meet the fit's near row (call 3, below).

**The lists against `statsref`: PASS** (s3a-end: `Hs` with nothing new, `Hw`, `Hu` and `Hk` (the snapshot's lists, paced by the TX buffer's room, 593 rows and Not played yet's 1), `Hu0`, `Hl00`-`Hl4`, `Hn`). The spare was identified by its volume, its `SYNTHCARD.TXT` and the root's cardId. Its `stats.bin` (generation 17, the boot's save, nothing written after) was copied with its base, each sha256 equal to the card's; the card was flushed and nothing written, and the copies were made read-only. Over them, at day 324 (2026-11-21), every list is `statsref`'s: Most played 7 days, 30 days and All time 66 rows each, New finds 43, Old favourites 216 and Recently played 216. Not played yet gives 1 album, the same name as the card listing's one unplayed folder. `Hk`'s six lists (50, 50, 50, 43, 200 and 200 rows) are exactly the first rows of `Hl00`, `Hl01`, `Hl02`, `Hl1`, `Hl2` and `Hl4` (ids, counts, and Recently played's last time).

**3a-4, the held snapshot: FAILS the byte rule.**
- **The first pair** (s3a-hu against main-c) was confounded. Its gap (`D1` +29,508 B, C1 +120,144 B, the placements from C2 on) was the card worker's tag-scan rest, not S3a. An investigation found it, and a skeptical second reader confirmed it:
  - after the boot's walk the scan's rest keeps its `TagStore::View` (33,416 B: Merge 144 + `kRunMax` 6,145 + the arena 22,372 + 24, which carve 29,188 B, and D's FatFile 4,228 B) until the rest is done (`restDone_`, `lib/core/CardJobs.cpp:737-738`), and the scan's work memory (90,636 B: `ScanWork` 23,044 B, `chunkBuf` 33,796 B, `readSet` 33,796 B) until 10 s after its last step (`src/app/CardTasks.cpp:29-31`, `:413-417`). In s3a-hv2 the View went about 5-10 s and the work memory about 15-20 s after the walk ended (the 5 s `[stats]` lines' PSRAM);
  - it gives them back at a library update's fence (`:684-691`), and a fence that cuts a rest restarts it after the Live (`lib/core/CardJobs.cpp:531-537`, `src/app/CardTasks.cpp:709`, `:715`);
  - main-c's first `gb` came 3 s after the walk and cut the rest, which was carved again after C1; s3a-hu's `Hu` pushed its `gb` to 8.9 s after the walk, after the rest;
  - no stats ask frees any of it: `Job::Stats` goes ahead of the scan (`lib/core/ScanScheduler.cpp:207-217`) and only delays the rest.
- **A run beyond 9.7's table,** the visits alone (s3a-hv: the model's A4 control, `Hv` at `Hu`'s points, each snapshot freed at its landing, since a console ask holds nothing), was added to separate the held snapshot's own effect from the visits'. Against main-c it shows the same gap.
- **The re-run** waits 30 s after the walk in every build (main-c2, s3a-hu2, s3a-hv2), past the rest and its 10 s. No log of the pass has a `[heap] FAILED`; the Opus played its 30 s both times in s3a-hu2 and main-c2 with underruns=0; and each of s3a-hu2's three fence lines counted the freed snapshot ("the listening stats 3892 B").
- **The visits alone** (s3a-hv2) are main's (main-c2) to the byte at every `D`, join and fence: a visit with nothing held leaves no trace. This run is the device's ground for the fallback's figures (below).
- **The held snapshot** (s3a-hu2) against main:
  - free exactly 3,908 B less while it is held (`D1`-`D10`), and main's after `Hu0` (`D11`, `D12`);
  - the largest free block main's through C1 (`D1`-`D4`: 638,964, 319,476, 319,476 and 319,476 B), and C1's two joins at main's address (C1's `Dc` lowest 171,504 B, main's 175,412 B less 3,908 B). The MP3 opens (`D4`) with the snapshot held;
  - from C2's update on, C2's rejoin at 0x3fbe71c0 ("76944 B at the top of the largest free one, 2 of 3 kept and 7 transient") against main's 0x3fa7dadc ("all in smaller free blocks"), its lowest 177,160 B against 488,460 B;
  - the largest 311,284 B against main's 393,204 B (C2, −81,920 B), 311,284 B against 401,396 B (the FLAC's open and C3, −90,112 B), and 311,284 B against 335,860 B (the Opus, after `Hu0` and the Opus again: −24,576 B, lasting after the snapshot was freed);
  - C3's joins at 0x3fbe71c0 against 0x3fa7d0e4 (2 of 3 kept against 3 of 3; the lowest 94,832 B against 78,448 B).
- **So by 9.7's rule (main's to the byte) 3a-4 fails.** The cause is INFERRED, not read: the model's A4 mechanism, a lasting carve placed around the held block, here the MP3's open (`D4`) made while the snapshot was held. The open itself reads main's largest free block; the difference shows from C2's rejoin on, about ten times the model's size.
- **By "If 3a-4 fails",** the figures and the fallback go to the owner before S3b relies on the snapshot. The fallback (the snapshot freed before each decoder's open and Auto DJ's join begin) would give the visits alone's figures (MODELLED: so in the heap model; the visits-alone run frees each snapshot at its landing, not before each open), and on the device the visits alone are main's to the byte. Its cost is a page's lists at each track change and each join while on the stats pages (the model: 39 of the suite's 68 snapshots). It is in the heap model only: the firmware doesn't have it, and its own run against main is owed if it is chosen.
- **The tag scan's rest and an ask** (for the owner, before S3b): a visit, or any S2 ask, taken while the rest's memory is held after the boot's walk (the View until the rest is done, the work memory until 10 s after the scan's last step) meets 90,636-124,052 B less PSRAM. The gates' `workerAhead()` counts the walk and compactions, not the rest (`src/app/CardTasks.cpp:274-275`). 3a-6's `Hv` took Full in it, at 45,585 ms on the spare (13's "The tag scan's rest"). *Settled (2026-11-21, later):* "Only while held" ("S3a: the owner's calls as built", below).

**3a-5, nothing held: PASS** (s3a-c against main-c). Every `D` (12), every `[dj] joined` line (7: the address, the placement, `Dc`'s lowest, the largest after) and every fence line (3) is main's to the byte.

**3a-6, a boot (default (b)): PASS** (s3a-6b on b16a189; s3a-6 on 852d982 the same but for the wrapped held time). `Hv` was sent about 3.9 s after the boot by the board's clock (its first phase line at 3,916 ms; 7.7 s on the run's log clock, which starts about 2.1 s before the reset), earlier than the table's "about 8 s", so it met the boot's scan first. Every ms figure below is the board's `millis()`.
- **Its waits,** said in order: "the boot's scan (its fold, its save)", "Auto DJ's join (working, or due)", "the card worker's walk or compaction (a rank comes after them)".
- **The phase:** Working (version 1, held 0 ms); Waiting at 3,000 ms ("held 3000 ms by the card worker's walk …, holding nothing"); Working again when the walk ended, 41,667 ms held.

- **The landing,** after 2,849 ms held, dark, at priority 1: queued 119.6 ms, ranked 721.8 ms, walked 1,987 ms in 76 passes, the longest 15.1 ms. Not played yet: 1 of 1,706.

**3a-7, G5 on a real stream: PASS** (g5-main-b and g5-s3a-b, the matched pair). OPUS.md 2.1's sequence 2: no `z`, the headphones the output and already paired (nothing paired, forgotten or set), the album digital silence.

They are 40 B apart in one matched pair, S3a reading more free although its `.bss` is 16 B over main's; the cause isn't measured. Both are over G5's session floor (43,008 B), by 7,504 and 7,544 B.

**Not covered:** a page's own ask (S3b's: every ask here was the console's); an ask with Bluetooth streaming (every ask run was in silent mode, and 3a-7 made no ask); a dark ask with the cap on (3a-1's dark control ran with it off); an ask with an Opus kept (3a-3 made none after the Opus); the fit's near cells (3a-3's asks came at L 319,476 B or more, and main's own history read 286,708 B where no ask was made); a main control for 3a-3 (each ask's `D` was read against the `D` before it, which after each update match main-c2's at the same points, a different script); more than one matched pair for 3a-7; the stall's 30 s; the fallback on the device; Auto DJ off; and the music card (S5a first). Only the re-run (main-c2, s3a-hu2, s3a-hv2) waited 30 s after the boot's walk: the other runs ran in the boot's own history, and 3a-3's asks after the boot and 3a-6's take met the tag-scan rest's memory.

**The board** was left parked in its ROM bootloader ("Staying in bootloader"), on main de4f52f's CI build, with the spare in and NVS untouched. The spare's state is that of the runs' plays and library updates.

**What goes to the owner.** S3b waits for these three calls; then the push and the PR, the owner's word. *Settled (2026-11-21, later; section 1 has them word for word):* 1 by another hold, "Borrow Dance's block" (the snapshot lent the Dance tab's block: neither the fallback nor the moves); 2 by "Cap on"; 3 by "Only while held" (a visit waits while the scan's memory is carved, not the rest giving it back at once). V8's review sent three cases of call 3 back to the owner (section 1's (a)-(c)), *settled 2026-11-22* (section 1: (a) "Don't wait at the floor", V10b; (b) and (c) "Keep both as built"). The push and the PR remain the owner's word.
1. **3a-4, the held snapshot:** the fallback (the snapshot freed before each decoder's open and Auto DJ's join begin; the page attached again at 0 rows, with Try again), or the held snapshot's moves accepted as measured, or another hold. The figures: while held, free 3,908 B under main's; from C2's update on, the largest free block 81,920 B under main's (C2) and 90,112 B under (the FLAC's open and C3), and 24,576 B under from the Opus's open, lasting after the snapshot was freed; C2's rejoin and C3's joins placed elsewhere than main's (C3: 2 of 3 kept against 3 of 3). The visits alone are main's to the byte. The fallback's cost in the model: 39 of the suite's 68 snapshots freed early.
2. **The lit cap:** `kLitCap` on for S3b (the plan's default after 3a-1), or an overrule. The figures: with the cap off, lit takes hold up to 6,104 ms (an MP3, Full; its visit's summed line 6,155 ms), past 3,000 ms for Full and Lists in every state and for Lean and No folders with a track playing, and the lit walk's longest pass was 4.9-6.2 ms idle and up to 15.5 ms with a track playing, against the plan's 4 ms; with the cap on, every hold under 2.4 s, 74-219 ms over the dark control (which ran with the cap off), the walk's longest pass up to 24.9 ms, and no `[ui] slow pass` from the stats' work. (Idle and lit, Full held 3,587 ms with the cap off.)
3. **The tag scan's rest:** whether a visit waits out the rest's memory, or the rest gives it back at once when it is done (`restDone_`). The figures: the View (33,416 B) is held until the rest is done and the work memory (90,636 B) until 10 s after the scan's last step, on the spare about 5-10 s and 15-20 s after the boot's walk ended (s3a-hv2); no gate counts either, and 3a-6's `Hv` took Full inside it. Where a library update's fence cut the rest and it was carved again, L read 286,708 B after C1's rejoin and from C3's rejoin on (main's own history, 3a-5's pair), so an ask there meets the fit's near row. The second way reaches beyond S3: it changes `CardTasks` for every ask, S2's included, and moves main's own PSRAM figures, the yardstick of 3a-4 and 3a-5, so it would need its own control run.

**S3a: the owner's calls as built (V8, V9, V8's review and V10, 2026-11-21; V10b and its review, 2026-11-22; MEASURED-HOST and MODELLED; not on a device; its device pass: below).** On `claude/listening-stats-s3`, after the device pass's docs (4540adc). Not pushed. Section 1 has the calls word for word, and the notes above (the snapshot, the fit, the API, the phase, `.bss`, the snapshot's life, the lit hold, the tests, Done when, the runs) say what each changed. The code lines cited here are 8d56155's (V10b's review: 86f1990's, but `CardTasks.cpp` from :438 down, 4 lines later; V10 changed docs only).
- **V8** a4853d1, "Code: the stats snapshot in the Dance tab's block, the tag scan's rest for the visits, kLitCap on (S3a's V8)":
  - **The lender.** `DanceMode`'s tracker scratch, 2,048 mono frames, 4,096 B of PSRAM, is carved at `begin()` and never freed (`src/app/DanceMode.cpp:33`). Only the boot's tracker bench (in `begin()`) and the tab's tracker (`loop()`, behind `active_`: `:192`, `:200`) read into it, and `TapReader` keeps no pointer to it. `lendScratch(n)` (`:104-106`) gives it while `statsvisit::loanOk(scratch_ != nullptr, active_, n)` holds (`lib/core/StatsVisit.h:64`); a `static_assert` holds the scratch to `kBound` (`src/app/DanceMode.cpp:20-21`). `setActive(true)` calls `dancelend::reclaim()` (`:80`) after its no-PSRAM and no-change returns, before `active_` is set. No member is added.
  - **The wiring** (`src/main.cpp:152-154`): `statshold::borrow()` is `danceMode.lendScratch()`, `statshold::danceUp()` is `danceMode.active()`, and `dancelend::reclaim()` is `playHistory.statsRelease("the Dance tab")`. `statshold::largestFree()` and its heap walk are gone from `src/app/ListeningStats.cpp`.
  - **`StatsLists`:** the loan at the turn (`src/app/StatsLists.cpp:721-742`, its refusal's cause `noLoanText()`, `:146-151`), the release (`freeSnap()`, `:1823-1839`), `snapshotBytes()` and `snapshotHeapBytes()` (`src/app/StatsLists.h:220`, `:224`), the rest gate (`restAhead()`, `:256`; `gateOf()`, `src/app/StatsLists.cpp:466`), `kLitCap` (`:48`), and the lines: carved and freed became lent, borrowed and released, and the asks' begin line lists the tag scan's wait. `finishVisit()` and `landVisit()` end Cancelled, nothing written, when the snapshot was released under them (`:1198-1202`, `:1406-1410`).
  - **The rest gate's word:** `CardJobs::scanHeld()` (`lib/core/CardJobs.h:250`): the scan's memory, from a scan step's `prepare()` to `trim()`. The View is a member of the scan's work memory, so it can't be open without it. `CardTasks::loop()` hands it at its end, after its trim (`src/app/CardTasks.cpp:445-446`, V10b's form), so memory given back that pass holds nothing.
- **V9** ec3f13c, "Model: the borrowed snapshot (S3a's V9)": the heap model's borrowed way ("S3a: tests", above). MODELLED:

  - **Wide** (72 histories): 0 of 432 figures moved; 0 of 360 steps failing (the control 2); 432 of 432 lent; a grade taken at 351 of 432, as the visits alone.
  - **Old decoders:** 0 of 78 moved; 0 of 65 failing (the control 2); 78 of 78 lent; a grade taken at 72 of 78, as the visits alone.

- **V8's review** 747a368, "Code: what the review found (S3a's V8)": its findings fixed in code, comments, README and tests. Among them: "frees" left over in comments and lines; the refused loan's line and `Hu`'s begin line; a visit whose loan would be refused ended before `stats.bin`'s directory is read (with no snapshot held); `statshold::danceUp()` for the refusal's cause; the remount's case in `CardTasks` (with the card jobs off the scan's memory may stay carved, and doesn't hold the visits); and `CardJobs::scanStuck()` (`lib/core/CardJobs.h:257`), so the word became `restAhead(active_ && scanHeld() && !scanStuck())`. Three cases went back to the owner (section 1's (a)-(c)), settled on 2026-11-22 (V10b, below). The check of its fixes (read-only, with its own host runs) found two nits, recorded and not changed: the early end covers only a visit with no snapshot held ("The snapshot's life"), and the model checks the loan after `stats.bin`'s directory ("S3a: tests").
- **V10**, these docs: section 1, 4.4, 4.6, 6.5, 6.6, 6.8, 7.1, 7.6, 7.11, 9.3, 9.7, 11-14; ARCHITECTURE's Listening stats bullet and its Dancing crab section. The README's console row and Layout line were V8's and its review's.
- **V10b** 86f1990, "Code: the stats visits don't wait for the tag scan at the battery floor (S3a, the owner's call)" (2026-11-22; section 1 has the call word for word): the gate's one condition. `statsvisit::restHolds(scanHeld, scanStuck, batteryFloor)` (`lib/core/StatsVisit.h:303-305`), a `constexpr` free function beside `kWhyRest` and the gate's other pure rules (`stallHolds()`, `waitStartMoves()`), so `CardJobs` doesn't learn the battery and `ScanScheduler` doesn't learn the visits. `CardTasks::loop()` hands `restAhead(active_ && restHolds(scanHeld(), scanStuck(), sched_.batteryLow()))` (`src/app/CardTasks.cpp:445-446`), the floor as this pass's `update()` left it. The floor (U13, `lib/core/ScanScheduler.cpp:129-142`): set below 10 % off USB; kept from 10 % to 15 %; let go by a reading above 15 %, or by USB at once; a reading not known keeps it (false from the start; `batteryRaw` is -1 until the UI's first reading); USB not read yet counts as in. A floor met mid-rest that lifts during a visit's hold carves nothing new beside it (the memory is already carved). The word reaches the asks a pass late (they run before `CardTasks`, in `src/main.cpp`'s `stepPlays()`), so a visit queued at the lift's own pass, or taken in its turn on the floor's word, is usually handed to the worker then and goes on, as one under way does; one still queued after it gives its block back and waits again (as worded since V10b's review). The texts say it: `CardJobs.h`, `CardTasks.cpp` and `.h`, `StatsLists.h` and `.cpp` (`gateOf()`'s comment; the visits' begin line, `:413-417`, "no tag scan holding its memory (not waited for while the battery floor pauses the scan: the take's decoder-room check then)"), the README's H row, and these docs (section 1, 4.4, 6.8, 9.3, 9.7, 11-13, ARCHITECTURE's Listening stats bullet). The tests and the gate: "S3a: tests" and "S3a: Done when", above.
- **V10b's review** 8d56155, "Code: what the review of the floor call found" (2026-11-22): `CardTasks::loop()`'s comment said a queued visit gives its block back when the floor lifts; the word reaches the asks a pass late, so one queued or taken at that pass usually goes on (the sentence above, as now worded; `src/app/CardTasks.cpp:438-444`). A comment only: the image's figures are 86f1990's to the byte (9.7's "As built (V10b's review)"). The review's other findings were these docs' cites and notes, fixed in the docs commit after it, and the kit's (RUNS-V10.md's cites, `synth.py`'s 4b banner). One nit is recorded and not changed: no host test covers `CardTasks`' wiring of the rule, checked by eye (`src/app/CardTasks.cpp:446`).

**S3a: V8-V10's device pass (owed; done: as run 2026-11-22, below).** Runs 3a-4 again, 3a-8 and 3a-9 (the run table), on the spare, with the first pass's board and rules and main de4f52f's CI build as the control. S3a's image for them is V10b's, built at its review's 8d56155 (86f1990's code and one comment; 86f1990's own image, never flashed, and V10's af1969f image are superseded: the code between af1969f and 8d56155 is the gate's condition and texts). They don't cover: the battery floor (case (a), as settled: a visit that goes ahead while the floor pauses the scan), which can't be driven on the device, since no console command changes the battery or the power state the scan reads and the console's USB lets the floor go, so it is host-tested only; a Rescan's rest; a full card's refused chunk (case (b)); a scan's first carve during a visit's hold (case (c)); and a page's own ask (S3b's). The owner's word on (a)-(c) is in (2026-11-22, section 1). S3b waits for the pass; then the push and the PR, the owner's word.

*As run 2026-11-22 (MEASURED on the spare; the kit's run book and its readers, `cmp4b.py`, `chk8.py` and `chk9.py`, each re-run on the logs after the pass):*
- **The order:** main-4b, main-8b, the coach check (nothing printed), main-9, all on main de4f52f's CI build (v0.14.0-163-gde4f52f, ELF e631b311); then 8d56155's core2 image written and verified (v0.14.0-190-g8d56155, ELF f2bbba07, md5 B7ED7602...: HEAD's code); s3a-8a, s3a-8b, s3a-4b, s3a-9; then main written back, verified, and parked ("Staying in bootloader"). Every run exited 0: one boot, nobody at the board, no coach cards, no bad line. The battery read 100 % in every `[stats]` line, so the floor (V10b) was never in play.

All 12 main's.

Their addresses, their lowest (165,968 B at the boot's; 175,412, 488,460 and 78,448 B at C1-C3's, `Dc`'s the same) and the largest after are main's.
  - **The fences:** each fence-up and built line's figures main's.

There were 4 releases (each fence's, before its fence-up line, and `Hu0`'s) and no rewrite, "freed" or "carved at" line, refusal or `[heap] FAILED`.

- **3a-8's first boot (s3a-8a): PASS** (chk8 8a: order, phase, gate, rise and full):
  - **The waits:** `Hv`, sent 3.9 s after the boot by the board's clock, said the boot's scan, Auto DJ's join, the card worker's walk, then, at the walk's line, "the card worker's tag scan (its memory held)".
  - **The phase:** Working (version 1, at 3,904 ms, held 0 ms); Waiting at 6,912 ms (held 3,006 ms); Working at 61,539 ms, its waits clear after 57,633 ms held. Every Asked line was "holding nothing".

It was Full 130,800 B (the free block above it 67,224 B, closed by 3 caps), the snapshot lent, PSRAM's free 659,292 B before and after. That is 123,741-124,764 B over the wait's lowest `[stats]` line (522K, 4 s after the walk; the bound 90,636 B) and 89,949-90,972 B over the last before the take (555K, reported only). The `[stats]` line prints PSRAM's free floored to whole K (`src/main.cpp:1157-1158`), so each margin is known to within 1,023 B, the higher figure being chk8's; the verdict holds at the lower one. The `[stats]` lines around the walk's line: 525K (-6 s), 525K (-1 s), 522K (+4 s), 555K (+9 and +14 s: the View closed), 643K from +19 s (the trim). The take came between the +14 s line and the +19 s line.

- **3a-8's second boot (s3a-8b against main-8b): hr PASS, visit PASS, as-main FAIL** (chk8 8b):
  - **`Hr` in the rest:** sent 2.0 s after the walk's line and taken 2.1 s after it in both builds, before the trim in both (on S3a before the View's close too, 4.1-9.1 s after the walk). S3a's block was 80,832 B at 0x3fbb42cc, main's 80,608 B at 0x3fbb43cc, and the free block above each "closed by caps, 2 caps". S3a ranked in 714.0 ms (queued 512.2 ms), main in 709.3 ms (511.4 ms). The lists' counts are equal: 66, 66, 66, 42, 216 and 216.
  - **The visit after the rest:** `Hv`, sent 31.4 s after the walk, after the trim, had no tag-scan wait (only the Rank lane's input wait). It was taken 0.64 s after its send, lent flat, and landed Full (held 1,543 ms).
  - **The as-main rule** wants `Hr`'s block bytes, address, close text, reads and lists to be main's. It fails on three figures; the close text and the lists' counts are equal:
    1. **The block, +224 B.** The parts besides the head are equal in both (work 11,456, lists 16,704, the windows' rows 49,632, the records' copy 1,440: 79,232 B), so the head is 1,600 B against 1,376 B. That is `parts[0] = up(sizeof(Ask))` (`src/app/StatsLists.cpp:557`, `kAlign` 16 at `:41`) over `sizeof(StatsLists::Ask)`: 1,600 B on S3a and 1,368 B on main (gdb on both ELFs). The head has been 1,600 B since V4. Since 93d38f6 a `static_assert` pins it to `statsvisit::kAskHeadBytes` (`src/app/StatsLists.cpp:290`, `lib/core/StatsVisit.h:263`), and the struct's body is unchanged from 93d38f6 to 8d56155. This is by design and older than V8 (section 1's 2026-11-21 status: "every grade is 112 B over").
    2. **The read, +5,216 B.** 512,028 B against 506,812 B is 326 DAYS rows of 16 B, from V1's DAYS pass (`lib/core/StatsRank.cpp:430`, `rep.bytesRead += static_cast<uint64_t>(rep.daysRows) * sf::kStrides[sf::kDays];`); main's ranker reads no DAYS. The visit's line in the same boot prints the span as 2026-01-01 to 2026-11-22, 326 days, and stats.bin's 60,984 B solves to 840 HOUR rows and 326 DAYS rows. This is by design and older than V8.
    3. **The address, 256 B lower** (0x3fbb42cc against 0x3fbb43cc; the block's end 32 B lower). The main session read it as the block placed at the top. A skeptic refuted that: both requests, with the heap's 12 B, round to one 81,920 B block (40 steps of 2,048 B), so the same place would give no shift, and a fixed end would give 224 B. The skeptic modelled it instead as `TopHold::take()`'s spacers (`lib/core/AutoDjJoin.cpp:311-330`). Each spacer takes the largest free block less `carvedAtMost(bytes)`, rounded down (`spacerFor()`, `:123-127`, called by `takeSpacers()`, `:129-167`; `carvedAtMost()`, `:106`), and leaves about `carvedAtMost(bytes)` for the block: 84,382 B against 84,151 B, 231 B more. `requestWithin()` (`:113-118`) rounds each spacer down to its class step. So 231 B less room drops the closing spacer one 256 B step (a room of 8-16 KB) or two 128 B steps (4-8 KB), which puts the block 256 B lower and leaves the free block above it 256 B larger. This is MODELLED only: a model of the heap model's TLSF rules (`ModelHeap`, `test/test_autodj_join/test_autodj_join.cpp:2022`) with the spacer code, outside the repo, gives a shift of 0 or -256 B over free blocks of 150-600 KB. The logs print no largest free block at `Hr`'s take, so a second skeptic couldn't recompute it. **Not explained by a measured figure: for the owner.** If it is the spacers, it follows from the head's 224 B, not from V8-V10.
  - **The generations:** the two boots read different `stats.bin` generations (S3a 26; main 24, then its boot save 25: plays counted between the runs). They are one play apart, both 60,984 B with TRKD 46 and RCNT 3,959 rows, so no compared figure moves with it.
  - **Reported, not judged:** main's `[stats]` line at +3.19 s, inside its own `Hr` hold, reads 373K (S3a printed none inside its hold). Before the walk's line both boots read 525K. After it S3a read 522K (+4.1 s, after its hold), 555K, 555K and 643K; main read 373K (inside its hold), then 555K, 555K and 643K, with no line between its hold's end (+3.34 s) and its first 555K line (+8.20 s). The walk took 21,535 ms on S3a and 22,405 ms on main, a gap the first pass's logs show too.
  - So 3a-8's second boot isn't called a pass. Its own rules (`Hr` in the rest, the visit after it) pass. The as-main rule fails: two of its three figures by design, and the address modelled, not measured. A rule S3a could meet would read main's block + 224 B, main's reads + DAYS rows × 16 B, and the address a spacer's step lower: the owner's call.
  - *Settled (2026-11-22, latest): "Accept 3a-8".* The as-main rule is that one. Main's block + 224 B and main's reads + DAYS rows × 16 B are exact, and the address is taken as following from the larger block (modelled, not measured). So 3a-8's second boot passes by the owner's call.

  - **The caught `Hv`s:** each ended "its snapshot released meanwhile ...: nothing written", its block given back, nothing asked again. During the rank: held 1,578 ms, no walk, the release 78 ms after the take's line. During the walk: held 2,519 ms, walked 1,242 ms, the release 1,844 ms after the take's line. With `ui3` and `ui1` both during the rank: held 1,410 ms, no walk.
  - **The dark:** the dark itself took the tab down (`[dance] off` just before `[ui] dark`); `Hu` was lent and landed, and the wake released it before `[dance] on (`.
  - **Against main:** the 10 `D` were main's to the byte (659,292 / 638,964 B, then 652,740 / 638,964 B), and the 16 `[dance]` on and off lines main's, in order. No `[heap] FAILED`, coach cards or `[dance] no PSRAM`. Not compared: the other 23 `[dance]` lines in each log (the tracker's bench, its 7 resets, 7 frame-rate lines and 8 of the 5 s lines, which carry timings and the internal RAM) and the internal RAM, which on S3a fell lower than main's around its `H` asks (section 11's "As run (2026-11-22)").
- **Not covered:** the battery floor, a Rescan's rest, cases (b) and (c), and a page's own ask, as above. `Hk`'s lists weren't compared with statsref: no card copies were made, and chk9 compares the two listings with each other.
- **The board:** left parked in its ROM bootloader on main de4f52f's CI build (written and verified), the spare in. esptool wrote nothing past 0x610000, so not NVS; the firmware's own counters moved as in the first pass. The spare's state is that of the runs' plays and library updates.

**S3b: device runs.** The same rules and commands as S3a's, plus `uiS…`, scripted taps (`uit<x,y>`) and drags (`uid<x0>,<y0>,<x1>,<y1>,<ms>`: the swipe resting 150 ms before its lift, so no fling; RAM), the scripted finger's skew (`uk0`-`uk2`, RAM), a tab's tap (`ui0`-`ui4`) and `X`. 3b-3's control is main's build (S3b's base, 37ed055), as S3a's runs had main's.

**A `uiS…` line after a wake waits for it** (S3b's review, finding 14). The console runs every line waiting in one pass (`SerialConsole::poll()`), and the wake is applied after it (ScreenControl's step, in that pass or a later one: `Ui::setDark(false)` makes the rise's ask, then draws the page). So after `uiv`, or a `ui0`-`ui4` or `uiS<…>` line sent to a dark screen (each wakes it; a bare `ui` or `uiS` doesn't), the next `uiS…` line is sent once the wake's `[screen] awake in … ms` line has printed, never in the same write (a dim screen is lit: no wake to wait for). Sent with it, a bare `uiS` reads the dark ("DARK", the held condition false), and a push is made in the dark. A push made on a dark screen (`uiSh`, `uiSw` or `uiS<shelf>[w]`, each waking it) ends its line "(pushed in the dark: drawn and asked at the wake)", not "(its first frame drawn)" (`uiSw`: "(its header and body drawn)"). Its ms times no frame, and its ask's `[listen]` line comes at the wake, after `[screen] off -> bright`. A push to be timed is sent lit.

Under `uiSx6` (no card) Week shows the No card line alone in A, as Working's line (no disc, no button). `uiSx5` (folding, a lifetime not whole) keeps the counting days the page held (7.10's latch). After `uiSx4`'s pages: the hub's rows 3-5 are disabled as on day 5 (dated by view 5's own first day: "Opens 30 Sep, after a month of plays"); Most played has no tray; New finds, Old favourites and Not played yet (`uiS1`-`uiS3`) read shut on that same "Opens 30 Sep, after a month of plays" line, not the Folding row's "Counting plays…"; Recently played and Week show the phase's line ("Counting plays…") as the Folding frame; each `uiS` ends "counting days 5 as latched (taken again at the lifetime's next whole reading)". After `uiSx1`'s hub: a full card folding (rows 3-5 open on the phase's line; Most played's three cells; `uiS1`-`uiS3` on the phase's line, the Folding frame's), `uiS` ending "counting days 63 or more as latched (taken again …)" (the latch keeps 63 at most) |

### 9.9 S5: the owner's card

S5a after S1 is merged and before any release or main build carrying S1 boots the music card; S5b (step 4) after S2. Only with the owner's go-ahead.

Before step 1: the board's settings and image backed up (9.1). After each boot, `z`. No taps, no `n`, `p` or space.

1. Copies of the card's `/.player/plays*.jnl`, read-only from a PC reader: `python tools/playlog.py read` gives the plays, deviceIds and epochs the first boot will fold.
2. The stats firmware boots with the card. **Nothing is played.** `H`, `Hm`: the fold equals the copies' count by 4.1's rules (`statsref`); the save; the trim of ACKed files only.
3. The owner syncs on the PC (the terminal writes ACKS); the next boot: no fold, the trim, `H` unchanged.
4. (S5b) `Hr`, `Hw`, `Hl0`: the lists on the real library (19.5k tracks); the walk's time; orphans.

After step 4: the board is left on the stats build, with the owner's word. A 0.14.0 image with this card undercounts (5.12).

**Effort:** 0.5-1 (guess; S5a 0.25-0.5, S5b 0.25-0.5), plus the owner's sync.

### 9.10 S6: the time zone

Free-standing from S3 on; two repositories.

- **C26, firmware:**
  - `HostLink::timeLine` reads an optional field 1 (−720 to +840; anything else: a range error) into `HostLink::Out`;
  - NVS `clock/tz`, an i16, schema 7, `migrate(6, 7)` doing nothing;
  - `tzOffsetMin` from it into `statsview`/`statstext`; Week's sub drops "(UTC)".
  - Tests: `test_host_link`, `test_nvs_layout`, `test_ui_stats`.
  - Docs: `docs/HOST-STATUS.md`'s `@time` (`:304-320`, "Always UTC").
- **C27, terminal:** `src/device/desk.rs` sends `@time <unix-ms> <minutes>` from `tz::local()` when it gives a zone: on terminal main after #56 lands, or on #56 once main (with 5eac90c) is merged into it. At 3edd4a4, `tz::local()` gives no zone on Windows (terminal `src/admin/tz.rs:6-7` there), and the owner's PC runs Windows. Firmware before S6 ignores the field.

**Done when:**
```
pio test -e native -f test_host_link -f test_nvs_layout -f test_ui_stats
pio test -e native
pio run -e core2 -e core2-dio
```
and the terminal's `cargo test` for `desk.rs`.

**Device** (the spare, silent mode): `@time <ms> 60`, then `@status`; Week's sub without "(UTC)"; a reset keeps `clock/tz`; `@time <ms>` with no field leaves it; `[nvs] schema 6 -> 7: ...` (`src/app/NvsSchema.cpp:85`) on the backed-up settings, then the settings put back (9.1).

**Effort:** 1.5-2.5 (guess, both repositories).

**Total:** about 19-28.5 agent-days, a guess (S0 1-1.5, S1 5-7.5, S2 4-6, S3 6-8.5, S4 1-1.5, S5a and S5b 0.5-1, S6 1.5-2.5). *As decided (2026-11-20, later):* S3 is 11.5-15 (S3a 5-6.5, S3b 6.5-8.5: 9.7; 10.5-14 before the checkers' round), so about 24.5-35 in all. S1 grew with the review's cases (a partial scan, the base's faults, the card's copy changed, the end's rotation, the twin, firstAt, retention) and runs G (a throwaway build) and L; S2 with the scheduler's lanes and the join's gate. The mood plan said 15-24, and parallel sessions built its S0-S5 in about a calendar day.

Before it (B1): a T of about 58 KB above the ranges' block and gaps of 1-17 KB between the chunks, where a long-lived carve made meanwhile split the largest free block for the session. **As built (the rework, R4-R6): each read's and save's work memory (`statsfile::kWorkBytes`, 11,456 B on the device) and a 4,148 B FatFile for each file open (`psramNew<FatFile>`, its FIL's own 4,096 B window), given back in the same call: 19,752 B in three blocks for a save, 23,900 B in four for a compaction; the boot's scan's ranges' block (1,284 B for 16 MARK rows and no ACKS row above them, 3,076 B at most), carved at the top keeping 64 KB free from its first piece to the fold's end; overflow chunks of 16,384 B (819 records) at the top, only past 96 records (40 since 2026-11-17; R6 found one for every boot's scan, its first piece's reserve of 128 slots, which the rework review took out), up to 16 (a full log: 262,144 B; the heap's peak with the boot save's work memory 273,632 B on the host), given back once the boot's save lands or after its fourth try; Auto DJ's join and an asked library update wait while the scan's blocks are held, the scan doesn't begin while an update is asked, and a track's start waits while chunks are held. In the model the saves' carves leave no trace, the ranges' block none either (1,284 B or 3,076 B), and a decoder opening under the scan's blocks fails no step beyond the control's at 1,284 B, but 10 of 65 under a full log's overflow beside a 3,076 B ranges' block: a track's start waits while chunks are held (6.7; the rework review).** S2 (planned): the walk's played set 33.8-169 KB (2-10 chunks) and folder states 25.1 KB, the rank's snapshot 16.9-152.1 KB (1-9 chunks), each for a rank's or a page's life. *As built (S3a, 2026-11-21; 9.7's "As built (S3a)"):*
- **PSRAM, transient:** the head is 1,600 B, so each ask is 112 B more. Full is 130,800 B (139,376 B), Lists 81,472 B, Lists without a clock 34,736 B, the lean ask 122,448 B (131,024 B), and No folders 109,856 B (118,432 B). READ from `statsvisit::layout()` with `sizeof(Ask)` (gdb).
- **Internal RAM:** 16 B of `.dram0.bss` over main's (`StatsLists` 24 to 44 B). MEASURED at the gate; run 3a-7 was still owed (run since: below).
- **Loop time:** V2's heap sort takes 279 µs for 696 rows on the host, against the insertion sort's 2,476 µs. The device's pieces are run 3a-2's.
- **Flash:** 26,488 B over main de4f52f's build; 8 B more since b16a189.

*As built (V8-V10, 2026-11-21; 9.7's Done when, "As built (V8-V10)"):*
- **PSRAM, held while on the stats pages:** none of the stats' own: the snapshot is lent the Dance tab's block (MODELLED, V9: every figure the visits alone's to the byte in all three builds; 3a-4 again owed).
- **Internal RAM:** `.dram0.bss` still 16 B over main's (`StatsLists` 44 B: the tag scan's gate a bit in the walk's byte); `DanceMode` main's 2,008 B.
- **Flash:** 26,932 B over main de4f52f's build (af1969f's clean build). *Since V10b:* 26,960 B (86f1990's clean build: +28 B, the gate's condition and the begin line; 8d56155's the same), `.bss` unchanged.
- **Waiting:** a visit after a boot also waits for the tag scan's memory: on the spare about 15-20 s after the walk's end (INFERRED from the device pass's `[stats]` lines; run 3a-8), on a fresh card or after a Rescan minutes.

*As run (2026-11-21; 9.7's "S3a: the device pass"; MEASURED on the spare, main de4f52f the control):*
- **PSRAM, held while on the stats pages:** run 3a-4 fails the byte rule. Held, free reads exactly 3,908 B under main's (the 3,892 B block and its header). The largest free block is main's through C1, then 81,920 B under (C2) and 90,112 B under (the FLAC's open and C3), and 24,576 B under from the Opus's open, lasting after the snapshot was freed. To the owner (9.7). *Settled (2026-11-21, later):* "Borrow Dance's block": the snapshot is lent, and 3a-4 is owed again (9.7).
- **PSRAM, transient:** the grades as built, each taken where asked: Full 130,800 B and Lean 122,448 B after the boot and after every update (3a-3), Lists 81,472 B and No folders 109,856 B in 3a-1; `Hw` 286,060 B. Each take held with the cap off 2,681-6,104 ms lit, with the cap on 1,207-2,379 ms, dark (the cap off) 1,133-2,160 ms (3a-1). After the boot's walk the card worker's tag-scan rest holds 90,636-124,052 B more, which no gate counts: its View until the rest is done and the scan's work memory until 10 s after its last step, on the spare about 5-10 s and 15-20 s after the walk ended (13, "The tag scan's rest"). *Since V8 ("Only while held"):* the visit's asks wait it out; S2's don't. *Since V10b ("Don't wait at the floor"):* not while the battery floor pauses the scan, when a visit's take beside the scan's memory is guarded by its decoder-room check.
- **Internal RAM:** run 3a-7, G5 on a real stream: 50,552 B since the boot against main's 50,512 B (40 B apart in one matched pair, the cause not measured), 7,544 and 7,504 B over G5's session floor (43,008 B).
- **Loop time:** run 3a-2, each piece under 35 ms. The turn is at most 10,350 µs in 3a-1 and 20,074 µs the first after a boot (3a-6; its directory 17,774 µs), the hand-off 6,824 µs, `finishRank()` 28,056 µs (the seal 16,611 µs in 3a-1 and 16,982 µs across the pass; 10.6-10.8 ms idle), `resolve()` at most 5,590 µs (mostly 0.6-0.7 ms) and `notPlayed()` at most 1,192 µs in 3a-1 (2,026 µs across the pass). The walk: lit, the slices averaged 4.6-7.4 ms a pass, and the longest pass was 4.9-6.2 ms idle and 9.1-15.5 ms with a track playing (Full 13.8 ms with an MP3), against the plan's 4 ms; dark or with the cap on, Full's 42-50 passes, the longest up to 24.9 ms. No `[ui] slow pass` came from the stats' work.
- **Worker time:** Full's rank lit at priority 0 took 2,539-2,594 ms idle, 4,445-4,478 ms with an MP3 and 3,482-3,616 ms with a FLAC; at priority 1 (dark or the cap on) 742-1,298 ms (3a-1).

*As run (2026-11-22; 9.7's "S3a: V8-V10's device pass"; MEASURED on the spare, V10b's image, 8d56155's, against main de4f52f's CI build):*
- **PSRAM, held while on the stats pages:** none of the stats' own. Run 3a-4 again reads main's to the byte, free included, at every `D` (12), `[dj] joined` line (7) and fence (3), each loan lent at one address with PSRAM's free the same before and after. The first pass's 3,908 B under main's and its 24,576-90,112 B moves are gone. Run 3a-9: the 10 `D` main's with the Dance tab coming and going.
- **PSRAM, transient:** the grades as built, each taken where asked: Full 130,800 B (3a-4 again, 3a-8, 3a-9) and Lean 122,448 B (3a-9). S2's `Hr` is 80,832 B against main's 80,608 B: the ask's head, 1,600 B against main's 1,376 B (main's `sizeof(Ask)` 1,368 B rounded up to 16 B, `kAlign`; V4's, older than V8). Since V8 a visit at a boot takes once the tag scan's work memory is given back: its loan's PSRAM free read 123,741-124,764 B over the wait's lowest `[stats]` line (3a-8; the line prints whole K).
- **Waiting:** a visit sent at a boot (3.9 s) waited 57,633 ms in all, its take 15.7 s after the walk's line on the spare (3a-8), inside the 15-20 s INFERRED before.
- **Internal RAM** (measured, not compared: none of this pass's rules reads it; run 3a-7 is the internal RAM's): in 3a-9 S3a's `[stats]` internal RAM (`ram=`) fell from 59K to 51K at 7 of its 5 s lines, each within 5 s of an `H` ask's send, and its lowest since the boot (`min=`) read 50K from the first of them (+94 s) on; main, whose script sends no `H` ask, read 59K and a lowest of 59K over the same stretch. The 5 s `[dance]` lines show the same lowests (`min=50K` against `min=59K`). In 3a-4 again the lowest was 50K against main's 51K; 3a-8's second boot read main's lowest (64K).
  - **The cause: the card worker's task, main's cost for any worker job** (2026-11-22, later; traced read-only in the code, the ELFs and every kit log, and two skeptics could not refute it). A visit's rank, like any job, makes the task when no worker is alive (`CardWorker::spawn()`, `src/app/CardWorker.cpp:58-73`). Its 7,680 B stack (`kStackBytes`) and its 352 B TCB (gdb on 8d56155's ELF) are both internal: FreeRTOS's `pvPortMalloc` asks `MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL`, and a task that reads the card can't have a PSRAM stack (`CardWorker.h:38-39`; neither `FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM` nor `SPIRAM_ALLOW_STACK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY` is set). It ends itself `kIdleExitMs` (3,000 ms) after `poll()` takes the rank's step in. With the heap's 12 B of poisoning and TLSF's rounding, that is about 8.2-8.6 KB (INFERRED), read as 8K or 9K.
  - **What the logs show** (MEASURED from the logs' times, unconfounded asks only). Every `[stats]` line inside that life read 7-9K low (69 of 69), and every line from 3.25 s to 9 s after it read clean (36 of 36). The edge follows the rank's end, not the landing: in 3a-1 a line 3.00 s after a rank's end still read low and one 3.06 s after another read clean, though its visit had landed 2.2 s before.
  - **Main pays the same.** Main's boot walk read 65K while the worker ran and 73K once it ended (main-9). Main's `gb` under a play read 59K, then 51-52K, then 59K once `library.idx` was saved (main-4b). S2's `Hl00`, from an idle worker, read 73K, then 65K (s3a-end, b16a189's S2 build).
  - **Why main showed no step.** Main's run of 3a-9's script sends no `H` ask, so its worker never woke while it played. Main-8b's `Hr` ran on a worker the boot's work already had up, so it showed no step either. Its 0.5 s queue was the console's input wait, `inputQuietMs`.
  - **The 1K under it.** Nothing S3a's visits carve beyond S2's asks is internal: their blocks, loans, files and work buffers are PSRAM or are freed in their own call. The 1K between `ram=51K` and `min=50K` is INFERRED to be a printf's line buffer: Arduino's `Print::vprintf` mallocs any line of 64 chars or more, internal under 4,096 B, and a landing line of 606-642 chars prints during the worker's life.
  - **Not measured:**
    - the dip in bytes (a run, 3a-10, was prepared in the kit and not run: nothing moved to check);
    - a visit's rank under way when the headphones link. S2's `Hr` and main's `gb` have the same exposure, and `release()` ends an idle worker during a link's set-up and its wait for AVRCP.
  - **Considered, not built:** ending the task in the pass that takes a visit's rank in. That gives the same bytes back about 3 s sooner and moves nothing; the owner's call.
- **Worker time:** `Hr`'s rank 714.0 ms against main's 709.3 ms (4.7 ms more; INFERRED: its DAYS read, 5,216 B more); a dark Full ranked 735.7-736.6 ms (3a-8) and, with an MP3 playing at priority 1, 1,054.5 ms dark and 1,236.8-1,256.2 ms lit (3a-9).

A slow pass is a UI hitch, not an audio risk: the sinks draw on a ring of about 1.5 s (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:83`).

**S0 baselines** (MEASURED-HOST, 2026-11-11, on this Windows box). The tree was cad1449: main 241642d's code with C0-C2's docs. Main since bc29b54 changed only `tools/version.py` (#58, NEXT_RELEASE 0.15.0) and docs, so this is bc29b54's firmware with the next version's string. Every later stage compares against these.
- **Host tests:** `pio test -e native` from Git Bash (PlatformIO Core 6.2.0): 108 suites, 2,286 test cases, 2,286 succeeded, 0 failed, in 18 min 46 s.
- **`pio run -e core2`** (PowerShell, MSYSTEM unset; a clean build, 8 min 19 s): flash 4,423,691 B (70.3 % of 6,291,456; `flash_guard: ok: app 4.31 MB = 72% of the 6.00 MB slot`), 12 B over 5ea8d34's 4,423,679 B (the version string). RAM 59,064 B (PlatformIO's figure: `.dram0.data` 25,112 B and `.dram0.bss` 33,952 B; 65,512 B of `dram0_0_seg` left for the heap). IRAM 5,161 B left: `iram0_0_seg` ends at 0x400A0000 and `.iram0.text` at 0x4009EBD7. `iram_diet: 51 of 51 libc objects moved to flash`.
- **The link map's `libc_a-*` objects in `.iram0.text`** (`.pio/build/core2/firmware.map`, the `libc_a-<name>.o` inputs between `.iram0.text` and `.dram0.data` in its memory map), 25: bzero, div, envlock, getenv_r, isspace, memchr, memcmp, memcpy, memmove, memset, strcat, strchr, strcmp, strcpy, strcspn, strlcat, strlcpy, strlen, strncmp, strncpy, strnlen, strrchr, strspn, strstr, tolower. A stage that adds a name to this list has linked a pinned object; IRAM left must stay 5,161 B.

**Main 89188f4's baseline** (2026-11-18: main with #62's Bluetooth change, which S1 took at 2ff47e0; read from its CI run and that run's link map: core2 ELF 475da049, core2-dio ELF 1601bbc1). The device's control image (ELF 4720fb38, built here, the Q runs' main) is 4,426,643 B, 16 B more than CI's, so a local build read against CI's figures carries those 16 B. S1's figures from 9a0532c on are read against these, S0's kept as history: against S0's they would count #62's cost as S1's.
- **Host tests:** 108 suites, 2,291 test cases, all passed (CI, not this box: S0's 2,286 and #62's 5; confirm by a run here).
- **`pio run`:** core2 flash 4,426,627 B (core2-dio 4,426,659 B), RAM 59,080 B (`.dram0.data` 25,112 B, `.dram0.bss` 33,968 B: 16 B over S0's), IRAM left 5,161 B (`.iram0.text` ends at 0x4009EBD7), S0's 25 `libc_a-*` objects in `.iram0.text`.

**Main 987216f's baseline** (2026-11-19: main with #63's hole hint and #64's waits and 4,095 B malloc limit, which S1 took at 985f4a9; read from its CI run's artifact and link map, its SHA256SUMS checked: core2 `v0.14.0-46-g987216f`, ELF 393c6599; core2-dio ELF 376d18ab). That artifact's image is the device's control for C4/M4, the linked pairs and visit A. S1's figures from 985f4a9 on are read against these.
- **Host tests:** 2,325 test cases, all passed (CI).
- **`pio run`:** core2 flash 4,428,759 B (core2-dio 4,428,791 B), RAM 59,120 B (`.dram0.data` 25,112 B, `.dram0.bss` 34,008 B: 40 B over 89188f4's, #63's and #64's statics), IRAM left 5,161 B (`.iram0.text` ends at 0x4009EBD7), S0's 25 `libc_a-*` objects in `.iram0.text`.
