## 13. Risks

What would change the plan:

- **The save is felt.** If S1 I or H shows the boot's save over 0.3 s, or a compaction over 1.5 s, with an MP3, or any break: move it to a worker job after Ready, and trim at a boot only what the card's MARK already covers. Each boot's slots are then trimmed one boot later, and folds during the worker's save need a frozen snapshot (its memory measured first). **Measured** (I: B1, C1-V and visit A's s1mv-a1 and s1mv-a2, 5.14's "Its device runs"): a full log's boot save took 2,487-2,689 ms on the loop, about 8-9 times the 0.3 s line, behind a held start, once a card; nothing played through it, so the "with an MP3" case (I's outcome (a)) is not measured. The next boot's compaction took 1,336-1,442 ms idle, under 1.5 s. No break. Whether 2.5-2.7 s once a card is acceptable is the owner's call (section 1; settled 2026-11-19: accepted). A failed save on a nearly full card is below ("A nearly full card").
- **The format is primary data.** A mistake found after S1's first release stays on cards: every later firmware reads v1. S0's reference and S1's proof are the guard. If S1 ships alone, that release is the first real-card run, so S5's steps 1-2 are S5a, run before that release.
- **A downgrade, or a 0.14.0 unit** with the card, trims without folding: an undercount, counted in HEAD's gap and said in the release notes. No double count.
- **The rotation hole.** A rotation deletes (or moves to a twin) a `plays.1.jnl` holding plays the card's MARK doesn't cover, and no save lands before the restart: those plays are lost to the stats (5.5). As built since the rework (5.14), S1 as first built's two give-back causes are closed, and others are open where S1 as first built kept the plays in RAM and a later save could still land (5.14's list). The sharpest needs one play, not a rotation's 6,553 (the review): a full log (13,106 slots) whose boot save fails all four of its tries over the boot's waits (about 1.4 s), by a fault that clears before the first play ends. A full log's `plays.jnl` is at its cap, so that play rotates, its save refused (behind), and up to 6,553 plays of `plays.1.jnl` are lost to the stats (`boot-overflow-full-rotation`). The others: a log not full whose boot save didn't land with an overflow, a session whose records' saves fail or are refused to 40 (96 until 2026-11-17), a held tmp lost before its promotion, a section damaged under the session; each then needs `plays.jnl`'s room in plays before a rotation. A session whose saves fail before it is behind loses the plays of every `plays.1.jnl` a rotation took out meanwhile too, held only in the records behind drops (`records-fail-rotations`: 30 at the fixtures' cap of 8; 77 at the cap of 16 with 96 records until 2026-11-17; the rework review). Holding the full log's overflow until that first rotation would close the sharpest (the join waiting up to a track, and steps failing in the model); folding the log again above the card's MARK, oldest file first, in windows of 40 records with a save each, would close the hole for good (and make behind unneeded): about 330 saves for a full log's 13,106 slots (about 140 with 96 records; first written as about 100) and a second reader of the log. Both the owner's call; the owner's default is the hole documented, not closed. As built, it happens when:
  - the rotation's save fails (a full or failing card), or its tmp is held and not promoted before the restart (5.5, step 5);
  - the stats are Off this session (Unread, Newer, no memory for the fold, the folds dropped while ending);
  - this session's saves are refused (a partial scan, a changed `stats.bin`, behind its log: 5.5);
  - the rotation comes in the orderly end's flush with under 1 s of its budget left (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:307`; `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:505, 437`);
  - a full log's boot save fails all four of its tries, by a fault that clears before the first play ends: that play rotates, its save refused (behind), and up to 6,553 plays of `plays.1.jnl` are lost (`boot-overflow-full-rotation`: 128 at the fixtures' cap of 128; its `-retry` twin, the second try landing, 0);
  - the session is behind its log otherwise (the records full after a save that didn't land or was refused, a boot overflow not saved in a log that isn't full, a held tmp lost before its promotion, a section damaged), and a rotation follows (`records-behind-rotation`: 64 at the cap of 64; `section-damaged` loses its 4 to the next boot's Corrupt read instead);
  - the session's saves fail or are refused until it is behind, and rotations came before: each took out a `plays.1.jnl` whose plays were in the records alone, which behind drops (`records-fail-rotations`: 30 at the cap of 8; 77 at the cap of 16 with 96 records until 2026-11-17; the rework review).
  Each but the full log's needs a rotation after the save that didn't land or the refusal: `plays.jnl`'s room in plays, up to 6,553 (as S1 as first built's needed the second rotation of a session, or a boot whose save failed or was skipped). statsref's sessions assert each count exactly (R2), and `test_stats_store` runs them through the firmware's glue (R5). S1 as first built's two give-back causes (a rotation while the blocks were given back, its own save unlanded: `update-rotation-unsaved`; one after a take-back that left RAM behind: `update-behind-rotation`, 2 lost) closed with the give-back.
- **The resident set's budget** (5.14). In the model a set held from setup costs by its bytes, wherever it sits. Up to about 4 KB it fails only steps main's own variation fails too, and from 8 KB steps the control doesn't (8,192 B: 3 of 360 with the device's decoder requests, 21 with the old shapes; 12 KB: 11 and 31). The edges in between move with the decoders' shapes (4,096 B: 0 or 4; 1,024 B: 1 or 0), so 3,584 B is a budget held by the `static_assert` and confirmed by the device re-run, not an edge the model measured (since the move, 2026-11-17: 2,048 B of internal RAM, nothing resident in PSRAM but MARK's block on a card past 8 rows). Nothing joins the block later unless it comes out of it, or R1's case still passes and the device confirms it. S2's plan (6.1, 6.5, 11) holds a hash → id map (16.9-67.6 KB) and the album bits resident: as planned they can't stay. S2 must keep them for a rank's or a page's life, carved at the top and given back, or redesign them before it starts; S3's week (96 B) fits in the state, S6's hours (1,536 B) don't. *Corrected (2026-11-20, later; call 2, "Lifetime line, no memory"):* S3 keeps no week, in the state or anywhere. The 7 days are read by each visit's ask into its snapshot, and nothing of S3 is resident. S6's hours remain S6's question.
- **The model is noise-bound, and doesn't reproduce round 2.** Main's own variation (its queue block 2,048 B longer) fails 2 of the wide set's 360 steps with the device's decoder requests and 6 with the old shapes, and main's joined moves with the history alone (H4's from 106,484 to 294,900 B in the device's shape). On round 2's own sequence (5.14's history D) the model gives S1 minus main -69,632, 0 and +36,864 B where the device showed 0, -106,496 and -67,584 B; the decoders' shapes alone move a step by 110 KB. The device has one sample per condition, and its room is about 100 KB under the model's. So the device re-run against main's figures (5.14's build plan, within the class of main's own figure) decides, not the model.
- **The records' save is felt.** It comes once in 24 plays (64 until 2026-11-17), 0.08-0.2 s typical and 0.35-0.7 s at the ceiling on the loop (ESTIMATED; on the device 91 ms with a 16 KB `stats.bin` on the move's M, 0 underruns; 225 ms with 60,688 B on B1's M2; 502 ms with 144,544 B after a full log; no `[ui] slow pass` from it; failing on a card with one free cluster 171 ms once, then about 5.3 s each before #63, 98-191 ms each with it: "A nearly full card", below), at a pass the write gate lets through, and at 40 records even in an update's fence, while the build runs on the worker (a carve and a free in one call, as the rotation's save already may). If run H or M shows a `[ui] slow pass` from it, or an underrun: cut it into pieces across passes (the records it holds are fixed at its start; nothing else writes `stats.bin` meanwhile), or move it to the worker as the first risk says. Its stack (R5's sums): 1,488 B from `PlayHistory::loop`, 2,080 B from `loop()`'s own, under the rotation's save (11).
- **A decoder opening under the boot's held blocks.** A tap on Play during the boot's scan opens a decoder while the stats' scan blocks are held. With the device's decoder requests (5.14): the ranges' 3,072 B cost nothing wherever they sit; the 10,896 B work memory the design first held cost up to 8 steps of 360, so it is no longer held; a full log's overflow (about 270 KB, about 1.4 s and its save, once a card) fails 0-29 steps by order and placement, none when the Opus opens after it. So a track's start the listener asks waits while overflow chunks are held (once a card, about 1.5-3 s late as estimated; measured since, 4.39-4.75 s for a full log, B1, C1-V and s1mv-a1: the scan about 1.2-1.4 s, its save 2.5-2.7 s; the owner accepted the wait on the estimate, section 1). If the owner would rather not hold a start, the cost is the model's 0-29 steps for that session. As built (R6): a start waits during every boot's scan (its first piece reserves one chunk), and with the firmware's blocks the model finds the Opus under the scan's blocks fails no step in either shape; with the old shapes it costs the boot's join 16,384 B more than the order's own cost. As built (the rework review): a start waits only while a full log's fold holds chunks (the scan reserves no look-ahead), once a card; and the wait is load-bearing after all: with the ranges' block at its largest (ACKS's 16 rows above MARK, 3,076 B) the Opus under a full log's overflow fails 10 of 65 steps and the boot's join 65,536 B under main's with the Opus first (5.14's R6 notes); with 1,284 B none, wherever the start comes in the scan.
- **The scan and a library update wait for each other** (the review). The boot's scan doesn't begin while an update is asked, and an update asked once the scan has begun waits in Asked for its blocks: up to about 1.4 s and the save for a full log (4.6 s in all on the device, c1-i1), and the boot's waits (about 1.4 s) more when that save fails. Without these the model fails 15-48 steps of 360 (the scan's blocks held through, or carved during, update 0's build); R1's case, with the design's own scan blocks (a full log's ranges and 16 chunks), 24-48; R6's, with the firmware's, 25-62. A post-sync rebuild at the boot (round 2's boot 2) is the case: the scan then waits for the build, the plays in the Tracker.
- **A section of `stats.bin` damaged under the session** (the review). Every save now reads the card's sections, so a CRC that fails with the header unchanged stops the session's saves at once (behind, `H` naming it), and the next boot reads the file Corrupt and renames it `stats.bad` (5.4): HEAD's totals, HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD are lost, and the stats start again from what the log still holds. S1 as built healed it at the next save from RAM's copy. It needs the card's data to change between reads without a write of ours (a failing sector); the console says why.
- **The boot's hold splitting PSRAM** (B1, 2026-11-17; 5.14, "The hold's T"): the free block the hold's top blocks left above them, and a full log's gaps between chunks, took a long-lived carve made during the hold (the first frame's canvas, a queue) and split the largest free block for the session (three classes at the join on I-3m, twelve on I-1; free unchanged). Fixed by A + G, host-gated and device-checked on the spare card (3 MARK rows) on 2026-11-18: every take Closed, the join's largest and `D` main's to the byte (c1-i1's after-stop `D` aside: the card's boot walk splitting it as it does main's, C3/M3; 5.14's "Its device runs"); G is not run on a device: no card past 8 MARK rows exists. Under #64's 4,095 B limit, which puts each File's 4 KB stdio buffer in PSRAM, C4/M4 and visit A (2026-11-19, 985f4a9 against main 987216f) gave main's to the byte again, a track's buffer opened inside the ranges' hold (s1mv-a3) and a long log's arena hold (s1mv-a1) among them; two cases the merge's PSRAM audit names are not run: another task's 4 KB carve in a take's window (now a track's open or an SDP search's list too; B1's split, -49,152 B in the model, the take's line then saying `above it a block in use`), and a remount's Room path, where an MP3's next track keeps its buffer in the uncapped T (-61,440 B in the model). What stays: a cap that misses (another task's carve first), another task's block at T's rest's head between a cap and its walk, no spacers, a block in a hole, a block above that grows between caps (another task's free), a decoder's room (the ranges' block's at every take, the arena's while a track plays: the caps given back, and the arena) or no room for the arena (its chunks then carved as fe61db9 carved them, the hold's caps given back) leave that hold as before, and its `[listen]` line says so (the rest's head said Closed until the recheck).
- **A nearly full card** (B2, 2026-11-18; 5.14's "Its device runs"). On the spare filled to one free cluster each failed save after the first held the loop 5,295-5,398 ms (the boot's 5,430 ms): FatFs's search for `stats.tmp`'s clusters starts after the last cluster it allocated, wraps, and reads the whole FAT, and the store checks no free space before it. Nothing else on the loop runs meanwhile (the next slot, `[stats]`, the amp's line). B2's tries all fell in pauses; with real tracks the write gate opens mid-track, so up to 17 such stalls a session could fall in a play. Nothing is lost but in the rotation hole's cases (5.5; above: a rotation's save fails on such a card too). **With #63's hole hint** (taken in at 985f4a9; visit B, 2026-11-19, the same card, B2's stops): each failed try 98-191 ms, the queue's writes 0-64 ms, 0 underruns; the boot's save, the first allocation after the computer's fill, 5,523 ms (FatFs's search from FSINFO's next-free: #63's known uncovered case, main's too). Still uncured, by code: a few free clusters more than 8,192 clusters apart (each try walks between them, about 2.6 s on average) and a free count not known (a whole walk each try). The other way, beside #63: the save asks `cardspace::freeBytes()` (src/storage/CardSpace) first and doesn't try with less room than `stats.bin` needs (the owner's call, section 1; settled 2026-11-19: no check, #63's hint).
- **S1's loop work and #64's waits** (the merge's scheduling audit, 2026-11-19; READ, not measured; 5.14's "S1 and #64's waits"). None of #64's waits holds the stats' loop work: a save of 1.3-2.6 s meeting a radio link's set-up delays `CardWorker::release()` for its length (rd-g5-fix1's shape, the worker's stack meeting the set-up: 42,544 B on #64's branch before its set-up wait), and a library update asked before S1's fold begins holds the play log's scan through each link's set-up and AVRCP, with no bound while radio links come and go (#64's own open call), plays waiting in the Tracker (a 33rd lost to the log and the stats). Answers if it shows: S1's saves and scan waiting on #64's waits too, `CardTasks`' release check before `stepPlays`, or the corner accepted as narrow.
- **PSRAM at the ceiling, S2 as built (A, 2026-11-20; MODELLED).** Nothing of S2 is resident. An ask's one block is 83,872 B for `Hr`, about 125 KB for most of `Hl`, and with the played set (`Hw`, `Hl3`) 158,876 B at P = 2,214 and 289,948 B at P = 19,478. The last is refused for room after a library update (6.7: the decoder's room kept), and so is P = 2,214's once an Opus holds its buffers; the boot's are taken. A refused ask says so and holds nothing. S3's Not played yet needs the played set too: its fallbacks are the plan's below (u32 prefixes confirmed by the full hash, fewer chunks walked in turn) or a walk per chunk. *Answered (2026-11-20, later; call 3, "5-byte fingerprints"):*
- **u40 prefixes, with no confirmation by the full hash.** Confirming would cost a card search for each matched album, about 25 s at P ≈ N. The error is one way only (4.3).
- **The regions are reused,** so the visit's Full grade is 130,688 B and the lean ask 122,336 B on the spare (9.7). Both fit at every point the board measured after an update.
- **The model's 249,844 B and lower points** still refuse Not played yet, and 180,212 B refuses every ask.
- **PSRAM at the ceiling** (S1 as first built, history, and S2's plan; the rework held 3,200 B, at most 3,584 B, whatever P, until the move to internal RAM: 0 resident since). A card where nearly every track has been played takes about 267 KB resident (in chunks) and up to about 195 KB more during a walk. A failed allocation leaves names as "Counting plays…" and never loses data. Fallbacks: 6.7's ladder; u32 prefixes for the played set (4 B, confirmed by the full hash on a match), album bits from fewer chunks. (History: S3 takes u40 prefixes with no confirmation, the bullet above.) Held through the updates, S1's blocks alone failed the model's pass rule at P = 19,478's resident ceiling (194,880 B) and after a full never-synced log's fold, whether its save landed or not (S1 C8). The owner chose to give them back in updates (2026-11-11), and the model passes the mood plan's rule for every shape, and since S1's gate after the give-back the device's 16,368 B of room over the line after a FLAC update too: with 16,384 B chunks the re-read at P = 19,478 cost joined one chunk, 16,384 B (the give-back review), a chunk over 11's budget that chunks of whole rows took out (6.7). S1 L on the device measures it.
- **The give-back's cost** (S1 as first built, history: the rework removed the give-back, 5.14). Each library update saves `stats.bin` first when the fold holds new plays, in the fence's pass (the update's safe point, and since the give-back review a pass the write gate lets through: the step waits in Asked for one), and reads it back whole, its base too (467 KB at 19,478 played), once the update and the rejoin are over (a read the card fails made again, the boot's waits apart). If S1 L shows either felt (a `[ui] slow pass`, an underrun): the re-read can skip the base's whole read when `stats.bin` is the copy the session stood on. A session whose give-back's save didn't land shows fewer plays until the next boot, saves nothing more, and loses to a later rotation that session the plays in `plays.1.jnl` RAM gave up (5.5). If that hole matters, the code's answer is to fold the log again above the card's MARK at the take-back (the remount's path over `plays.1.jnl` and `plays.jnl`), which makes the behind state unneeded: the owner's call.
- **Orphans.** Renames, Opus conversions and NFC/NFD changes take plays out of the shelves until ORIG is followed. The totals stay right.
- **RCNT's cap** shortens the 30-day window for very heavy listening. Its cell says how many days.
- **UTC days** mark the wrong "today" in the listener's evening west of UTC (or morning east of it) until S6. O4 accepted that.
- **Translations** are machine-made; 684 texts owe a native review (666 new, 18 short forms of ARB words). (As decided, 2026-11-20, later: 693, of which 675 new and 18 short forms: 8.2.)
- **The tray's splits** are untested on this panel.
- **The scratch spec and frames** go with Windows' temp cleanup in about a week. This plan inlines the spec; S0's C1 copies the frames from the canvas, which persists.
- **A `stats.bin` the card fails to read at every boot** (a bad sector) keeps the stats off and the trims held. The log stays bounded by the rotation, which then deletes plays no MARK covers (the rotation hole); the console says why; a disk check is the fix.
- **Two hashings with Auto DJ on.** The library is hashed twice after a Live (the join's and the stats' walk): about 0.1 s more CPU. If S2 F shows it felt, the observer ride (14) comes back.

*S3's risks (2026-11-20, later; the design run's review, 9.7):*
- **Lit asks.** Every UI ask is lit: the pages hold their lists only while the screen is lit, so it ranks at priority 0. 9.6 measured only dark asks (rank 0.70-1.02 s, walk 0.65-0.90 s at priority 1). "Accept dips" covered 1-3 s dark peaks, during which Auto DJ's join and a library update wait. No UI ask is covered by it until S3a measures the lit holds (run 3a-1). The gate takes no block while the Rank lane would wait, and a wait after the take gives it back at once, so a block never queues behind a wait. What is left is the rank and the lit walk, ESTIMATED 3-5 s together at priority 0. Past 3 s, S3 caps it by default (UI asks rank and walk as dark, and a visit's block is given back at 3 s in all), and the figures go to the owner, who may overrule. *As run (2026-11-21, run 3a-1; MEASURED):* past 3 s. With the cap off each lit take's hold was 2,681-6,104 ms (the visits' summed lines up to 6,155 ms): Full and Lists in every state, Lean and No folders with a track playing. So `kLitCap` goes on for S3b, and the figures go to the owner (9.7's device pass, call 2). With the cap on they were 1,207-2,379 ms, 74-219 ms over the dark control (run with the cap off), and none was capped. The lit walk's longest pass was up to 15.5 ms with a track playing, against the plan's 4 ms a pass. *Settled (2026-11-21):* "Cap on": `kLitCap` is `true` since V8.
- **The held snapshot** (call 1). A plain 3.9-4 KB PSRAM block held for minutes, across decoder opens and the dark's edge. The precedent is FilteredAlbums' 6.8 KB block on Albums, whose device record is one `D` before and after its carve, with no control run (`docs/MOOD-FILTERS.md:1051`). 5.14 shows the model missed a resident block's 81,920 B on the device, so the model's A4 is a pre-check and run 3a-4 decides, to the byte: the B and C lines would have passed that very case. If it fails, the fallback that frees the snapshot before any decoder opens or join begins (9.7), its own run against main, and the owner. *As run (2026-11-21, run 3a-4, run again 30 s after the boot's walk; MEASURED):* it fails. Held, free is 3,908 B under main's. From C2's update on the largest free block reads 81,920 B (C2) and 90,112 B (the FLAC's open and C3) under main's, and 24,576 B under from the Opus's open, lasting after the snapshot was freed, with C2's rejoin and C3's joins placed elsewhere than main's. The visits alone are main's to the byte. The fallback, the moves accepted, or another hold: the owner's (9.7's device pass, call 1). *Settled (2026-11-21, later):* another hold, "Borrow Dance's block": the snapshot is lent the Dance tab's block and nothing of it is carved (9.7's "As decided (2026-11-21): the snapshot in the Dance tab's block"); 3a-4 is owed again. *As run (2026-11-22, 3a-4 again on V10b's image; MEASURED):* the borrowed snapshot leaves PSRAM main's to the byte, free included, at every `D`, join and fence, held across the MP3's, the FLAC's and the Opus's opens and each update's rejoin. That measures the console's `Hu`; a page's own hold is S3b's (run 3b-4).
- **Changes to S2's checked core:** the RCNT-first order, the u40 set, the reused regions, the DAYS pass and the copy-out by index. They are guarded by statsref's equality on every fixture, the u40-against-u64 test, the overlay pattern test and `Hw`'s count on the device (run 3a-3).
- **The u40 error.** At worst about 1e-4 wrongly hidden albums a walk, deterministic until the played set changes. It never shows a played album, and a host test pins it.
- **Margins that rest on estimates.** Full at P = 19,478 has 4,504 B to spare at the device's worst after-update point (286,708 B) with the snapshot elsewhere, and is 3,440 B short with the snapshot carved out of that block. Both are within one class of the line, where the rule's floors can't call it (9.7's fit). They rest on T (about 5.4 KB, which varies), the head's 1,488 B (ESTIMATED) and where the snapshot lands. Past it, the visit takes Lists, and Not played yet's Try again makes its lean ask (4,912 B to spare, near the line too). *As built (S3a, MODELLED; 9.7's fit):* the head is 1,600 B. In the model's 286,708 B heap, Full at P = 19,478 is taken either way (+6,205 / +2,109 B), so a page there never reaches the lean ask. `Hn`'s lean ask is taken either way too (+14,397 / +10,301 B). Run 3a-3 decides. *As run (2026-11-21, run 3a-3; MEASURED on the spare):* the head is 1,600 B, every snapshot came out elsewhere than the largest free block, and T was 8,184, 12,284 and 9,884 B after the updates, more than the fit's 5.4 KB. 3a-3's asks came after each `Dc`, at L 319,476 B or more, so Full and Lean were taken with no near cell met. The 286,708 B point did come up on the device, in main's own history on the spare (main-c, and both builds in 3a-5): where the first update's fence cut the tag-scan rest and the rest was carved again, L read 286,708 B after C1's rejoin and from C3's rejoin on. No ask was made there, and P = 19,478 isn't the spare's, so the near cells are untested on the device, not absent ("The tag scan's rest", below).
- **The seal's loop time.** S2's `Wanted::seal()` is an insertion sort on the loop, never timed (ESTIMATED tens of ms at 696 rows). V2 makes it a heap sort, and run 3a-2 holds `finishRank()` under 35 ms. *As built:* 279 µs for 696 rows on the host, against the insertion sort's 2,476 µs (MEASURED-HOST). *As run (2026-11-21, run 3a-2; MEASURED):* on the device the seal takes 10.6-17.0 ms across the pass (10.6-16.6 ms in 3a-1), and `finishRank()` at most 28,056 µs, under 35 ms.
- **Opus cards.** Opus's buffers (128,023 B) stay held while the take still asks a whole decoder's room (`StatsGlue.h:232`). If run 3a-3 shows frequent refusals with an Opus kept, "a decoder's room by what's held" goes to the owner. *As run (2026-11-21, run 3a-3):* nothing was refused, but no ask came with an Opus kept: not measured.
- **The turn's loop work** (`lookFile()`, the two opens, `finishRank()`, `resolve()`) runs outside `Ui::loop`, unseen by `[ui] slow pass`. A touch that lands meanwhile waits for it. Run 3a-2 holds each under 35 ms. *As run (2026-11-21, run 3a-2; MEASURED):* each under 35 ms. The turn is at most 10,350 µs in 3a-1 and 20,074 µs the first after a boot (3a-6), the hand-off 6,824 µs, `finishRank()` 28,056 µs, `resolve()` at most 5,590 µs (mostly 0.6-0.7 ms) and `notPlayed()` at most 1,192 µs in 3a-1 (2,026 µs across the pass).
- **The tag scan's rest and an ask's take during it** (2026-11-21; run 3a-4's confound, MEASURED on the spare). After the boot's walk the card worker's tag scan keeps its rest's `TagStore::View` (33,416 B) until the rest is done (`lib/core/CardJobs.cpp:737-738`) and its work memory (90,636 B) until 10 s after its last step (`src/app/CardTasks.cpp:29-31`, `:413-417`): on the spare about 5-10 s and 15-20 s after the walk ended. A library update's fence gives them back, and a fence that cuts the rest restarts it after the Live (`lib/core/CardJobs.cpp:531-537`, `src/app/CardTasks.cpp:709`, `:715`). The gates' `workerAhead()` counts the walk and compactions, not the rest (`src/app/CardTasks.cpp:274-275`), and no stats ask frees it: `Job::Stats` goes ahead of the scan and only delays the rest (`lib/core/ScanScheduler.cpp:207-217`). So a visit, or any S2 ask, taken while either is held meets 90,636-124,052 B less PSRAM: 3a-6's `Hv` took Full in it, at 45,585 ms. Where a fence cut the rest and it was carved again, L read 286,708 B after the update (main's own history, 3a-5's pair), the fit's near row. It also moved the first 3a-4 pair's figures by up to 120,144 B, which is why that run waited 30 s after the walk. Whether a visit waits out the rest's memory, or the rest gives it back at once when it is done (`restDone_`), is the owner's (9.7's device pass, call 3). The second way changes `CardTasks` for every ask, S2's included, and moves main's own PSRAM figures, so it would need its own control run. *Settled (2026-11-21, later):* "Only while held": a visit waits while the scan's memory is carved (`kWhyRest`). As built, a rest on a fresh card or after a Rescan is the whole scan (minutes); memory no step gives back (`scanStuck()`) doesn't hold them; and the gate stops a take only, so a scan's first carve can come beside a visit already taken. A battery floor met mid-rest keeps the memory with no step to give it back; since V10b (2026-11-22, "Don't wait at the floor") it doesn't hold the pages while the floor pauses the scan, and the visit's take checks there's room for a decoder. The owner settled all three (section 1: (a) that way, (b) and (c) "Keep both as built"). *As run (2026-11-22, 3a-8; MEASURED on the spare):* the boot's visit waited the scan's memory out and took 15.7 s after the walk's line, with 123,741-124,764 B more free than at the wait's lowest `[stats]` line (a whole-K line). S2's `Hr` still takes in the rest, as on main (its block, read and address not main's: 9.7). A fresh card's or a Rescan's rest (minutes) wasn't run.
- **The Dance tab's block** (2026-11-21, "Borrow Dance's block"). The stats pages' snapshot lives in a block another module owns. A change to `DanceMode` that frees or shrinks its scratch, or reads it with the tab down, would break the loan: a `static_assert` holds the scratch to `statsvisit::kBound`, the loan is keyed on the block (not on `ready()`), and `setActive(true)` releases first; later changes to `DanceMode` must keep all three. A library past 18,432 albums gets no snapshot (No room at every visit), and a larger block would change main's PSRAM from the boot. The tab counts as up only while shown and lit: a console ask made in the dark on the Dance tab is lent, and loses it at the wake (run 3a-9). *As run (2026-11-22, 3a-9; MEASURED):* so on the device. The tab took the block back first at every `ui3` with a snapshot held (4 of the 5), an ask it caught wrote nothing, a visit's ask (`Hv`) while it was up was refused (`Hn`, needing no snapshot, landed), and the dark's loan was released at the wake. Every `D` and `[dance]` on and off line was main's.
- **Remounts.** A remount while the card worker is busy restarts the board (`src/main.cpp:3274-3290`). Each UI ask's rank, about 1 s on the worker, raises the overlap's odds slightly.
- **A read fault shown as one.** Unread now comes only from Io, Changed, Damaged or Base. A card that keeps failing shows "Couldn't read the stats" with Try again, and nothing is lost (5.4).
- **The UI's object.** S3b must leave `ui::Ui`'s carve main's (call 2). "Main's carve" (its size within main's TLSF class) is the plan's reading of the owner's "no growth", put to the owner with S3b's gdb figure (section 1). S3b's bytes in it are about 8 B (7.2). If the owner reads "no growth" as main's size to the byte, they move to `StatsLists`' `.bss`. A member past its TLSF class goes to the owner either way, since it is a resident shift ("Move it").
- **A shelf's acts.** The queue's calls gain an id-view form so a shelf acts on its u16 ids where they are (7.6). The span forms call the view's, so every Library act runs through the new code: `test_queue` pins the two equal.

## 14. Considered and left out

**Other directions on the canvas.**
- A, Glance: a stats screen of tiles, a 4 x 64 px period tray at the right edge, a month view. Its data model, the entry row's bars, the empty state and the stat value were grafted into C.
- B, Recap: a story of pages (the week, the hours, the top, the find), reached from the Dance tab. Its big day, its "found by", its Auto DJ rate and the undated anchor to the sync were grafted. Its overlay band and its hash-to-track lookup were not.
- A dedicated tab: the tab bar's four tabs stay; the stats live where the listener browses.

**Taken out by the owner's decisions.**
- The plays line on album track rows (frame AlbumPlays; O3), and `stats.not_yet*`.
- A filter by deviceId (O2), and TRKS's "another device's" flag bit.
- Not folding `z`'s plays (O6).

**Other homes for the stats pages' snapshot** (2026-11-21; 9.7's "As decided (2026-11-21): the snapshot in the Dance tab's block").
- A plain `psramAlloc` block held while on the pages (S3a's V1-V7): run 3a-4 found it moves main's figures.
- Free + recount (that block freed before each track's open and Auto DJ's join, counted again by itself): the owner's first pick that day, replaced before it was built.
- The plain block carved at the top through the hold: worse in the model (9.7's "A top placement").
- A static 4 KB in RTC slow memory: new resident memory, its speed unmeasured.
- Internal RAM while a page shows: about half of G5's 7.5 KB margin.

**Other shapes for the data.**
- Three files renamed one by one: disagree after a cut (1.1).
- TRKS rewritten whole at every boot: up to 467 KB a boot at the ceiling.
- A log-only v1, counting the log at each boot: every trim and rotation loses history.
- Counters in NVS: a write a play, against `src/app/NvsLater.h:19-21`.
- DAYS alone, without HOUR: no local days when the zone arrives.
- HOURS for 13 months (the spec): nothing in v1 shows hours past a week; DAYS keeps the shape of a year at 6.4 KB.
- Folding drop notes into a lost figure: they carry no seq, MARK can't dedupe them, and on a card never synced each note counts plays already folded.
- A save mid-session on a timer: each costs a 0.05-0.4 s loop hold, and gains nothing the next boot's fold doesn't. The rotation's save and `Hs` are the only ones, both synchronous, so no fold happens under them.
- A staged boot fold beside the base: reading `stats.bin` again at a restarted scan does the same with no second copy.
- A resident TRKS: 53 KB typical but 467 KB at the ceiling.
- The worker saving before `finishScan()`: no window (1.1).

**Other shapes for the map.**
- The spec's id-keyed played-track table (8 B a played track, dense past half the library): can't name the rankers' hashes, and is dropped at every fence.
- A dense 4 B-a-track array: Auto DJ's filters block's size class, which once failed to place.
- Riding Auto DJ's join (an observer in `hashSlice`, `lib/core/AutoDjJoin.cpp:631-643`): saves about 0.1 s of CPU after a Live with Auto DJ on, which the owner keeps off (`docs/MOOD-FILTERS.md:1525`); at the boot the join starts before the stats' lists exist. The code is shared; the pass is not.
- `Join::setPathHash()` as the hook: it turns the folder states off (6.2).
- A walk on the card worker: the index isn't thread-safe (`lib/core/LibraryIndex.h:100`), and the fence clears it on the loop.

**Other shapes for the UI.**
- The artist page's " · N plays" sums: left out by this plan's reading of O3 (3.2), not by the owner.
- A "Today" window and a 4-cell tray: under the panel's right-edge skew.
- "Per day" filler rows, a "New to you" row with no list: dropped by the design's judges.
- An hour-of-day view: needs the zone, and a room the design didn't have.
- Swipes between windows: the house has no swipe on lists.
- Inert segments drawn dim: dropped; offered cells only.
- An inert NP row for a first play: no row instead.

**Later, not v1.**
- mStream's per-track counts on the card (spec 8.6): Not played yet means never, counts include other clients.
- An artist page "Top tracks" row.
- Following renames through ORIG's audioHash.
- Dating undated plays back from a later slot of the same boot (2.20.6 item 7), in the live and the boot's folds alike.
